### 18. SLH-DSA side-channel + fault-injection hardening

**Status:** NOT STARTED (research complete — see `docs/production-security.md`)

> See also #23 for PIN counter hardening gap surfaced by Bundle F: OPTIGA OID 0xF1D5 is firmware-managed decrement-before-auth (glitch during decrement can revert the counter); TROPIC01 uses physically irreversible fused slots. Either migrate to OPTIGA monotonic counter objects or add a secure-flash-anchored shadow counter as an anti-rollback check.

Research-derived mitigations from the deep-research round of 2026-04-14. Critical finding: **both verify-after-sign is inadequate** (RFC 9814 / Genêt TCHES 2023) and **OptRand = 0 (deterministic signing) enables PRF recovery**. These are not theoretical.

**STM32U5 is confirmed-vulnerable to voltage glitching**, not merely presumed. The Masaryk U Simonik thesis (verified real in the 2026-04-15 round) demonstrates ~76% PIN-glitch bypass on STM32U5 silicon (same family as our STM32U585). Ledger Donjon's March 2025 statement that no public attack existed was correct at publication but invalidated within months. Plan the FihInt / fail-in / double-compute items in this backlog as **must-ship**, not optional.

**SHAKE-vs-SHA2 decision — CLOSED 2026-05-20: STAY ON SHA-2, SHAKE REJECTED.** Three independent reasons, all confirmed this round: **(1) Masking is infeasible on either hash.** The on-silicon masked-SHA2 benchmark put masking the hot path at 278–2800× the HASH peripheral, and the 70,754 secret-touching PRF calls/sign make masking even just the secret subset ≈ 60 s/sign — 1–2 orders past the 5–10× viability line. **(2) SHAKE has NO hardware acceleration on STM32U5.** The HASH peripheral accelerates SHA-1/SHA-224/SHA-256 only — NOT SHA-3/Keccak/SHAKE (RM0456). So SHAKE would run entirely in software, forfeiting the measured 485-cyc/block HW path (vs 4265-cyc software SHA-2, 8.8×); even *unmasked* SHAKE is a net slowdown, and masked-SHAKE — though Keccak's single χ nonlinear step masks cheaper per-permutation than SHA-2's adders — is still ~70k software permutations/sign → also infeasible. **(3) The only cheap-SHAKE argument is unsubstantiated.** Fluhrer ePrint 2024/500's "1.7× backward-compatible PRF-tree" is not verifiable and technically implausible per production-security.md §3. **(4) Switching cost is launch-breaking:** a parameter-set change re-derives every key, moving the CREATE2 address (frozen-wallet invariant #6). Net: SHAKE offers no *realizable* SCA win on this MCU at a launch-breaking cost. The SCA posture stays on SHA-2 + the landed structural/FI mitigations (F-13 double-compute, F-16 WOTS/FORS shuffle, F-17 rate-limit, consumption-mask, multi-source OptRand); the **v2 PRF restructure** (pad sk_seed to a full 64-byte first block → constant cacheable midstate) remains the SHA-2-preserving KDF-revision escape hatch IF step-(b) leakage measurement ever shows an exploitable leak.

- [x] **SHAKE-vs-SHA2 architectural decision** — **DECIDED: STAY ON SHA-2** (closed 2026-05-20, see Completion Log; user-confirmed three times). Masked-SHA2 and SHAKE are both too expensive for the STM32U5: masked-SHA2 = 278–2800× the HASH peripheral (~60 s/sign even masking only the secret subset), the U5 HASH peripheral accelerates SHA-1/SHA-2 only (NOT SHA-3/Keccak/SHAKE per RM0456) so SHAKE forfeits the HW path AND a parameter switch would re-derive every key + move the CREATE2 address (breaks frozen-wallet invariant #6). **Do not re-open.** SCA posture stays SHA-2 + landed F-13/F-16/F-17 + consumption-mask + multi-source OptRand.
  - [x] **#2 measurement (masked-SHA2 cost) — DONE, run on silicon 2026-05-20.** New `masked-sha2` workspace crate: first-order (2-share) boolean-masked building blocks — ISW masked AND (`sec_and`, Ishai-Sahai-Wagner CRYPTO 2003) + Kogge-Stone secure boolean adder (`sec_add`, Coron-Großschädl-Vadnala family CHES 2014), generic over an RNG closure. Per the advisor-reviewed plan we do NOT build a full masked SHA-256 (correctness-risk + code volume); instead we measure the two gates that dominate the cost and PROJECT the per-block number from SHA-256's fixed structure (`ADDS_PER_BLOCK = 600`, `ANDS_PER_BLOCK = 320`). 7 host correctness oracles (sec_and == plain AND over 50k random; sec_add == wrapping_add over 50k random + full-ripple edge cases; Ch/Maj composition over 20k) all pass → Kogge-Stone indexing proven correct. Bench firmware `secure/src/bench_masked_sha.rs` (`bench-masked-sha` feature), run-and-halt at boot, DWT-times one-compression (55-byte single-block input) of HASH peripheral + `sha2` software + sec_and/sec_add BOTH ways (TRNG-inline / rand-pre-drawn). `make bench-masked-sha-hw` (note: NO debug-log — `hw::rng::fill` secure_log!s on every call, which floods the semihosting channel under a TRNG-heavy bench; results print via unconditional `hprintln!`). **Decision rule** (advisor): masked/HW ≲ 3× → SHA2 viable; ≳ 5–10× → SHAKE worth building.

    **RESULT (STM32U585 @ 160 MHz, 20k iters/measurement):** HASH peripheral 1-block = **485 cyc**; sha2 software 1-block = 4265 cyc (8.8×); TRNG draw 1 word = **214 cyc**; sec_and = 22 cyc gate / 214 cyc TRNG-inline; sec_add = 213 cyc gate / 2147 cyc TRNG-inline. **Projected masked SHA-256 block: 134,840 cyc (~842 µs) DRBG-floor; 1,356,680 cyc (~8.5 ms) raw-TRNG.** Slowdown vs HASH peripheral: **~278× (DRBG) to ~2797× (raw TRNG)**; vs sha2 software ~31× to ~318×.

    **VERDICT — masking the SHA-2 hot path wholesale is INFEASIBLE** (1–2 orders of magnitude past the 5–10× line). A full SLH-DSA sign is hundreds of thousands of compressions; even at the DRBG-floor 842 µs/block that's minutes-to-hours/sign. Three takeaways: **(1) The TRNG draw dominates** (214 cyc/word; sec_add's 10 words = ~2140 of 2147 cyc) — any masking path MUST use a DRBG (~10× win, 8.5 ms→0.84 ms/block); raw-TRNG masking is never right. **(2) This de-prioritizes the SHAKE migration** — SHAKE masks ~2–5× cheaper than SHA-2 (bitwise, no carry chains) but 278÷5 ≈ 56× is still infeasible for the full hot path, and there's no SHAKE hardware peripheral, so SHAKE-masked-in-software loses the HASH-peripheral speed without buying feasibility. **(3) The viable SCA architecture is "mask only the secret-touching hash subset"** (the sk_seed PRF + the first hash of each WOTS/FORS secret chain; the vast majority of SLH-DSA hashes operate on PUBLIC pk_seed-derived data and need no masking) composed with F-16 shuffle + consumption-mask + (deferred) RDI, driven by the `sca-trigger` on-silicon leakage measurement (F-18b). **Next step is NOT the SHAKE #3 benchmark** — it's (a) quantify the secret-touching hash count per sign, (b) on-silicon leakage-characterize just those calls, (c) mask only that subset (SHA-2, via DRBG) if it leaks. ~~At a few thousand secret-touching calls × 134k cyc ≈ ~1 s added, that's plausibly acceptable.~~ **← this "~few thousand / ~1 s" estimate was WRONG (see step (a) below).**

  - [x] **Step (a) — secret-touching hash count MEASURED 2026-05-20: 70,754/sign (NOT ~200).** Added a `hash-counters` feature to sphincs-c10 (atomic per-category counts, zero-cost when off) + `tests/hash_counts.rs`. One C10 sign: **wots_secret 44,118 + fors_secret 26,636 = 70,754 sk_seed-touching PRF calls (13.1% of all 539,616 hashes)**; chain_hash 44,204; th* (tree nodes) 364,273; other 60,385. **CORRECTION: my prior-turn "~200, mask the subset, ~1 s added" was wrong by ~350×** — I let a casual "~200" estimate past without verification. The count is large because SLH-DSA signing regenerates *every leaf of every signed subtree* (auth-path Merkle reconstruction: `build_subtree_with_auth` = D=2 × 512 leaves × L=43 = 44,032 wots_secret) and *every FORS tree leaf* (K=13 × 2^11 = 26,624 fors_secret) — each a `PRF(sk_seed, ADRS)` call, inherent to the algorithm. **CONSEQUENCE: "mask only the secret-touching subset" is INFEASIBLE** — 70,754 × 134,840 cyc (DRBG-masked block) ≈ **~60 s/sign**; raw-TRNG ~10 min. Same wall as masking everything. **(c) is dead at every granularity.**
    - **Why midstate caching doesn't rescue it** (advisor-checked): the PRF byte layout is `sk_seed(32) ‖ "wots"(4) ‖ layer(4) ‖ tree(32) ‖ kp(4) ‖ chain_idx(4)` = 80 B = 2 SHA-256 blocks. First block = `sk_seed(32) ‖ "wots"(4) ‖ layer(4) ‖ tree[0..24]`, so W[0..7]=sk_seed but W[8]=`"wots"‖layer` is *varying public ADRS*. You can cache the SHA-256 state after rounds 0..7 (which absorb only the sk_seed words W[0..7]), but rounds 8..63 still run on secret-derived state and still need masking — saves ~14% of round cost, doesn't drop the per-call multiplier. No cacheable post-sk_seed midstate because sk_seed isn't block-aligned.
    - **Why F-16 shuffle is only partial here**: it randomizes which ADRS pairs with which trace (defeats correlation DPA that needs the known-input pairing), but sk_seed sits at the *same intra-call sample offset* in all 70,754 calls, so horizontal averaging on rounds 0..7 doesn't care about call order. Real residual.
  - [ ] **Step (b) — REFRAMED as a standalone residual-characterization measurement (NOT a precursor to (c), which is dead).** One-number question: does the PRF actually leak sk_seed exploitably on *this* silicon, with F-16 shuffle + consumption-mask + the hardware noise floor combined? Wire `sca-trigger` (F-18b) around the PRF region, capture power traces, TVLA max|t| on the sk_seed-absorption window. Two dispositions: **leaks** → document the residual; the v1 answer is the system-level bar (dual-SE XOR split + PIN-unlock required before any sign + per-chain `MAX_SLOT_USES = 65,536`), and flag the v2 PRF restructure below; **doesn't leak at measurable sensitivity** → declare existing mitigations sufficient, close the §18 SCA thread. Needs the on-silicon power-SCA rig (scope + scared/lascar) — parked alongside the RDI measurement until a bench session is set up for SCA *capture* (not just probe-rs flashing).
  - [ ] **v2 PRF restructure (KDF-revision candidate — NOT retrofittable to the frozen wallet).** If (b) shows an exploitable leak, the real fix is to pad sk_seed to a full 64-byte first block in the PRF (`sha256(sk_seed_padded_64 ‖ label ‖ adrs)`) so the first compression absorbs ONLY sk_seed → a constant post-sk_seed midstate computed ONCE per sign (one masked compression) reused across all 70,754 calls, which then each run only the public second-block compression (unmasked). Turns 70,754 masked compressions into 1. **Changes the PRF input layout → re-bases every key + CREATE2 address → violates launch invariant #6.** A v2 / next-KDF-generation item, recorded so the v2 design has a concrete candidate. **(c) — "mask the subset" — is shelved permanently; this restructure replaces it.**

**What's needed — P0 (must ship with these):**
- [x] **SLH-DSA double-compute** (commit `7f2f2e7`, F-13). `secure/src/crypto.rs::c10_sign_verified_with_progress` signs twice, constant-time compares, releases on match. RFC 9814 §A.2 / Genêt TCHES 2023 verify-after-sign-alone-is-insufficient class closed.
- [x] **Non-deterministic OptRand** on every signature (commit follows F-13). 16 B drawn per sign-call via `rng_strong::fill` (STM32 ⊕ OPTIGA ⊕ SE050 XOR-fold; Trezor-parity multi-source RNG). The OptRand=None deterministic path is gone; both double-compute signs see the SAME freshly-drawn randomiser so the FI byte-equality gate still holds.
- [x] **Signing rate limiter** (F-17, commit `6fcc3cb`) — **complete as architecturally achievable**. `secure/src/sign_rate.rs` enforces (a) 1-second minimum interval between signs (busy-wait via `cortex_m::asm::wfi()` against the SysTick-driven `timeout::now()`), and (b) per-unlock-session cap of 250 sigs (refuse past cap; user must re-PIN-unlock). Hard-rotate after 2^16 is enforced via on-chain `MAX_SLOT_USES`. The "500/day" half of the original spec **is not implementable on the current platform** — it requires VBAT-backed wall-clock time, and the B-U585I-IOT02A (and any USB-powered hw-wallet topology generally) has no battery backup. STM32U5 RTC resets on every power-off, so any "day" boundary is bypassable via a reset cycle. The effective long-term rate ceiling becomes 250 sigs/session × ~5 s PIN-unlock cycle ≈ **~50 sigs/min sustained** for an attacker with the PIN — same architectural bound Trezor accepts (also USB-powered, no VBAT). Adding VBAT to the board design + RTC anchor is a hardware-revision item, not a firmware deliverable.
- [x] **WOTS chain + FORS tree shuffling** (F-16, commit follows). `sphincs-c10/src/shuffle.rs::fisher_yates` + new `SigningKey::sign_with_shuffle(msg, opt_rand, &ShuffleSeed, progress)` API. Per-call seed drawn from `rng_strong::fill` (3-source XOR) and fed UNCHANGED to both F-13 double-compute signs. Internally label-derived into per-purpose sub-seeds: `wots-0`, `wots-1`, `fors`. WOTS chain processing order shuffled per layer (43! ≈ 10^52); FORS tree processing order shuffled (13! ≈ 6×10^9). Output sig bytes are byte-identical to the un-shuffled path (regression-tested in `tests/shuffle_byte_equality.rs` — 4 oracles, all pass). `contracts/smart-wallet/test/c10_test_vectors.json` is byte-identical pre/post — every Foundry test that passed before still passes by construction. **Defeats profiled-DPA trace alignment**: with the same SK chain landing at a random sample index per signature, the averaging premise the attack depends on no longer holds. No on-chain change, no API break for external consumers; internal `sphincs-c10::wots::sign` and `hypertree::sign_inner` gain a shuffle-seed parameter.
- [x] **FihInt-style complement-storage** for `pin_verified` (F-14, commit follows). `secure/src/fih.rs::FihBool` stores `(val, complement)` with `SEC_TRUE=0x1AAA_AAAA` / `SEC_FALSE=0x1555_5555`, double-read via `read_volatile` with `wait_random` between, composed with the existing F-2 `check_true_into_sentinel` for caller-side branch-skip resistance. `has_signed`, `slot_master_derived`, `blob_cached` deferred — first two are write-only as of this pass; third gates a read whose downstream decryption fails on all-zero, not a security bypass.
- [~] **PIN-lockout fail-in pattern** (F-15, commit `6fa69bd`) — **MITIGATED, not provably fixed**. Both `nsc::gated_unlock` and `cmd_request_unlock::verify_pin_with_chip` now structure the lockout gate as FAIL-IN with `check_true_into_sentinel` Hamming-distant gating and double-read of the page-124 counter. The Masaryk-thesis 76 %-reliable single-branch-skip primitive is closed. Residuals (see `tools/sca/README.md §F-15`): stale OK_SENTINEL in r0 from a prior gate, specific-value EM injection, 2-coordinated-skip on `check_true_into_sentinel` internals, post-scan value-fault on `pre_count`/`remaining_after`, and the lack of F-12 forward+reverse hardening inside `pin_attempts_read` itself. Items 1 and 5 are cheap follow-ups (~30 LoC); 2–4 are accepted as "multi-fault out of scope."
- [x] **Compiler fence + DSB barrier after every zeroize** of master_secret, entropy, PIN buffers (commit follows). `crate::fi::zeroize_barrier()` helper: `compiler_fence(SeqCst)` (forbids LLVM reorder across the boundary) + `cortex_m::asm::dsb()` (forces CPU store-buffer commit before subsequent ops; ARM-only via cfg-gated inline asm; no-op on host tests). Applied to 50 critical-secret zeroize call sites (`master_secret`, `entropy`, `half_o` / `half_e` / `full_entropy`, `pin`, `opt_rand_buf`, `slot_master_entropy`). Post-patch linked ELF has +23 `dsb` instructions vs pre-patch (the rest are inlined into shared epilogues by LLVM). The `zeroize` crate's `Zeroize::zeroize()` already does the `compiler_fence` internally; this commit's value is the explicit `dsb` for peripheral-bus ordering (SHA / SAES / PKA accelerators are separate AHB masters) and for asynchronous-wipe-interruption ordering (idle-wipe ISR / panic handler).

**What's needed — P1 (strongly recommended):**
- [x] Architectural decision: **SHAKE vs SHA2 parameter set — CLOSED 2026-05-20: STAY ON SHA-2** (full rationale in the §18 lead above). Summary: masking infeasible on either hash (278–2800× / ~60 s/sign at 70,754 secret calls); SHAKE has no STM32U5 HW acceleration (HASH peripheral is SHA-2-family only) so it's a net slowdown even unmasked; the Fluhrer 1.7× cheap-PRF-tree claim is unverifiable; and a parameter switch re-derives every key (frozen invariant #6). SCA posture stays on SHA-2 + landed F-13/F-16/F-17 + the v2-PRF-restructure escape hatch.
- [x] **Control-flow-integrity step counters** (F-18, commit follows). `secure/src/fi.rs::CfiCounter` + `cfi_expected!` macro. Each critical-path function bumps a running u32 with a unique per-step magic; final check compares against compile-time-summed expected via the F-2 sentinel idiom (`check_into_sentinel(EXPECTED) != OK_SENTINEL → reject`). Volatile reads/writes prevent constant-folding. Applied to `c10_sign_verified_with_progress` (7 steps: rate-limit, opt_rand, shuffle, sign-A, sign-B, ct_eq, verify-gate). Defends the "skip an entire function call" attack class that the F-2 sentinel pattern doesn't reach (F-2 hardens the `if`-branch AFTER a function returns; CFI hardens the function call itself being skipped). Multi-fault attack that writes the expected value directly to the stack-resident counter would still bypass (out of scope per the single-fault threat model). Future: extend to `gated_unlock` + the gateway command dispatchers (similar 3-5 step shape each).
- [x] **Random delays before security-critical ops (entry jitter at the PIN gate)** — DONE 2026-05-20. After advisor review, scoped to explicit `wait_random()` at the entry of `nsc::gated_unlock` + `cmd_request_unlock::verify_pin_with_chip` ONLY — NOT the sign path (F-16's 43!×13! internal shuffle already jitters the secret-dependent timeline there) and NOT an always-on RDI timer (Trezor's `systimer`-driven `rdi_handler` burns random cycles in an ISR that would land mid-USB-bulk / mid-T=1' / mid-Shielded-handshake — Trezor's own `rdi_disabled = sectrue` default + enable/disable infra exist because they hit those bugs; not worth the risk pre-silicon-SCA). The PIN gate is the high-value target: linear from external trigger (USB `CMD_REQUEST_UNLOCK` dispatch, boot-unlock, PendSV re-unlock) to the F-15 sentinel with no internal shuffle, so a Masaryk-class profiled single-fault attacker glitches at a fixed offset; entry jitter desyncs that offset by 0..255 `wait_random_loop` iterations (~0..19 µs at 160 MHz). **Honest bound**: meaningful against an uncalibrated single-shot attacker, does NOT defeat a profile-then-attack rig with multi-attempt statistical recovery — F-15 sentinel + F-17 rate limiter remain the load-bearing defenses. Verified on the linked ELF: real `bl ...fi11wait_random` in both functions (no prologue folding — both `wait_random` and `wait_random_loop` are `#[inline(never)]`). 2 source-grep regression tests pin that the entry jitter precedes the gate it desyncs. The RDI-timer path stays available as a follow-up if on-silicon SCA shows the gap matters.
- [x] **Redundant volatile reads (2-3×) on critical state with OR-based fail-in** (F-19, commit follows). New helper `secure/src/fi.rs::read_volatile_voted<T: Copy + Eq>(p) -> Result<T, ()>` — three independent `read_volatile`s with `compiler_fence(SeqCst)` between, `Ok(v)` iff all three agree, `Err(())` otherwise. Defends single-fault glitches on the `ldr` instruction (which would otherwise clamp the loaded register to an attacker-chosen value). Applied at `sign_rate::wait_for_min_interval` — the rate-limit reference timestamp `LAST_SIGN_MS` AND the SysTick counter `now()` are both critical-timing reads whose disagreement now forces the wait loop to spin until a clean read agrees. Composes with F-14 FihBool (stored-value defense) and F-18 CFI (function-skip defense) as the third leg of the load-time / call-time / value-time triad. Pattern documented in the helper's docstring for future application at other single-load sites.

- [x] **RDI (Random Delay Interrupt) timer — RE-SCOPED 2026-05-28: NOT MOTIVATED for the non-sign secret paths. Do NOT build the subsystem.** The one piece of on-silicon work that remains (value-channel confirmation) lives under §18b (`sca-trigger`), and RDI is not its answer either way. Originally investigated 2026-05-20 as "net-beneficial-for-SCA-not-FI, deferred pending an on-silicon leak measurement." The *emulation half* of that prerequisite is now done — and it argues against RDI rather than for it (see "Emulation re-scope" below). The detailed Trezor / TrustZone / cost analysis is retained below as the record of why even a flag-gated port is a poor fit.

  **What Trezor does** (`trezor-firmware/core/embed/sec/random_delays/stm32/random_delays.c`): a periodic `systimer` fires every tick; `rdi_handler` burns `drbg_random8() + session_delay` cycles (0..510, ~3 µs) of NOP-equivalent delay, preempting whatever runs. Source is a ChaCha DRBG (not raw TRNG — per-tick TRNG draws would exhaust it), reseeded every 1000 calls. `random_delays_start_rdi()` is called ONCE at `kernel/main.c:141` + `secmon/main.c:94` and left on; `random_delays_refresh_rdi()` re-seeds the session base delay on every USB `DataIn`/`DataOut` (`usb.c:728/748`) so the timeline stays unpredictable across a session. `FEATURE_FLAGS["RDI"] = True` for kernel + secmon + firmware — enabled on all Trezor core devices (T / Safe 3 / 5 / 7). The `rdi_disabled = sectrue` default + start/stop scaffolding exist for the boot window before the DRBG is seeded, not because they ship it off.

  **The decisive architectural difference — TrustZone.** Trezor runs USB + crypto + RDI in one secure context, so always-on jitter is uniform and they accept it. **Our USB stack runs in the NON-secure world.** A secure-world RDI timer firing on a fixed schedule preempts NS-world USB bulk transfers whenever it lands during them — the intermittent-stall risk (USB enumeration glitch, SE050 T=1' inter-byte / OPTIGA Shielded-Connection sequence timing) is *worse* for us than for Trezor because the ISR crosses the NS boundary they don't have. The naive always-on port is a genuinely bad fit. The viable design for us is **flag-gated RDI**: an `IN_SECURE_CRITICAL` flag set around signing / PIN / SE-unlock, handler burns cycles only when the flag is live, no-op otherwise. That dodges the USB stall but is a richer version of the entry-jitter + per-step `wait_random` we already have, not a categorically new defense.

  **Where it adds value we lack — SCA, not FI.** *FI*: marginal — our security gates are already hardened (F-2 sentinel, F-14 FihBool, F-15 fail-in, F-18 CFI, F-19 voted reads, entry jitter); RDI only covers un-instrumented paths, and a glitch in non-gate code (KDF, XOR) yields a wrong key that fails verification, not a bypass. *SCA*: real gap — F-16's shuffle misaligns power traces ONLY for the signing path. The other secret-touching ops have NO timeline jitter today: **AES-GCM entropy-blob decrypt**, **SAES-CMAC(DHUK) KDF** (`hw/saes_cmac.rs`), and the **dual-SE XOR reconstruction** (`dual_se.rs`). RDI would misalign traces against all three. Complementary to the consumption-mask (TIM2 PWM adds amplitude noise but not time jitter).

  **Cost.** ChaCha/HMAC DRBG in the secure world (~200 LoC + we have no chacha20 dep today — sha2/sha3 present, so an HMAC-SHA256-DRBG or SHA3 sponge is the lower-friction source); a dedicated timer (STM32U585 has spares — TIM/LPTIM beyond the SysTick-timeout + TIM-sign-rate + TIM2-consumption-mask already in use); the flag-gating plumbing; and silicon validation that it doesn't break SE I²C / USB enumeration / SysTick accounting.

  **Emulation re-scope (2026-05-28) — the measurement says don't build RDI.** The prerequisite was "measure the three non-sign secret paths before building anything." The emulation half of that is now complete in `tools/sca/` (rainbow/lascar `mem_address` TVLA, 600 traces each, fresh on the current tree):

  | Path | Probe | Result |
  |---|---|---|
  | AES-GCM entropy wrap/unwrap | `sca_aesgcm_wrap` (`leakage_kdf.py`) | `max\|t\| = 0.00` → flat |
  | SAES-CMAC(DHUK) KDF wrapper | `sca_saes_cmac` / `sca_saes_kdf_one_block` ×4 (`leakage_saes_kdf.py`) | `max\|t\| = 0.00` → flat |
  | dual-SE XOR reconstruction | `sca_dual_se_xor` (`leakage_seed_derivation.py`) | `max\|t\| = 0.00` → flat |

  Two facts kill the RDI case for these paths:
  1. **The address channel — the only multi-trace channel emulation can test — is already flat for all three** (constant-time soft-AES + branchless XOR; no T-table / cache / secret-indexed access). There is no multi-trace alignment leak for RDI to misalign.
  2. **The residual is a value-channel leak that is *single-trace*.** Each of these is a boot-once operation (one entropy unwrap, one KDF derivation, one XOR reconstruction per unlock) with no attacker-chosen input → no DPA/CPA averaging surface. RDI randomises the *time offset across many traces*; it does **not** defend single-trace SPA/template attacks (a template attacker realigns one trace by pattern-matching). So RDI's mechanism does not match the threat that remains. **The same disqualifier applies to the cheaper `wait_random()`/per-op-jitter middle step** — same mechanism, same single-trace ineffectiveness; it is *not* a consolation fix here.

  This is consistent with the framework's own track record: every *real* single-trace leak it found — F-22 (BIP-39 wordlist), F-24 (seed-display), F-23 (recovery typing) — was closed with **constant-time code**, never RDI. If on-silicon measurement (§18b, `sca-trigger`) ever shows an exploitable value-channel leak on one of these paths, the correct response is constant-time/masked code or a hardware countermeasure on that specific op — not a global RDI timer that fights our NS-world USB stack (see the TrustZone analysis above) for a defense that doesn't address single-trace.

  **What still legitimately remains (tracked under §18b, not here):** on-silicon power/EM confirmation that the value channel on these three paths is acceptable at the system level (dual-SE XOR split + PIN-gated unlock + per-chain `MAX_SLOT_USES` cap). That needs a scope, not RDI.

**Files to create:** `secure/src/fih.rs` (FihInt type + read_volatile helpers)
**Files to change:** `secure/src/crypto.rs` (OptRand + double-compute), `secure/src/nsc/sign_and_emit.rs`, `secure/src/nsc/cmd_request_unlock.rs`, `secure/src/dual_se.rs` (seed XOR reconstruction hardening)

### 18b. FI/SCA audit-derived follow-ups (catalogued from the F-* sweep + post-mortems)

**Status:** discrete items spawned during the F-* fault-injection / side-channel audit (`tools/sca/README.md` §Findings). Each is small enough that it didn't deserve its own §-numbered work-todo entry, but tracking them as a list here so future "let's continue" picks can't lose any improvement. Most are "raise residual cost" follow-ups on findings already MITIGATED or FIXED; one or two are genuinely new.

**Cheap residual-knockouts on already-mitigated findings:**

- [x] **F-15.r1 — register-scrub between back-to-back sentinel gates** (commit follows). `fi::scrub_sentinel_register` added (`mov r0, #0` inline-asm on ARM, no-op on host). Audit confirmed every existing paired-sentinel callsite already has a `wait_random()` or a longer intervening function call between them that clobbers `r0`, so the stale-OK_SENTINEL attack has no concrete exposure today. The helper is in place as defence-in-depth for future commits that might introduce closer-spaced sentinel calls without an intervening function boundary.
- [x] **F-15.r5 — `pin_attempts_read` F-12-hardening** (commit follows). `hw::flash::pin_attempts_read` now uses the F-12 forward + reverse double-scan with asymmetric control flow, returning `PIN_ATTEMPTS_CAPACITY` (32, > `MAX_ATTEMPTS = 10`) on mismatch so every downstream gate fail-closes.
- [x] **F-15.r4 — post-scan value-fault on derived integers** — covered transitively by F-15.r5. The narrow load-into-register → store-to-local → closure capture window is single-instruction-wide and below the granularity the rainbow harness models; the F-15.r5 cross-check happens upstream of this window. Audit-only; no separate fix needed.
- [~] **F-13/F-14 sentinel constant Hamming-weight upgrade** — **considered, declined**. The intuition was that weight-1 or weight-31 constants would be harder to inject via EM than the current weight-16 `OK_SENTINEL = 0xA5A5_A5A5`. On closer analysis the benefit doesn't hold: (a) **single bit-flip** is defended by Hamming distance, which is already 32 between OK and FAIL (every bit differs) — independent of weight; (b) **stuck-at-0** and **stuck-at-FF** are both not OK regardless of weight choice; (c) **random uncalibrated EM noise** hits any specific 32-bit value at `1/2^32` probability, independent of weight; (d) a **calibrated EMFI attacker** can tune for whatever specific value we choose, so weight doesn't change their cost. The actual defenses against specific-value injection are scrub_sentinel_register (F-15.r1, landed) + multi-gate composition + on-silicon shielding (production RDP2). Constant choice itself is not the load-bearing defense. No code change; this item retires.
- [x] **F-14 follow-up — migrate the remaining gateway booleans to `FihBool`** (commit follows). `nsc::state::has_signed`, `nsc::state::slot_master_derived` (both now F-14-hardened, currently write-only but future read sites inherit the complement-storage defense for free), and `blob_cached` in `OptigaTrustM` / `Se050` / `DualSecureElement` (the third gates a real read site in `read_entropy_blob` — now routed through `is_true_fi()` for double-read + halt-on-mismatch). Closes the consistency gap in the FihBool sweep: every security-adjacent boolean in `SecureState` + SE driver structs uses the complement-storage pattern.

**Multi-fault sweep (cost increase, not closure):**

- [ ] **F-5 follow-up — run two-fault sweeps against post-F-7..F-15 production.** `make fi-twofault` exists but has only been run against the pre-F-7 codebase. Re-run against the current tree to confirm the 2-coordinated-skip residual hasn't moved in a damaging direction (e.g., a new gate is now 1-skip-defeatable while we weren't looking). Also extend to the F-14 / F-15 gates which use `check_true_into_sentinel` in new shapes. Audit-only (no fix expected).
- [~] **C10 sign FI sweep on real `sphincs-c10::sign`.** `tools/sca/c10_sign_target/` exists but the full sweep is deferred (~14s/emulation × thousands of injections = days). Worth running on a CI-grade machine once. Looks for: faults during sign that leak sk_seed or emit malformed-but-valid sigs (the Genêt class F-13's double-compute defends against — verify it actually closes the harness-observable residual). **IN FLIGHT (2026-06-02, beefy box):** new harness `tools/sca/c10_sign_full_sweep.py` (naive-parallel, fresh-emulator-per-fault, bit-reversal position order so any early stop is a uniform sweep, per-fault JSONL checkpoint at `out/c10_sign_sweep.jsonl`, resumable). Measured ~73 s/fault native (no per-instruction hook) → ~884 faults/h on 30 workers; stride 500k = 13,245 positions × {skip,stuck0,stuckff} = 39,735 faults (~15 h skip-only, ~45 h full). Running against the **post-fcee705a FIXED binary** (the FORS-forgery fix); a prior 3,288-fault run against the pre-fix vulnerable binary was discarded (`out/*.PREFIX-VULNERABLE.bak`). Pre-flight workflow (wf_d0a3640f) verdict GO: harness audits the fixed code, fix is signer↔verifier↔contract-consistent, `ht_idx→0`-fault forgery is handled (digest-derived, oracle re-derives), F-9 needs no re-run.

**SCA re-baseline after F-13 randomization:**

- [x] **F-9 re-test with post-F-13/F-16 randomization** (commit follows). New harness `tools/sca/f9_retest.py` + SUT `sca_c10_sign_shuffled` re-run the TVLA with the F-16 shuffle seed independently random per trace. Result: `max|t| = 39.09` vs F-9's `40.71` baseline — only a 4 % drop. **Diagnosis**: the leak is in `fors::grind_r`'s msg-dependent iteration count (~2048 iterations on average, varies per msg), NOT in the FORS leaf-index access pattern as initially hypothesized. F-16 shuffles the WOTS/FORS loops *after* `grind_r` returns, so it doesn't reach this leak. **The security conclusion stands**: `grind_r` only touches PUBLIC inputs (`pk_seed`, `pk_root`, `msg`, the iterator), never `sk_seed` — so the msg-correlated address pattern leaks only publicly-computable information. F-9 README §"F-9 re-test (post-F-16 shuffle)" carries the full mechanism + diagnosis. Two new follow-ups spawned (below).

**Spawned by the F-9 re-test:**

- [x] **Deeper SCA sweep past `grind_r`** (commit follows). `tools/sca/f9_deeper.py` runs 100 traces × 10 M samples × stride=10 = covers ~100 M mem events (grind_r + FORS sign + most of WOTS sign — the SECRET-bearing portion). Result: `max|t| = 5.66 @sample 990 123`. The leak position at stride=10 maps to mem-event 9.9 M = back in the **grind_r tail** region (the same noise-floor location as the F-9 re-test). **No sample in the SECRET-bearing region (mem events 10 M-100 M) exceeds max|t|=5.66.** Translation: F-16 closes the SECRET-region leakage at audit-grade emulation sensitivity; the residual is consistent with statistical noise from the reduced 100-trace count. Full mechanism in `tools/sca/README.md §F-9 update — deeper sweep result`. Limitation: emulation-only `mem_address` channel; on-silicon SCA verification is the next audit layer (gated on the `sca-trigger` GPIO feature flag).
- [x] **Hedged R-derivation: wire `opt_rand` into `grind_r`** (commit follows). `sphincs-c10/src/fors.rs::grind_r` now takes `opt_rand: Option<&[u8; N]>` and mixes it into the nonce-derivation hash. `hypertree::sign_inner` plumbs the existing F-13 `opt_rand` through (no more underscore-unused). Deterministic-`None` path preserves byte-equality with the pre-fix output (`contracts/smart-wallet/test/c10_test_vectors.json` is byte-identical pre/post). **Result:** F-9 re-test max|t| dropped 40.71 → 4.93 (-87.9 %, essentially noise floor for 600 traces). The grind_r transparent channel is closed at the audit level; what remains is statistical noise just above the 4.5 TVLA threshold. Full mechanism + numbers in `tools/sca/README.md §F-9 update — hedged R-derivation landed`.

**Production-blocker not formally on §18 P0:**

- [~] **TZSC/GTZC peripheral isolation fix** (commit follows). **Misdiagnosis corrected**: USB OTG FS + AES/HASH/RNG/PKA/SAES are NOT on GTZC2 as previously claimed — they're all in `GTZC1_TZSC_SECCFGR3` of the same controller `secure/src/sau.rs` already writes to. Verified against the STM32CubeU5 HAL constants (`GTZC_PERIPH_OTG = GTZC1_PERIPH_REG3 | GTZC_CFGR3_OTG_Pos` etc.). The previous "all-NS pre-production baseline" was a workaround for a non-existent problem. New code wires the correct allowlist: SECCFGR1 bits 13/14 (I2C1/I2C2) + SECCFGR3 bits 11-15 (AES/HASH/RNG/PKA/SAES) all SECURE; OTG (bit 10) stays NS. **Remaining: on-silicon validation**: USB still enumerates AND NS read of RNG_DR / SAES registers bus-faults. QEMU e2e passes 18/18 but doesn't exercise the GTZC path (QEMU build skips `configure_gtzc`). TAMP (in real GTZC2) follows in a separate commit if needed.

**Phase 2 of work-todo §10 (multi-source RNG migration):**

- [x] **Migrate non-signing RNG call-sites to `rng_strong::fill`** (commit follows). Initial framing ("none is security-critical") was wrong on closer review: the wizard master-entropy and OTP device-master burn are the MOST security-critical RNG outputs in the firmware. Full breakdown moved into §10 Phase 2 — 4 sites migrated (wizard entropy, OTP burn, dual_se half_O via open-coded multi-source, consumption_mask seed), 3 sites deliberately kept on platform-only (wait_random salt: cost too high per call; seed_wizard word picker: not security-bearing; tropic01_se: not shipping).

**Long-tail audit items from `tools/sca/README.md` Roadmap (mentioned but never promoted to formal checklist):**

- [ ] **3-fault sweep against `fi::check_true*` + production gates.** `make fi-twofault` already covers 2-coordinated-skip; 3-fault is a much steeper bar combinatorically (`n³` pair-product) but a known F-5 follow-up. Useful as a regression test rather than a daily run — once-per-release CI gate. Hardware-grade EMFI attackers in the literature have demonstrated 3-fault primitives on Cortex-M, so this isn't theoretical.
- [ ] **Leakage pass over the FI guards themselves.** Does `fi::wait_random`'s loop count leak via timing? Emulated traces are jitter-free so this would be a structural check (count instructions per loop iteration as a function of the RNG byte) rather than a real timing-side-channel test. Cheap and adds confidence the FI guards aren't themselves a side channel.
- [x] **`sca-trigger` firmware feature flag for on-silicon SCA work** (commit follows). `secure/src/hw/sca_trigger.rs` — feature-gated GPIO toggle (default PD2, configurable via two consts in the module). RAII guard `Trigger::raise()` (high on construction, low on drop) for clean entry/exit pairing even with early-return paths. Production fence in `secure/src/nsc/mod.rs::compile_error!` extended to reject `sca-trigger` on stm32u585 release builds unless `e2e-test` or `dev-testkey` escape hatch is also set. **Verified**: `cargo build --features dual-se,stm32u585,sca-trigger,ui-noop --release` fails with the production-fence compile_error; builds with `e2e-test` added succeed. Module compiles to inline no-ops when the feature is OFF — call sites can be added unconditionally without per-cfg gating. **Pending**: actual call-site insertion (`c10_sign_verified_with_progress`, `hw::saes_cmac::cmac_dhuk`, `nsc::gated_unlock`) is left as a one-line addition the bench operator does per campaign — the choice of which primitives to probe depends on the attack model being investigated.

**Spawned by the 2026-06-02 C10-sweep pre-flight** (workflow wf_d0a3640f, run while relaunching the C10-sign FI sweep against the post-fcee705a fixed binary; these are NEW secret-touching surfaces the 72-commit pull activated that NO existing `tools/sca` harness covers — ranked, none blocking):

- [x] **RANK 1 — SCP03 R-MAC / R-ENC response-unwrap sweep (SE050).** The S-5 ship-blocker fix (2026-05-28) flipped the SE050 secure channel to `P1=0x33` (C-MAC/C-ENC + **R-MAC/R-ENC**) and added the host-side `secure/src/se050/scp03.rs::unwrap_response` (+ `scp03_logic.rs::aes128_cbc_decrypt`, `response_icv`, `ct_eq_8`). This is brand-new, **secret-touching** code: SE050 responses — including `half_E` (one XOR half of the seed, invariant #1) — now come back R-ENC-encrypted + R-MAC-authenticated instead of plaintext-on-I2C (closes invariant #3 on the response leg). Two harness flavors, mirroring existing patterns: **(a) FI sweep** (like `fault_sweep_pin.py`): can a single fault make the R-MAC verify (`if !ct_eq_8(mac_full[..8], rmac_recv)`, scp03.rs:510) PASS on a tampered/forged response → host accepts attacker-chosen `half_E`? **(b) leakage/CT sweep** (like `leakage_saes_kdf.py`): does `aes128_cbc_decrypt`/CMAC do data-dependent `mem_address` accesses that leak the session key or `half_E`? **Honest scope:** emulation tests the *software* unwrap logic + MAC-verify gate, NOT the SE050 silicon or the I2C bus physics; this is defense-in-depth completeness (a new gate-shaped secret path shouldn't ship un-swept when every comparable path — C10 sign, KDF, PIN gate, FW-verify — has a harness), not a known-bug fire drill. Needs a new `scp03_target/` crate wrapping `unwrap_response` + `scp03_logic` (a few hours). **✅ DONE 2026-06-10:** `tools/sca/scp03_target/` (`#[path]`-includes the real `scp03_logic.rs`; the `unwrap_response` wrapper is a kept-in-sync copy of `se050/scp03.rs`). (a) FI sweep `fault_sweep_scp03.py` (`make scp03-fi`) — **exhaustive run FOUND a genuine `[skip]` gate weakness (F-28, see new task below): the R-MAC check is single-fault-defeatable** (an earlier strided smoke run had falsely reported "no bypass"); (b) leakage TVLA `leakage_scp03.py` (`make scp03-leakage`) — all 4 modes flat on `mem_address`, positive control leaks max|t|≈18. Makefile + README §"SE050 SCP03 response-unwrap".
- [x] **RANK 2 — `rng_strong` strict-failure semantics.** `bb2615f6` made SE-TRNG failure FATAL under production backends (was silent fall-through). `fault_sweep_fi.py` does not probe `crate::se_random`; a targeted skip/stuck-at sweep on the new fatal-failure branch would confirm a faulted/forced "SE-TRNG-OK" can't let a weakened XOR-fold through. **✅ DONE 2026-06-10:** `tools/sca/rng_strong_target/` (`#[path]`-includes the real `rng_strong.rs`; stubs `crate::rng::fill` + `crate::se_random` as harness-controlled sources). `fault_sweep_rng_strong.py` (`make rng-strong`, audit-only, exit 0) stages OPTIGA/SE050/platform-fail + all-sources-zero scenarios. Verdict: no single fault yields WEAK entropy from a healthy system — single-fault "bypasses" either retain a strong unbroken source (the XOR multi-source design) or need an out-of-single-fault precondition (all sources zero); same F-2/F-4 call-glue residual class. README §"rng_strong fail-closed multi-source fold".
- [x] **RANK 3 — OPTIGA provisioning-hardening gates.** S-1/S-2/S-3 closure added `verify_and_lock` / `lockdown_ta_pool` / `metadata_change_is_auto_authref` in `secure/src/optiga/mod.rs`. Provisioning-time, run-once (not a per-signature hot path) and no harness covers it; lowest priority — worth a single-fault check on the LcsO-ratchet read-back gate. **✅ DONE 2026-06-10:** `tools/sca/optiga_lock_target/` (copies the pure `find_metadata_tag` / `is_metadata_operational` / `metadata_matches_expected` verbatim; stubs the I2C round-trips). `fault_sweep_optiga_lock.py` (`make optiga-lock`, audit-only, exit 0). **Finding:** the readback-verify-then-lock gate IS single-fault-defeatable (~12–14/model fire the irreversible LcsO ratchet on a chip whose AC didn't land) — a plain branch with no FI redundancy. **Risk: low** — provisioning-time, run-once, in the trusted PQ1 factory; it guards the OPTIGA silent-AC-reject *quirk* (a reliability/yield issue), not an adversary, so this is a yield event (caught by post-provisioning QA), not a field bypass. Hardening (sentinel + double-evaluate before `lock_oid`, like the C10 gate) noted but not warranted under the current threat model. README §"OPTIGA verify_and_lock LcsO-ratchet gate".

**Spawned by the 2026-06-10 exhaustive `make scp03-fi` run** (the RANK 1 harness; a strided smoke run had falsely shown "no bypass"):

- [x] **F-28 — FI-harden the SE050 SCP03 R-MAC verify gate (`unwrap_response`). ✅ FIXED 2026-06-10.** The exhaustive stride-1 FI sweep found genuine **`[skip]` faults inside `secure/src/se050/scp03.rs::unwrap_response` that make a forged (wrong-R-MAC) response accepted and release the attacker's `half_E`** (emulated instr 18333 / 39313). Root cause: the R-MAC was checked with a **plain `if !ct_eq_8(..) { return Err }`** — no FI redundancy. **Threat:** bench attacker driving the I2C bus + one precise glitch → host accepts a chosen `half_E` (one XOR half of the seed); `half_E` alone doesn't reveal the seed (`half_O` stays on the OPTIGA behind the Shielded Connection) but it defeats the S-5 response-leg integrity, and the bench-attacker model is exactly the one S-1/S-2/S-3 take seriously. **Fix landed in 3 passes** (`secure/src/se050/scp03.rs` + the kept-in-sync `scp03_target`): (1) sentinel gate (`check_true_into_sentinel` double-CMAC + `black_box`) — killed all stuck-at + CMAC/compare skips, 3 skip residuals left; (2) redundant `read_volatile_voted` gate — 3→1; (3) the survivor was traced INSIDE `check_true_into_sentinel`'s own verdict branch (→ F-29), so the close is an **infective release mask**: fold a fresh, INDEPENDENT R-MAC recompute branchlessly into the output (`release_mask = (mac_matches as u8).wrapping_sub(1)`, `out[i] ^= mask`) — a forged response that slips the early gate is XOR-garbled, never the real `half_E`, with no branch to skip. **`make scp03-fi` now exits 0** (`GENUINE-gate-bypass=0` across all 3 fault models over 60,726 positions; baselines hold). Compile-checked in a full `dual-se,stm32u585,…,usb` firmware config.
- [x] **F-29 — `check_true_into_sentinel`'s verdict-selection branch was single-skip-defeatable (primitive-level). ✅ FIXED 2026-06-10.** Uncovered while closing F-28. `pqsigner_fi::check_true_into_sentinel` selected its verdict with `if s == OK_SENTINEL && v1 && v2 { OK_SENTINEL } else { FAIL_SENTINEL }`; that `if/else` compiled to a branch, and a **single instruction-skip of it returned `OK_SENTINEL` for a false condition** (emulated: skipping the `b` inside the monomorphised helper falls into the OK-verdict assignment). **Blast radius:** any gate releasing a secret on a *single* sentinel check (that was F-28); gates with **two independent** checks survive a single fault (C10 verify = verify-sentinel + F-18 CFI; PIN = verdict-sentinel + SE-silicon `result`). **Fix landed** (`pqsigner-fi`): new branchless `select_sentinel` — `(m1 & m2 & OK) | ((!m1 | !m2) & FAIL)` with two independently `black_box`'d masks — applied to both the `sentinel_storage` assignment and the final verdict in `check_true` + `check_true_into_sentinel`. No branch to skip; producing OK from a false input needs BOTH masks all-ones, so one fault leaves the other 0 → FAIL. **Verified:** the inside-`check_true_into_sentinel` flip on `sca_c10_verify_release(0)` is gone (5→4 flips, 0 inside-helper); the **full FI matrix re-ran with ZERO regressions** (`fi`/`c10`/`pin`/`ns-ptr`/`fw-verify`/`c10v`/`scp03-fi` all exit 0); 38 `pqsigner-fi` host tests pass. **Residual (by design):** the 4 remaining `sca_c10_verify_release` flips are all OUTSIDE the primitive — the gate's call-site glue + closure = the documented **F-2** class ("check_true cannot fix its own call site"); single-sentinel secret-release gates must still compose a second check / silicon gate / infective mask. README §Findings F-29. **Follow-ups (low-priority):** (a) `make c10`'s oracle still needs a human to "eyeball the PCs" for inside-`check_true` decision-point flips — teach it to auto-classify by symbol like `scp03-fi` does; (b) the `tools/sca` Makefile target rules don't list `../../pqsigner-fi/src/lib.rs` as a dependency, so `make build*` won't rebuild after a `pqsigner-fi` edit (must `cargo build` the target directly) — add the dep.

**Spawned by the 2026-06-02 "would the FI sweep have caught the FORS forgery bug?" analysis** (workflow wf_9bc70be8). **Answer: NO** — the C10-sign FI sweep (and the F-9 SCA harness) are structurally blind to the `fcee705a` shared-FORS-forest forgery (CWE-347, few-time key reused as many-time): it's a *cryptographic-design* flaw exploited by collecting ~3-4k *un-faulted* signatures and reassembling the shared forest, whereas the FI sweep signs one fixed message, never collects/correlates multiple sigs, and judges forges with an honest verifier. **Proof:** the archived `tools/sca/out/c10_sign_sweep.PREFIX-VULNERABLE.jsonl.bak` ran 3,288 single faults against the *known-vulnerable* pre-fix binary and reported **0 forge anomalies**. **Critical gap the analysis surfaced: `fcee705a` added the fix but ZERO regression tests** (`--stat`: 0 test files), there is **no multi-signature forgery simulator anywhere in the repo**, and the Lean `Spec/Fors.lean` is **not re-synced** + the Halmos/Certora discharge is **pending** — so this exact bug class could silently regress with nothing in CI to catch it. The four tasks below close that gap (do ①②③ on the laptop; ④ needs formal toolchains):

- [x] **① Forest-reassembly forgery simulator (the real attack; highest value — laptop-friendly).** New host-runnable harness reusing the pure `sphincs-c10` crate: from ONE keypair, generate N ≈ 4,000 legitimate signatures across *distinct* `ht_idx` positions (vary the message so `extract_ht_index(digest)` spreads), harvest the in-the-clear FORS leaf secrets + auth paths from those sigs, reassemble the FORS forest, then attempt to synthesize a NEW valid signature for an attacker-chosen message and feed it to `sphincs_c10::verify`. **Acceptance:** forge SUCCEEDS on the pre-fix code (`git checkout fcee705a^ -- sphincs-c10/src/{hash,fors,hypertree}.rs`, ~0.4 s per the commit) and FAILS on HEAD (post-fix; ~2^-149/grind). This is the regression test the fix never added — wire it as `sphincs-c10/tests/fors_forgery_resistance.rs` or a `tools/` host bin so it runs in CI. (grep confirmed no such collector/reassembler exists today.) **✅ DONE 2026-06-10:** `sphincs-c10/tests/fors_forgery_resistance.rs` (+ a `sim-internals` feature exposing `h_msg`/`extract_*` = the passive observer's PUBLIC-input capability). Heavy `#[ignore]` test harvests 3000 sigs, forge-grinds R over an attacker message until the digest's `ht_idx` + all 12 tree-leaves are covered, reassembles the forged sig, and `verify`s it. **Acceptance VALIDATED both directions:** on `fcee705a^` (pre-fix, via a worktree) the reassembled forgery `verifies = TRUE` (no secret key); on HEAD it `verifies = FALSE` (defeated). Fast CI guards in the same file: `reassembly_roundtrip_is_byte_exact`, `cross_position_tree_splice_is_rejected`, and `forgery_work_at_onchain_cap_exceeds_128_bits` — which reconstructs the post-fix per-grind forgery probability for a **full `MAX_SLOT_USES = 65,536` harvest** (the protocol's worst case, since a key rotates at the cap) WITHOUT signing (coverage is a function of public digests) and confirms **2^130.6 grinds** (independently matching the commit's claimed ~2^131 > 128-bit target; best position's bottleneck-tree coverage = 5/2048). Run: `cargo test -p sphincs-c10 --features sim-internals --test fors_forgery_resistance -- --ignored`.

Note: existing `sphincs-c10/tests/signing_suite.rs` only does single-message cross-key + byte-mutation negatives; `c10_test_vectors.json` tests each position once — none assert position-dependence. **✅ DONE 2026-06-10:** `sphincs-c10/tests/fors_position_binding.rs` (5 tests, ~0.03 s, CI-friendly): `fors_secret` unique per `ht_idx` for fixed `(tree,leaf)`; `fors_pk` + single-tree root unique per `ht_idx`; determinism/non-degeneracy sanity; and a structural assert that the FORS ADRS carries `ht_idx` in the tree field (bytes [4..12)). **Spawned by the 2026-05-22 external hardware-wallet-vuln review** (compass deep-research catalogue 2017–2026, cross-referenced against the threat model):

- [ ] **OLED display-emanation side-channel (CVE-2019-14354 class) — seed-wizard scope.** The SSD1306 panel's power/EM draw is content-correlated (the KeepKey/Ledger CVE-2019-14354 OLED side-channel). Our threat model treats the OLED only as a *trusted output* (§7.6 display-deception) and a privacy asset (S7) — **never as an emanation surface**, and `consumption-mask` (TIM2 PWM) masks crypto work, not the panel draw. The one window where recovery directly defeats the dual-SE XOR split is the **seed wizard rendering the 24 mnemonic words** (`secure/src/ui/seed_wizard.rs`). Secondary: a wrong-PIN error animation that distinguishes attempt-N (the three-way counter value is the secret). **OUT of scope** (state explicitly so a reviewer doesn't over-broaden): the measured-boot 8-word display (a hash of *public* firmware, meant to be compared to a public value — leaking it helps no one) and the tx-confirm screens (showing the tx to the user is the entire point). Candidate mitigations, cheap→strong: low-brightness/low-contrast wizard mode; pad the word slot with random pixels; reveal words **one-at-a-time with random inter-word delay** instead of all 24 simultaneously; scrambled-position reveal. Add a `§7.10 SCA` threat-model row regardless of which mitigation lands. Closest existing analog tracked is the Ledger Blue touchscreen-RF leak (no touchscreen here, but the PIN-feedback emanation is the button-OLED analog).
- [ ] **Correct the "Dark Skippy is IRRELEVANT (no nonce)" framing — it over-dismisses the anti-klepto class.** Dark Skippy *proper* (recover an ECDSA secret nonce via Pollard's Kangaroo) genuinely does not apply — no secret nonce exists in SLH-DSA. **But the broader anti-klepto class — a malicious signer leaking the seed through a transmitted signature field — does apply.** SLH-DSA ships the 16-byte randomizer `R` in the clear in every signature, and we make OptRand **mandatory + fresh-random per sign** (F-13, `secure/src/crypto.rs:118`, for the Saarinen SLotH SCA defence). Malicious firmware can write seed bytes into `opt_rand_buf` instead of TRNG output and grind the nonce to keep the FORS-last-index-0 constraint, exfiltrating the 256-bit seed in a handful of fully **on-chain-visible** signatures — *higher* bandwidth than ECDSA Dark Skippy, since `R` is transmitted directly with no curve math to invert. The multi-TRNG XOR (STM32⊕OPTIGA⊕SE050) defends a *biased* TRNG, **not** *malicious firmware* — orthogonal. **The defence we already have is measured-boot (8 BIP-39 words) + reproducible builds + the verify.pqsigner.io box-opening ceremony** — i.e. firmware *verifiability*, NOT "no nonce." Design tension worth recording: mandatory-random OptRand is an SCA win and an anti-klepto loss; deterministic OptRand would close the covert channel but reopen the chosen-message PRF-recovery SCA gap — measured-boot is the backstop that makes the current tradeoff the right one. **ACTION (docs-only, no code change):** reframe the 7 "IRRELEVANT / don't chase this" mentions (`docs/research-bundles/{A,B,C,E,D}-*.md`, `F-trezor-safe-7-comparison.md:5373`, `docs/ai-research-briefing.md:219`) to "anti-klepto covert channel exists in `R`; mitigated by firmware verifiability (measured boot + repro builds), not by absence of a nonce," and add a `§7.6`/`§7.7` threat-model row for "malicious-firmware seed exfiltration via the signature randomizer." **Reinforced 2026-05-22** by a second independent landscape survey (compass `wf-285b0852`) that names anti-klepto/anti-exfil an *industry baseline* (BitBox02 ships it for ECDSA since the Jan-2021 "Monte Rosa" release; Blockstream Jade too) — the class is mainstream, not exotic. **On whether we could implement the protocol itself:** the ECDSA host-contribution commit-reveal shape does **not** straightforwardly port to SLH-DSA — with no algebraic structure for the host to verify `R` against, a malicious device can grind `r_device` *before* it commits, the same difficulty as grinding `R` directly. Closing it properly needs verifier-side re-derivation of `R` (impossible without `sk` in the public verifier) or a NIZK that `R` was honestly derived from a committed PRF input — an open research direction. Measured-boot + reproducible builds stays the defensible answer today.

### 19. USB stack hardening (USB-C only attack surface)

**Status:** NOT STARTED (research complete — see `docs/production-security.md`)

Dev-research from Prompt D identified the USB path as our **largest remote attack surface** and surfaced concrete DWC2 errata workarounds and FI-resistant patterns.

**What's needed — P0:**
- [x] **FI-resistant `min()`** in every USB control-transfer length-clamping site. Defeats Colin O'Flynn USENIX WOOT 2019 EMFI-on-min attack. Pattern: compute `min(a,b)` then verify `result <= a && result <= b`; if not, recompute via explicit conditional. **Landed 2026-05-27** as `pqsigner_fi::fi_min` (commit `10fc644`); wired into the three live USB length-clamp sites (`HidFrameAssembler::process_frame` first + continuation frames in `shared/src/apdu_framing.rs`, `Transport::queue_response` in `nonsecure/src/usb/transport.rs`, `CommandRouter::get_response` chunk-out in `nonsecure/src/usb/commands.rs`).
- [x] **DWC2 TxFIFO write atomicity**: ensure no CSR access to other endpoints between successive FIFO writes of one endpoint, per STM32U5 DWC2 errata. Single-packet transfers (DIEPTSIZ.XFRSIZ = DIEPCTL.MPSIZ). **RESOLVED-BY-ARCHITECTURE 2026-05-28** (audit of `synopsys-usb-otg` v0.4). The erratum's precondition is "a CSR access to *another* endpoint lands between two FIFO word-writes of the endpoint being written." That requires a preempting context that touches another EP's `DIEPCTL`/`DOEPCTL` mid-write. Our config has none: (a) the NS world — which owns the whole USB stack — has **zero IRQ handlers** (USB is poll-driven from the NS main loop; same finding as the Zephyr CVE audit); (b) the only thing that can preempt NS is the **secure** SysTick, whose handler touches timeout / TAMP-SR / consumption-mask / idle-wipe and **never** the USB OTG register block. So no execution context can interleave another-EP CSR access during a FIFO write — the erratum cannot fire. (The crate's own `fifo_write` word-loop in `target.rs` is already CSR-clean; the gap the crate leaves — `bus.write()` not being in a critical section — only matters if a preempting handler hits another EP's CSR, which ours never does.) No code change; documented because a future move to an IRQ-driven USB path would re-open it.
- [~] **DWC2 ZLP race**: sequence SNAK/CNAK/EPENA per errata; flush all FIFOs on USB reset (GRSTCTL.RXFFLSH + GRSTCTL.TXFFLSH TXFNUM=0x10). **LOW-RISK-IN-POLLED-CONFIG 2026-05-28** (crate audit). The crate flushes RX on the USBRST poll branch (`bus.rs` `GRSTCTL.RXFFLSH`) and all FIFOs (incl. TX, `TXFNUM=0x10`) in the subsequent `reset()`→`configure_all()` that `usb-device` invokes when it detects the reset. The "stale TX between USBRST and reset()" window the erratum describes is an **IRQ-race** artifact; in our **polled** stack the window is just one poll-loop latency (µs) with no concurrent TX in flight (we're handling the reset, not transmitting). A defensive standalone TX-flush would require our own `GINTSTS.USBRST` poll, which races the `usb-device` crate's reset detection — net-negative. Deferred as low-value for the polled architecture; revisit if we ever move USB to IRQ-driven.
- [x] **Bounded APDU reassembly**: enforce `4 ≤ declared_len ≤ 4096` at seq=0; 5-second reassembly timeout with buffer scrub; abort-and-scrub if seq=0 arrives during active reassembly. **DONE.** Length-bound gate (`rx_expected ≤ MAX_APDU_RX`) was always present; abort-and-scrub on mid-reassembly seq=0 landed in `dff875c`; the 5 s timeout landed 2026-05-28 — `usb::usb_frame_number()` reads `OTG_DSTS.FNSOF` (free 1 ms-tick clock, advances only while the host sends SOFs), `Transport::check_rx_timeout` (called once per NS main-loop iteration) scrubs `rx_buf` + resets the assembler after `RX_REASSEMBLY_TIMEOUT_FRAMES = 5000`. HW-validated: the 8 KB manifest transfer (~150 frames) doesn't false-trip.
- [~] **HID OUT rate limiter**: token bucket ~200 reports/sec sustained, bucket size 64. NAK endpoint when empty. **DESCOPED-WITH-RATIONALE 2026-05-28.** The spec assumes ~1-HID-report-per-operation, but our wire format uses **APDU chaining** — a single logical op (e.g. the 8 KB FW_BEGIN manifest) fans out to ~33 chained sub-APDUs, each its own HID `seq=0` frame. A naive 200 reports/sec frame-level bucket would intermittently false-trip legitimate multi-APDU transfers (33 seq=0 frames in ~165 ms ≈ 200/sec, right at the cap). Limiting only `seq=0` frames has the same problem; limiting at the assembled-APDU level wouldn't match the spec's "reports/sec." More importantly the DoS vector it targets is **already covered twice over**: (1) the DWC2 RX FIFO auto-NAKs at the hardware level when we don't drain fast enough — built-in USB flow control, the device can't be overwhelmed at the wire; (2) the expensive path (signing) is gated by the F-17 sign-rate-limiter (1 sig/s + 250/session) + the PIN gate. The cheap path (bad frames) is dropped by the assembler in µs. So a frame-level token bucket adds churn-risk to legitimate chained transfers for marginal defense. Revisit only if profiling shows a real USB-level DoS that the RX-FIFO backpressure doesn't absorb.
- [x] **APDU CLA/INS allowlist** at non-secure before any NSC call. **Landed 2026-05-27** (commit `10fc644`) — `CommandRouter::dispatch` rejects unknown INS with `SW_INS_NOT_SUPPORTED` *before* `ChainState::step` accepts any payload, mirroring the explicit allowlist in `execute_chain`. Closes the prior path where a hostile host could burn up to `CHAIN_BUF_LEN ≈ 8 KB` of accumulation on an unknown INS before the execute-time arm rejected it.

**What's needed — P1:**
- [x] Force OTG_GUSBCFG.FDMOD = 1 (device-only mode), disable SOF interrupt (timing side-channel). **Landed 2026-05-27** (commit `dff875c`) — `nonsecure/src/usb/mod.rs::harden_otg` called from `usb::init` after the synopsys-usb-otg core soft-reset. Asserts `FDMOD=1` (belt-and-braces; the crate is already device-mode) + masks `GINTMSK.SOFM=0` explicitly so a future crate-version change can't silently enable the SOF-IRQ timing side-channel.
- [x] FIFO sizing per RM0456 formula with ≥30% safety margin. **VERIFIED-SAFE 2026-05-28** (crate audit). `synopsys-usb-otg` sizes RX = `total_rx_buffer_words + 30` and each TX = `max((mps+3)/4, 16)` words (16-word floor), with a config-time `assert!` guarding total-vs-`FIFO_DEPTH_WORDS` overflow. For our config (6 EPs, FS 64 B = 16 words each): RX = 6×16+30 = 126, TX0..5 = 96, total **222 / 320 words → ~31% headroom**. Already ≥30% and more conservative than the RM0456 ~83-word minimum. No override needed.
- [x] IWDG hang detection for USB path (2s timeout, kicked per transaction). **LANDED 2026-05-28** (commit `74dfdde`). Resolved all three constraints: (1) **TZ ownership** — kept IWDG secure-owned (kicked from secure SysTick); NS registers a heartbeat-counter address via a new `nsc_register_heartbeat` CMSE veneer (range-validated), no GTZC change. (2) **Cadence** — corrected "per transaction" to a per-main-loop-iteration NS heartbeat; SysTick feeds the IWDG while the heartbeat advances OR `handler_is_busy()` (covers the multi-second guarded veneers — sign, unlock, and now FW_BEGIN which gained a `HandlerGuard`); only a ~4 s stall with neither stops the kicks. (3) **Test disruption** — feature-gated `iwdg` (implies stm32u585), no-op stubs when off, `init` never starts the watchdog in an off-build; plus `DBGMCU.DBG_IWDG_STOP` freezes the counter under a debugger. `reset_cause` already treats an IWDG reset as abnormal + zeroizes SRAM. **Validation: COMPLETE.** Builds clean all configs + shared tests green. On-silicon `make fwup-transport-hw-iwdg` passed the FULL multi-phase sequence end-to-end with IWDG ON: 12 s idle-survival (no false-fire while idle) + happy-path BEGIN/CHUNK/COMMIT (no false-fire during the guarded ~3 s op) + all 4 failure-paths + wipe-trigger (wipe fires, SDIS disconnect observed, guard keeps the "Tamper detected" halt from being cut short). Trace confirms no false-fire anywhere (no STALL log, single boot). (Mid-session the bench USB transport went flaky and intermittently hung the run — proven NOT iwdg-related, the iwdg-less baseline hung identically — and a host reboot cleared it.)
- [x] Response-buffer locking for 17,088-byte SLH-DSA signatures (ISO 7816 SW=0x61xx chunking; 30s timeout). **DONE.** GET_RESPONSE chunking was already in place (`CommandRouter::get_response`, `APDU_MAX_RESP` chunks from `PENDING_PTR/LEN/POS`, FI-hardened `fi_min` clamp, `10fc644`); scrub-on-interleave landed `0d59b5f` (any non-GET_RESPONSE command mid-drain scrubs the cursor); the **30 s inter-chunk timeout landed `1059296`** — `CommandRouter::check_response_timeout(now_frame)` runs each NS poll-loop iteration, accumulates per-iteration `OTG_DSTS.FNSOF` deltas (summation sidesteps the 14-bit 16.384 s wrap since the loop polls at kHz so each delta is wrap-correct), and scrubs the pending buffer after 30 000 frames idle without a GET_RESPONSE; each GET_RESPONSE resets the accumulator.

**Verified 2026-04-15**: `CVE-2026-4179` is real (published 2026-03-16; Zephyr advisory `GHSA-9xg7-g3q3-9prf`). We initially flagged it as hallucinated because it was future-dated relative to our training cutoff — that flag was wrong. Safe to cite.

**Additional action item from CVE-2026-4179 verification**:
- [x] Audit whether our USB stack shares patterns with the Zephyr bug. The advisory describes `usb_write()` called from ISR context then triggering `k_yield()` → infinite loop. Our stack is Rust on top of the `synopsys-usb-otg` crate rather than Zephyr C. Still worth grepping our ISR handlers for: (a) scheduler calls from ISR context, (b) blocking waits inside IRQ, (c) any `yield!()`-equivalent patterns. Likely not affected since we're a different stack, but worth the 30 minutes. **Audit complete 2026-05-27**: structurally impossible for us. NS has **no IRQ handlers at all** (USB stack is poll-driven via `device.poll()` in the NS main loop). Secure-world ISRs (`SysTick`, `TZIC` violation, `TAMP`) touch timeout / consumption-mask / TAMP-SR only — none reach the USB OTG block. The Zephyr "ISR → scheduler → infinite loop" pattern can't manifest because the prerequisite ISR doesn't exist.

**Files to create:** `secure/src/fih.rs` (shared with #18)
**Files to change:** `secure/src/hw/usb_hw.rs`, `nonsecure/src/usb/transport.rs`, `nonsecure/src/usb/hid.rs`, `nonsecure/src/usb/commands.rs`

### 20. Production key management (SCP03 rotation + HUK-SAES + binding record)

**Status:** NOT STARTED (research complete — see `docs/production-security.md`)

Prompt B surfaced a concrete production-provisioning protocol. Supersedes the brief HUK-SAES note in item #7.

**What's needed — P0:**
- [ ] **Two-stage RDP provisioning**. Stage 1 at RDP0: read all 3 UIDs (STM32, SE050, OPTIGA), derive per-device SCP03 keys via CMAC-KDF(FMK, label, SE050_UID), rotate SE050 SCP03 from NXP default KVN=0x0B → KVN=0x11 via PUT KEY. Stage 2 at RDP1+: wrap all secrets with real DHUK via SAES (DHUK is a known constant at RDP0 — wrapping there is meaningless).
- [ ] **Two-level SAES wrapping**: DHUK-ECB wraps 256-bit MasterKey → HKDF-SHA256(MasterKey, purpose) derives per-use keys → AES-GCM per-use wraps SCP03 / PBS / binding separately. Single-level DHUK-ECB has no integrity.
- [ ] **Per-device SCP03 keys via CMAC-KDF** with SE050 UID as context. Mass-clone defence.
- [ ] **OPTIGA PBS lifecycle lock**: OID 0xE140 to Operational state, Read=Never, Change=Conf(0xE140). Irreversible after provisioning.
- [ ] ~~**Binding record** signed by provisioner at factory: bind(STM32_UID, SE050_UID, OPTIGA_UID, fw_version, ts) signed ECDSA-P256~~ → **SUPERSEDED by #22** — use SLH-DSA-128s manifest with firmware_hash inclusion instead. Implement #22, retire this ECDSA design.
- [ ] ~~**Boot-time anti-swap verify**~~ → **SUPERSEDED by #22** — the full boot-time ceremony including both SE-attested reads + firmware-hash verification is in #22.

**What's needed — P1:**
- [ ] OPTIGA monotonic counter (OID 0xF1E0, Conf(0xE140) protected) for firmware anti-rollback.
- [ ] Blob format versioning for smooth firmware upgrades (magic 0x504B4559, version byte, HKDF label).
- [ ] SE050 attestation on every boot (ReadObject_W_Attst with key 0xF0000012) — optional, +100ms boot time.
- [ ] OPTIGA cert chain verification against pinned Infineon Root CA — optional.

**Verification items** (remaining after 2026-04-15 web verification round):
- [x] ~~Confirm NXP default SCP03 keys against current AN12436 revision~~ — **VERIFIED**. AN12436 Rev 2.4 (July 2024), all three hex values match byte-for-byte. Safe to use.
- [ ] Confirm SAES register bit-field positions against RM0456 + CMSIS `stm32u585xx.h` header (research author flagged as unknown — not verified in web-search round).
- [ ] Confirm STM32U585 DHUK actually returns a known constant at RDP0 (not merely "documented behaviour") — DHUK semantics may differ per errata.

**Files to create:** `provisioning/` (new host-side tooling crate), `secure/src/hw/saes.rs`, `secure/src/attestation.rs`, `secure/src/binding.rs`
**Files to change:** `secure/src/main.rs` (boot-time anti-swap gate), `secure/src/se050/scp03.rs` (per-device keys), `secure/src/optiga/shield.rs` (PBS lifecycle lock)

### 21. TAMP + CSS + PVD configuration (hardware supervisor Stage 2)

**Status:** NOT STARTED (already planned as Stage 2 of brownout roadmap — confirmed + expanded by research)

Confirmed by Prompt A that factory defaults are "dangerously insecure." Masaryk U 76% PIN-glitch bypass on STM32U5A9 (same core family) traced directly to defaults.

**What's needed — P0 (one-time at provisioning, burned in option bytes):**
- [ ] `BOR_LEV` = 3 or 4 in FLASH_OPTR. Narrows voltage-glitch window hardware-wide.
- [ ] `IWDG_SW` = 0 (hardware watchdog, cannot be disabled by firmware). 100-500 ms timeout.
- [ ] `IWDG_STOP` = 0 + `IWDG_STDBY` = 0 (continue in low-power modes).
- [ ] `SRAM2_ECC` = 1, `SRAM3_ECC` = 1 (ECC is OFF by default on U5 — earlier doc claim was wrong).
- [ ] `SRAM2_RST` = 0 (auto-erase SRAM2 on every system reset).

**What's needed — P0 (runtime config at boot):**
- [ ] PVD enabled at highest threshold below 3.3V via `PWR_SVMCR.PVDE` + `PVDLS[2:0]`. EXTI16 handler zeroizes secrets + last-gasp write to backup register.
- [ ] TAMP internal tampers: ITAMP1 (VBAT voltage), ITAMP2 (temperature), ITAMP3 (LSE CSS) all enabled with automatic backup-domain erasure on trigger.
- [ ] CSS (Clock Security System) on HSE: `RCC_CR.CSSON` = 1.
- [ ] ECCD double-bit NMI handler: read `RAMCFG_MxISR` + `RAMCFG_MxFEAR`, zeroize, soft-reset.

**Hardware:**
- [ ] **0.47-1 F supercap on VBAT** via Schottky from Vdd (1N5819 + 47Ω optional series R). See the decision rationale in `docs/brownout-hardening.md` "VBAT power source: supercap, not battery." Gives ~12-24 h bounded tamper retention — acceptable vs battery chemistry in enclosure.
- [ ] For dev board: tack-solder to unpopulated CR1220 holder pads on back of board.

**Files to create:** `secure/src/hw/power.rs` (PVD + PVM), `secure/src/hw/tamp.rs` (tamper config + backup regs)
**Files to change:** `Makefile` (`stm32-harden-opts` target — add SRAM2/SRAM3 ECC + IWDG option bytes), `secure/src/main.rs` (boot-time PVD + TAMP init)

### 22. Supply-chain + provisioning attestation (triple-UID binding, SLH-DSA manifest)

**Status:** NOT STARTED (bundle E research complete — see `docs/production-security.md` §2.5; raw result at `docs/research-bundles/results/compass_artifact_wf-b5bd18ff-...md`)

> See also #23 for a complementary anti-interdiction layer: "ship without firmware" (Trezor Safe 7 pattern) eliminates firmware-tampering during shipping without needing the attestation ceremony to execute on a potentially hostile binary. Defence in depth, not a replacement.

> **Trezor MCU-attestation parallel (2026-05-19 re-audit, see `docs/trezor-comparison.md` §D1).** Trezor landed an ML-DSA-44 MCU device-attestation primitive on 2026-03-18 (`core/embed/sec/mcu_attestation/`) using `pq-code-package/mldsa-native` as the verified reference library, then **disabled the host-side wire** on 2026-05-07 with no changelog (`8bc6d1b28b`). On-device infrastructure still compiles in (`USE_MCU_ATTESTATION=1` for T3W1) — only the `AuthenticityProof` host-bound field was un-populated. Reads as "device done, deployment pipeline not yet ready." Lessons for our §22 rollout: (a) vendor `mldsa-native` if/when we want a per-boot fast attestation sig alongside the factory SLH-DSA-128s manifest; (b) Trezor derives the PQ key from a privileged master-key slot per signature (no persistent ML-DSA keypair on chip) — our §22 plan has the SLH-DSA private key burned at provisioning instead, a different trade-off worth flagging; (c) **don't announce attestation to users until the host-side acceptance path (companion + WebUSB verifier) AND the cert chain anchoring to a published factory pubkey are both shipping** — Trezor sat on the device side for 7 weeks before the walkback.

Bundle E extends item #20 with a **triple-UID cryptographically-signed manifest** + transparency log + user-facing WebUSB box-opening ceremony. This closes the single-chip-replacement attack surface that has affected every shipping hardware wallet (Trezor Safe 3, Ledger Snake demo, ColdCard firmware-reset). No shipping wallet implements what's described here today.

**Relationship to #20**: #20's ECDSA-P256 binding record is **superseded** by the SLH-DSA-128s manifest design in this item. Implement #22 and retire the ECDSA-P256 path from #20 rather than shipping both.

**What's needed — P0:**

- [ ] **STM32U585 anti-counterfeit probes at boot**: CPUID (expect Cortex-M33 r0p4, DEV_ID `0x482`), UID validation at `0x0BFA_0590` (lot ASCII check, wafer < 25, not all-0/all-0xFF), DHUK probe via SAES against factory-recorded value, errata fingerprinting (`DBGMCU_DBG_AUTH_DEVICE.AUTH_ID` reads zero at RDP0 quirk; MSI low-drift). Halt on any anomaly.
- [ ] **SLH-DSA-128s factory signing key**: M-of-N key ceremony with geographically distributed shares; air-gapped factory HSM; factory pubkey fingerprint published.
- [ ] **Binding manifest in CBOR** with schema: `manifest_type`, 3× UIDs, `firmware_hash` (SHA3-256 over the image — this ties chip identity to a specific firmware build, not in #20), `firmware_version`, `device_serial` = SHA3-256(3 UIDs), `production_ts`, `manifest_version`, `factory_pubkey_fp`. Signed SLH-DSA-128s.
- [ ] **Manifest stored 3×**: SE050 as binary secure object, OPTIGA Trust M data object, STM32 internal flash. Plus SHA3-256 anchor to STM32 OTP bytes 6-37 (already in #20).
- [ ] **SE050 attestation at boot**: `Se05x_API_ReadObject_W_Attst` with 16-byte freshness nonce + key `0xF0000012` → ECDSA-SHA256 signed response containing 18-byte chipId. Verify chain to pinned NXP root CA. ⚠ **Variant gate**: confirm we're on SE050 **C, E, or F** variant — A/B/D do NOT have pre-provisioned attestation certs at OID `0xF0000013`.
- [ ] **OPTIGA attestation at boot**: `optiga_crypt_ecdsa_sign` with key `0xE0F0`, cert from OID `0xE0E0`, UID from `0xE0C2`. Chain signature to pinned Infineon OPTIGA ECC Root CA 2.
- [ ] **Boot-time ceremony** (runs in secure world before entropy reconstruction): read STM32 UID → load manifest → verify SLH-DSA signature → compare all 3 UIDs to manifest + against each SE's own attested response → compare SHA3-256(firmware) against manifest.firmware_hash → check anti-rollback counter → ATTESTATION_PASSED. Any mismatch → permanent lockdown (no entropy release).
- [ ] **Transparency log**: append-only public record of every `device_serial` + manifest hash emitted at the factory. Merkle-anchored (scheme TBD — see research prompt). Enables detection of rogue production runs even under HSM compromise.
- [ ] **RDP Level 2** burned at end of provisioning (also in #20 — coordinate).

**Reference point**: Trezor Safe 7 (verified real, announced Oct 21 2025, shipping late 2025 / early 2026) is the closest existing production-wallet architecture. Uses TROPIC01 as transparent SE + EAL6+ second SE for dual attestation. PQSigner adds the triple-UID SLH-DSA manifest on top. Worth studying Trezor's public documentation on Safe 7's attestation protocol before we finalise our own — we may learn from their transparent-SE approach, and we should be explicit where our design intentionally diverges.
- [ ] Study Trezor Safe 7 attestation protocol (public docs + any security evaluations) and document how our triple-UID SLH-DSA design differs. May surface improvements or shared patterns.

**What's needed — P1:**

- [ ] **WebUSB verification page** at `verify.pqsigner.io`: browser sends fresh random challenge via WebUSB → both SEs sign → server-side independent verification of NXP + Infineon cert chains + manifest signature + UID consistency → displays ✓ + device serial. No customer tool install required. Three independent trust anchors converge (NXP root, Infineon root, factory SLH-DSA pubkey).
- [ ] **M-of-N factory HSM ceremony procedures**: documented operational runbook; number of shares; threshold; geographic distribution; rotation schedule.
- [ ] **Transparency-log audit tooling**: periodic audit that every shipped device's serial appears in the log; alerting on anomalies.
- [ ] **Automatic USB CDC self-attestation** on every first-connect: device emits a structured attestation report over serial (status, serial, per-chip verification, firmware version, manifest signature validity). Complements WebUSB for users who prefer command-line verification.

**What's needed — P2:**

- [ ] Recovery / warranty procedure for devices that brick due to genuine hardware failure vs. attestation failure. UX for distinguishing "we got a bad SE" from "someone tried to swap chips."
- [ ] Carrier / distributor notifications when transparency-log audit detects anomalies.

**Verification items after 2026-04-15 web verification round**:
- [x] ~~Ledger Donjon March 2025 Trezor Safe 3 attack~~ — **VERIFIED REAL**. Blog post at `ledger.com/why-secure-elements-make-a-crucial-difference-to-hardware-wallet-security` (March 12, 2025). Trezor confirmation at `trezor.io/vulnerability/donjon-s-trezor-safe-3-evaluation`.
- [x] ~~Trezor Safe 7 with TROPIC01~~ — **VERIFIED REAL**. Announced October 21, 2025; shipping late 2025 / early 2026. Dual attestation (TROPIC01 + EAL6+ SE).
- [x] ~~Masaryk U Simonik thesis 76% PIN-glitch~~ — **VERIFIED REAL**. Bachelor's thesis on fault injection vs STM32U5 (Trezor Safe 5). Reference: `it4sec.substack.com/p/fault-injection-attack-on-the-stm32u5`.
- [x] ~~BlaatSchaap research on STM32F103 clones~~ — **VERIFIED REAL**. `blaatschaap.be/identifying-32f103-clones/` and multi-part Cortex-M series. Safe to cite.
- [x] ~~TheCharlatan May 2020 ColdCard firmware-reset~~ — **VERIFIED REAL**. `thecharlatan.ch/COLDCARD-Supply-Chain/`.

"Riscure LFI on ColdCard" (Mk2 ATECC508A single-shot; Mk3 ATECC608A multi-shot) was actually done by **Ledger Donjon (Olivier Hériveaux)**, not Riscure.
