### 32. Duress PIN → decoy wallet (plausible deniability, Keycard-pattern)

**Status:** SCOPED 2026-05-20; **feasibility VALIDATED on silicon 2026-05-20** (`make duress-probe-hw`, PASS on real B-U585I-IOT02A + OPTIGA Trust M + SE050). The probe confirmed every load-bearing assumption, all at LcsO=Creation (nothing locked, fully recoverable): (1) a SECOND OPTIGA AuthRef at F1D8 with `Execute=ALW` (no E120/LUC binding) provisions cleanly **without moving E120**; (2) HMAC-auth against F1D8 succeeds and **leaves E120 at 0** — a duress AuthRef with no LUC binding never bumps the silicon lockout counter; (3) the real F1D0 still bumps E120 (0→1) on wrong PIN + resets on correct PIN with F1D8 present — adding the duress credential didn't break the real lockout; (4) a SECOND SE050 UserID (`max_attempts=0`, unlimited) provisions + auths alongside the real one. The probe lives behind the `duress-probe-e2e` feature (`OptigaTrustM::probe_provision_duress_authref` / `probe_hmac_auth_at`, `Se050::probe_provision_and_auth_duress_userid`) + `make duress-probe-hw`. **Implication for the counter/wipe open question (P3):** since F1D8 auth is decoupled from E120, the duress path itself never drifts the real E120 — the only drift source is the always-run real-F1D0 verify failing during a duress entry (the timing-uniformity requirement), exactly as analysed. Implementation (P1/P3/P4) not started. Supersedes §23's "BIP-39 passphrase" line as the chosen deniability mechanism — same goal ($5-wrench / coercion defence), but a second numeric PIN reusing the existing PIN-entry UI instead of an ASCII passphrase keyboard. Modelled on Keycard's duress PIN (`keycard-shell/app/keycard/keycard.c`): a second PIN unlocks a decoy wallet; always-provisioned (random if the user declines) so its presence reveals nothing.

**Decoy-entropy model: SEPARATE entropy (user decision 2026-05-20, the stronger option).** The duress credential gates a DISTINCT XOR-split entropy; the real seed never enters SRAM during a duress session. Robust even against an attacker who duress-unlocks then dumps SRAM. Bonus: this *removes* the "duress flag must flow through every NSC handler" requirement that the same-entropy model would have needed — handlers just operate on whichever entropy got loaded, and the decoy entropy produces only the decoy wallet through the unchanged derivation path.

**Design:**
- **Two SE credentials per chip.** Real PIN = existing lockout-protected credential (SE050 UserID with `max_attempts`, OPTIGA F1D0 AuthRef bound to E120 LUC). Duress PIN = a SECOND credential provisioned with **unlimited attempts** (SE050 `max_attempts=0` like the admin UserID; OPTIGA AuthRef WITHOUT the E120-LUC lockout binding) so it never itself triggers the wipe. Real PIN ≠ duress PIN enforced at setup.
- **Two XOR-split entropies.** Real: `real_half_o` (OPTIGA, gated by real AuthRef) ⊕ `real_half_e` (SE050, gated by real UserID). Decoy: `decoy_half_o` ⊕ `decoy_half_e` under the duress credentials. Invariant #1 (neither chip alone reveals a seed) holds for BOTH entropies.
- **Timing-uniform unlock (mandatory).** `gated_unlock` must run BOTH credential verifies on every unlock — never short-circuit on duress success — else the real path's extra SE round-trip lets a coercion attacker *time* which PIN is real and defeat the deniability. Select which entropy to release from which credential matched; discard the unused verify's result.
- **Counter / wipe — semantics (locked) + drift fix (open design decision).**
  - *Lockout semantics (settled):* an entered PIN is classified by which credential it matches. **Real PIN → real wallet, counter resets. Duress PIN → decoy wallet, counter ALSO resets** (a coerced user must enter it smoothly + repeatedly; it must NOT bump or wipe). **Neither (genuinely wrong) → both verifies fail → MCU page-124 counter stays bumped.** The "bump" is keyed on matching *neither* credential, so a correctly-entered duress PIN never counts toward lockout. **10 consecutive genuinely-wrong → `trigger_lockout_wipe()` → `factory_reset_admin`, identical to today.** The unlimited duress credential (`max_attempts=0` / `Execute=ALW`) does NOT weaken brute-force protection: a brute-forcer hits "neither" on every random guess → the MCU master counter climbs → wipe at 10; the unlimited credential never helps an attacker who doesn't know the duress PIN (and if they do, it just opens the decoy). The wipe destroys **both** wallets' state — the real is recoverable from the 24-word seed; the **decoy (separate random entropy) is NOT recoverable**, so any bait funds are lost on wipe and a re-provisioned device gets a *new* decoy (acceptable for a sacrifice wallet, inherent to the separate-entropy model).
  - *Timing-channel measured on silicon 2026-05-20 (`make duress-timing-hw`):* one OPTIGA HMAC verify = **~160 ms** (157–167, n=15), one SE050 UserID verify = **~103 ms** (102.6–105.4), so the skip-vs-run "extra real verify" delta = **~263 ms, ~6% jitter**. Unlock is SE-bound (the ~1–3 s C10 keygen runs later on first sign/address, NOT during unlock), so 263 ms is a large, low-jitter, **observable** fraction of unlock time. **Conclusion: timing uniformity IS load-bearing** — the advisor's "verifies are ~10–50 ms so just skip the real verify" path is OFF the table (the verifies are 2–3× slower than assumed). We cannot skip the real verify on a duress match without leaking ~263 ms.
  - *Drift fix — three options, the data narrows it to two real ones:*
    - **(A) Run both verifies every unlock (perfect timing uniformity, real drift).** The real F1D0/UserID verify runs on a duress entry (fails) → bumps the real counters → drift. Fix the drift by binding the reset to the duress credential: OPTIGA E120 `Change = Auto(F1D0) OR Auto(F1D8)` (validate the reset-via-F1D8 path on silicon), and accept SE050 user UserID reduced to **informational** (it can't be reset by another credential — silicon has no such primitive) → three-way lockstep becomes **two-of-three** (MCU + OPTIGA E120). Stronger on timing, weaker on the direct-I2C-attacker defence.
    - **(B) Skip the real verify on a duress match + busy-wait a calibrated ~263 ms ± jitter (approximate timing uniformity, NO drift).** The real verify NEVER runs on a duress entry → no counter touched → **full three-way lockstep preserved**, no E120 metadata change, no SE050 weakening, simpler. Cost: the dummy delay must match the real-verify distribution (~263 ms mean, ~6% jitter) or a residual remains; mitigate by drawing the delay from the measured distribution and/or adding randomized jitter to BOTH paths. Stronger on lockout integrity, slightly weaker on timing perfection (~±10 ms residual vs A's perfect match).
    - **(C) Skip + no padding (~263 ms leak) — REJECTED** (defeats the deniability the feature exists for).
    **DECIDED 2026-05-20: option B.** Spec check (`plug-and-trust` AN12413-derived `se05x_APDU_apis.h`) confirmed SE050's `MAX_ATTEMPTS` is **creation-only** ("only when the object identifier is not in use yet") and there is **no `POLICY_OBJ_*` bit / APDU to reset one auth object's attempt counter via another credential** — so A's SE050 drift is genuinely unfixable and A is forced to reduce SE050 to informational (two-of-three). B avoids the drift on both chips and keeps full three-way lockstep. The old MCU-only-wipe "alternative" is subsumed by A and moot.

  - *B — minimizing the timing residual (the "real-operation pad"):* do NOT pad with a NOP busy-wait (that residual = "my fixed-delay estimate vs reality"). Instead, on a duress match, run a **second verify against the duress credential** as the pad — a real SE operation of the same type, with the same natural jitter, that touches only the unlimited duress counter (harmless, no drift). Structure: (1) verify duress credential FIRST; (2a) if it SUCCEEDS → decoy unlock + run a second duress verify as the pad (skip the real verify entirely → real counters untouched); (2b) if it FAILS → run the real verify (real PIN → real wallet + counter resets; wrong PIN → real counter bumps → lockout). Every path runs exactly two SE verifies → timing uniform; the real counters are touched only on real/wrong entries, never on duress → no drift. **SE050 side is a perfect match** (duress UserID verify and user UserID verify are the identical operation, both ~103 ms). **OPTIGA side — plain-vs-auto-state delta MEASURED on silicon 2026-05-20 (`make duress-timing-hw`):** F1D8 **plain** verify = ~160 ms (157.6–167.2, n=15); F1D0 **auto-state/LUC** verify = ~149 ms (147.2–151.0, n=15); **delta ~10.4 ms** (auto-state is *faster* — leaner Trezor 16 B-nonce shape vs the plain path's 64 B compound input). So a simple second-plain-F1D8 pad leaves a **~11 ms CONSISTENT residual** (duress path runs ~11 ms slower) — a 24× reduction from the raw 263 ms, and practically unexploitable for coercion (an attacker can't calibrate a baseline without already knowing which PIN is which), but consistent (not jitter) so a precision rig averaging many unlocks could in principle detect it. **DECIDED 2026-05-20: matched-LUC duress credential** (null the residual). Bind the duress F1D8 to its OWN LUC counter at a second LUC slot (OPTIGA has 4: E120–E123 → use e.g. E121) so the duress verify uses the same **auto-state** path (~149 ms) as the real F1D0 verify → the pad is a byte-for-byte timing twin → ~0 residual. The duress LUC is **unenforced** (never `curr>=limit`-checked) — it just counts harmlessly. No drift: the real E120 is only touched on real/wrong entries; the duress LUC absorbs the duress-path bumps. Cost: one extra LUC counter object + the duress credential uses the auto-state path (both within P2 provisioning). The simple-plain pad (~11 ms residual) was the rejected fallback.
  - *Dual-LUC coexistence — VALIDATED ON SILICON 2026-05-20 (`make duress-probe-hw` step 10, PASS).* The matched-LUC config (F1D8 `Execute=LUC(E121)` re-provisioned alongside F1D0 `Execute=LUC(E120)`) was the one untested OPTIGA AC expression; the probe now confirms all three load-bearing assumptions on a real B-U585I + OPTIGA Trust M: **(a) two LUC-bound AuthRefs coexist** at LcsO=Creation — F1D8 re-provisioned from plain to `Execute=LUC(E121)` cleanly (Change=ALW, never locked); **(b) bump isolation holds** — one duress auto-state verify moved E121 0→1 while the real E120 stayed at 15 (zero drift on the real lockout counter); **(c) timing twin holds** — F1D8 auto-state mean **22428 µs** vs F1D0 auto-state **21598 µs**, ~830 µs delta, well inside the ~10 ms per-verify jitter band → the option-B pad is a byte-for-byte timing twin and the ~1179 µs plain-vs-auto-state residual is nulled. Probe scaffolding: `OptigaTrustM::probe_provision_duress_authref_luc` / `probe_read_counter` + apdu `build_metadata_auth_ref_luc_oid` / `build_metadata_pin_ctr_oid` (the latter two double as the P1 production builders). The matched-LUC layout is now safe to bake into P1/P2 code. *(Absolute per-verify cost here is ~22 ms OPTIGA / ~104 ms SE050, not the ~149 ms placeholder previously written — corrected.)*

**Phases (medium-large, multi-session — touches the validated three-way PIN lockstep + provisioning ceremony):**
- [x] **P1 — derivation + provisioning of a second entropy — LANDED 2026-05-20 (`duress-pin` feature).** `crypto::provision_from_mnemonic` now takes `duress_pin: Option<&[u8;8]>` and, after the real wallet, generates a FRESH independent 256-bit decoy entropy (separate-entropy model: STM32 TRNG ⊕ SE-combined TRNG) and provisions a decoy wallet via the new `WalletStore::provision_duress`. **Always-provision**: `None` (user declined) provisions a decoy with a fresh random 8-byte PIN, so "duress configured vs not" is indistinguishable on-chip. Host tests pin the always-provision + independent-entropy + invariant-#1-on-the-decoy properties (`negative_dual_se_duress_no_full_entropy_to_a_single_chip`, `positive_crypto_duress_always_provisions_decoy`). All 1916 secure-crate host tests pass.
- [x] **P2 — second SE credential (storage) — LANDED 2026-05-20 (`duress-pin`).** Coexistence feasibility was proven on silicon (`make duress-probe-hw`); production storage now wired: `WalletStore::provision_duress` (default no-op; real impls on OPTIGA / SE050 / DualSE). OPTIGA `store_duress_objects` provisions E121 matched-LUC counter + F1D8 AuthRef (`Execute=LUC(E121)`) + 4 decoy data OIDs (F1D9/F1DA/F1DB/F1D6) gated on F1D8, never locking (stays LcsO=Creation). SE050 `store_duress_objects` provisions `DURESS_USERID_OBJ` (max_attempts=0, unlimited) + 3 decoy data objects, all carrying the admin-delete policy so `factory_reset_admin` reclaims them; includes an admin-auth stale sweep for idempotent re-provision. DualSE XOR-splits the decoy entropy (fresh 3-source half) and dispatches to both. `is_provisioned()` stays real-only; `duress_is_provisioned()` is the new diagnostic. Builds clean across `duress-pin` / `duress-probe-e2e` (no duress-pin) / `mock-se+ui` matrices. **Remaining for P2:** (a) ~~on-silicon validation of the FULL production `provision_duress`~~ — **DONE 2026-05-20 (`make duress-provision-hw`, PASS).** Provisioned a real wallet + an independent decoy via the production `provision`/`provision_duress` path with a known decoy entropy, read both decoy halves (OPTIGA F1D9 via F1D8 auto-state auth + SE050 `DURESS_ENTROPY_OBJ` via duress UserID auth): `half_o XOR half_e` == known decoy entropy (production XOR-split correct end-to-end); the decoy read bumped ONLY E121 (16→17), real E120 untouched at 15; real wallet still unlocked with the real PIN. E121 idempotency-skip worked; all at LcsO=Creation. (b) ~~re-provision idempotency~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-05-20 (`make duress-provision-hw` steps 15–19, PASS).** The feared brick was a false alarm: the duress data OIDs use `Change=Auto(F1D8) OR Conf(E140)` (via `build_metadata_protected`), so the shielded-connection `Conf(E140)` arm rewrites them on re-provision exactly like the real OIDs — no F1D8 auth needed. E121's `Change=Auto(F1D8)` (no Conf escape) is handled by the idempotency-skip (metadata persists across a wipe → skipped) + the high `DURESS_CTR_LIMIT` (stale current value is unenforced). `factory_reset_body` now ALSO best-effort blanks the duress OIDs (F1D8/F1D9/F1DA/F1DB/F1D6) for hygiene/parity (E121 left intact). Silicon-validated end-to-end: wipe (`factory_reset_admin`) → re-provision real + a NEW decoy entropy/PIN (mnemonic-change case) → read both halves back → reconstruct the NEW entropy → `gated_unlock(new duress)` + `gated_unlock(real)` both work. (c) **Provisioning wall-time** roughly doubles under `duress-pin` (E121 + F1D8 + 4 decoy OIDs on OPTIGA, each with the hard-reset dance, + the SE050 duress objects) — expect the first-boot wizard to run noticeably longer. The unlock-side dispatch is P3.
- [x] **P3 — timing-uniform dual-verify `gated_unlock`** + the counter/wipe design decision above. **DONE 2026-05-20 — implemented + advisor-reviewed (diff) + SILICON-VALIDATED (`make duress-provision-hw` steps 10–14, PASS on real B-U585I + OPTIGA + SE050):** `gated_unlock(duress)` → decoy master through the production dispatch with the real E120 untouched (real verify genuinely skipped, no drift); `gated_unlock(real)` → real master (falls through after the duress verify fails); `gated_unlock(wrong)` → rejected; real unlock recovers after a wrong+duress sequence. Landed: `WalletStore::unlock_duress` (default `Err(PinIncorrect)`) + `duress_pad` (default no-op); `DualSE::unlock_duress` reads both decoy halves via the production `duress_read_half` (promoted from e2e gating) with NO short-circuit (both chips always verified → uniform op-count), XOR-reconstructs the decoy entropy, caches the decoy entropy-blob, returns the decoy master; `DualSE::duress_pad` runs one duress verify per chip; OPTIGA/SE050 `duress_verify` (verify-only); `gated_unlock` feature-gated duress-first dispatch (try `unlock_duress`; on Ok run `duress_pad` + return; else fall through to real `unlock`) preserving the existing FI hardening + the MCU pre-commit/reset (a duress-correct unlock resets the MCU counter exactly like a real success). Both correct paths execute 4 SE verifies + 2 reads → uniform total latency. 2 new source-grep host tests; 1918 host tests pass; builds clean across duress-pin / duress-provision-e2e / mock-se. **Advisor-reviewed 2026-05-20 (diff); two fixes landed (`0fc97ff`):** (i) **E121 lifetime lockout** — the duress F1D8 verify fires LUC(E121) on EVERY unlock, INCLUDING the failed duress verify `unlock_duress` runs on a real-PIN entry (the no-short-circuit timing structure); that bump is UNRESETTABLE (E121.Change=Auto(F1D8) needs a *successful* F1D8 auth), so E121 climbs ~1/unlock regardless of duress use. The load-bearing fix is the high limit `DURESS_CTR_LIMIT = 0x00FF_FFFF` (~460 yr @ 100 unlocks/day); a duress-correct unlock ALSO resets E121 to 0 (F1D8 auth active) to bound the decoy-heavy case — but that reset only covers duress use, NOT the real-entry climb, so the doc's "reset E121 on duress success" must be read with that qualification. (ii) **FI master cross-check** — `unlock_duress` now reads the stored decoy master (F1DA) in the F1D8 session and cross-checks `kdf(reconstructed)==stored` via two `ct_eq` + the hamming-distant sentinel (parity with `DualSE::unlock`). *Note:* the E121 reset write rewrites `(0, DURESS_CTR_LIMIT)`, so the first duress-correct unlock on an already-provisioned bench chip (E121 limit 0xFFFF from the probe era) silently MIGRATES the limit up to 0x00FF_FFFF — expected, free, one-time. **STILL NOT validated on silicon** (the gated_unlock dispatch — real/duress/wrong PINs — has not been run on hardware), and the read-position sub-ordering differs between the duress (V,R,V…) and real (V,V,R) paths (total time uniform; fine-grained I2C order differs — out of the coercion threat model). **DESIGN (as implemented):**
  - *Dispatch — every path does exactly 2 SE verifies → uniform timing (an observer can't tell real vs duress vs wrong from latency):*
    1. Pre-commit bump MCU page-124 (unchanged from today's `gated_unlock`).
    2. Verify the **duress** credential first (OPTIGA F1D8 auto-state + SE050 duress UserID).
       - **Duress match** → SKIP the real verify; run a SECOND duress verify as the matched-LUC timing pad (fires LUC(E121), the validated ~0-residual twin of the real F1D0 auto-state verify). Reconstruct the DECOY entropy (`unlock_duress` read path: OPTIGA F1D9 + SE050 `DURESS_ENTROPY_OBJ`, XOR, cross-check vs decoy master). Return the **decoy** master. Reset MCU page-124 + E121.
       - **No duress match** → verify the **real** credential (F1D0 auto-state + SE050 real UserID).
         - **Real match** → reconstruct REAL entropy (existing `DualSE::unlock`). Return real master. Reset MCU page-124 + E120.
         - **Neither** → wrong PIN. Leave MCU bumped (10 ⇒ `factory_reset_admin` wipe, unchanged). E120 (real verify) + E121 (duress verify) already bumped by the silicon LUCs.
  - *Why duress-first:* lets the duress-match path SKIP the real verify (no E120 drift) while the pad keeps timing uniform. The real-match + wrong paths both run duress-verify + real-verify = 2 verifies; the duress-match path runs duress-verify + pad-duress-verify = 2 verifies. Symmetric.
  - *Counter semantics:* a CORRECT duress PIN is a *successful* unlock (of the decoy) — it MUST reset the MCU counter exactly like a real success, else timing/lockout-state would distinguish duress from real (breaks deniability). Reset E121 on duress success (Change=Auto(F1D8) authorises it). E120 is never touched on a duress unlock (real verify skipped) — frozen-but-fine. A wrong-on-both entry bumps all counters (per the user's "duress wrong PIN must also count toward the 10").
  - *Transparency:* `gated_unlock` returns the master (real OR decoy); the caller / `SecureState` caches it as `master_secret` and ALL downstream (signing, address, off-chain) use it unchanged — the decoy wallet simply *is* "the wallet" for that session. No caller learns which wallet unlocked (deniability).
  - *New production surface needed:* `unlock_duress` read path on OPTIGA / SE050 / DualSE (the `duress-provision-e2e` `duress_read_half` helpers are the validated preview — promote to production, add the master cross-check + entropy-blob cache like `DualSE::unlock`). Restructured `gated_unlock` must preserve the existing FI hardening (F-2 sentinels, double-reads, `wait_random` on both sides of each verify, fail-in verdict) across the new two-verify shape — extend, don't weaken.
  - *Open questions for review:* (1) ordering of the two SE chips within each verify (OPTIGA-then-SE050) for timing symmetry across the duress/real branches; (2) whether the pad must be a full dual-SE duress verify (OPTIGA + SE050) or OPTIGA-only suffices given SE050's ~104 ms dominates — likely must pad BOTH to stay uniform; (3) FI structure for the branch select (which-credential-matched) so a single glitch can't force the decoy→real or real→decoy mis-route.
- [x] **P4 — wizard UI — DONE 2026-05-20.** `ui::seed_wizard::collect_duress_pin` — yes/no chooser → `enter_pin_with_confirm`, bounded to 3 attempts, confirmed-DISTINCT from the main PIN. Runs ONLY on the unprovisioned first-boot path; threads `Some(pin)` into `provision_from_mnemonic` (declined/exhausted → `None` → random decoy, always-provision preserved); duress PIN zeroized after provisioning. Dialog flow is interactive-only (`e2e-test` short-circuits `confirm`/`enter_pin`), validated by source-grep + an interactive harness `make play-hw-duress-ui` (`duress-ui-test` feature — mock-se, drives JUST the dialogs via the physical gpio-buttons; main PIN fixed to `12345678` so the distinct-check reject is exercisable). **Interactively validated on OLED + perfboard buttons 2026-05-20:** the `Set duress PIN?` chooser navigates/selects, a distinct PIN is accepted, `Wipe on duress?` works, `mode: WIPE/DECOY` renders, and the harness loops cleanly. **Manual-UI checklist to re-verify during HW bring-up / pre-ship (NOT covered by automated e2e — the dialogs are short-circuited there):** (a) entering the main PIN as the duress PIN holds `Same as main / pick another` ~1.8 s (readable, not a split-second flash); (b) a mismatched enter/confirm holds `PIN mismatch / try again` ~1.8 s; (c) declining at `Set duress PIN?` (or 3× distinct-fail) shows `Declined / random decoy`; (d) the wizard's duress dialog appears ONLY on first-boot, never on the already-provisioned unlock path. (a)-(c) reproducible via `make play-hw-duress-ui`; (d) needs the full first-boot wizard (`duress-pin` non-e2e build).
- [x] **P5 — wipe-on-duress option — DONE 2026-05-20 + silicon-validated.** `ui::seed_wizard::choose_duress_wipe_mode` → persisted as a 1-byte flag in secure flash page 125 QW2 (`hw::flash::arm_duress_wipe_mode`/`is_duress_wipe_mode`), blank=decoy (safe default), programmed=wipe; armed BEFORE provisioning so a crash can't downgrade wipe→decoy. `gated_unlock`: on a duress match in wipe mode → zeroize the decoy master, `factory_reset_admin` both chips, return `PinLocked` (NO `duress_pad`; NO `pin_attempts_reset` — the Err arm leaves page-124 correct). Decoy still always provisioned regardless of mode. Silicon-validated via `make duress-provision-hw` steps 20–22 (PASS): arm flag → `gated_unlock(duress)` returns `PinLocked` AND both chips wiped.

### 33. AI-aided Lean verification track (Rust ↔ SphincsCVerify)

**Status:** SCOPED 2026-06-10. Background assurance track — **does NOT gate the
October ship** (params + wire formats are frozen, so proofs landing later still
apply to shipped firmware). Full research report + tool comparison + risk list in
`docs/lean-verification-research-2026-06.md` (deep-research run, 24 sources,
claims adversarially verified). Decision: **Aeneas** (Charon → LLBC → Lean) for
extraction (NOT hax — see report §2), a Lean Squad-style scheduled AI-agent CI
loop for proof grinding (Claude primary, Aristotle API/MCP fallback), and an
adversarial spec-validation layer (`plausible` counterexamples + Facade-taxonomy
audits + Lean-spec-as-oracle differential fuzzing per verified-ledger).

**Goal theorems (in value order):**
1. *Firmware↔chain composite:* the bytes the firmware signs over parsed input
   are exactly the `userOpHash` the verified wallet model checks
   (`aa` extraction ∘ existing `Wallet/ValidateUserOp.lean`).
2. *Signer/verifier correspondence:* extracted `sphincs-c10` functions ≡ the
   existing `SphincsCVerify/Spec/{Adrs,Wots,Fors,...}.lean` the on-chain
   verifier was proven sound against (SHA-256 axiomatized as the SAME
   `Spec.sha256` symbol as bridge axiom A1 — composes through one shared axiom).
3. *Panic-freedom* on every attacker-facing parser (`proto`/`shared`/`aa` input
   paths) — near-free under Aeneas's `Result` monad.
4. Tier B (post-Oct): Merkle bundle verifiers (`tx`/`pqsigner-erc7730`),
   `fw-manifest` 75-B preimage chain, `domain` derivation determinism +
   invariant-#6 salt stability, `bip39` round-trip.

**Trust framing (MUST accompany any "verified" claim, internal or external):**
kernel-checked theorems modulo an *enumerated* TCB — SHA-256 axiom (KAT-backed),
Aeneas translation fidelity, rustc. Extends the existing `AXIOM_STATUS.json`
discipline to the extracted code: every axiom named + linted, zero
`assume`-style escapes, KAT per axiomatized primitive. Says nothing about
compiler/silicon/FI/SCA — those stay with `tools/sca` + silicon validation.

      - **DECISION 2026-06-13 (workflow `verity-assessment` + 2 adversarial
        reviews, both Keep):** split into two paths. **(i) Verity EDSL
        (write-in verified compilation) → DO NOT PURSUE.** Two independent
        dispositive reasons: it cannot ingest existing Solidity/Yul/bytecode
        (it only proves its own EDSL→Yul output), so it is the wrong tool
        class for an axiom (A3.1) that is ∀ over the *deployed* runtime; and
        even a hypothetical port is a different-bytecode, different-CREATE2-
        address contract barred from the shipped wallet by invariant #6 — plus
        v0.1.0's fragment (zero-bound loops only, word-keyed single-word
        memory, no calldata/sub-word/sha256-precompile primitives) can't
        express the 233-line hand-Yul verifier (~8–12 pw upstream Phase-0
        alone, verifier port unestimated). It also leaves A1/A2/A4 + solc
        Yul→bytecode in the TCB and keeps the functional "==Rust ref" leg an
        axiom witnessed by the same KAT harness. **(ii) verity-FRAMEWORK
        refinement (the upstream `c13_refines_spec` shape: hand-transcribed
        in-Lean EVM model + framework refinement, same Lean v4.22 as
        SphincsCVerify) → REMAINS the tracked A3.1-ceiling option above**, but
        scoped honestly: it proves ∀ over a hand-transcribed EVM *model* (so
        model↔deployed-bytecode is a transcription-TCB) and still axiomatizes
        SHA-256. The complementary option that proves the *actual* deployed
        bytecode with SHA-256 INTERPRETED is **Kontrol/KEVM** (tractability at
        C10 depth ~322 hashes/verify unproven; best as a 1–3 week spike on one
        sub-routine, e.g. WOTS+C chaining, for methodological diversity). Both
        (ii)/Kontrol are DEFERRED behind the S-1/S-2/S-3 OPTIGA ship-blockers
        and are NOT primary-assurance commitments; status-quo A3.1 (KAT 10/10
        + ~250-mutant screen + Rust↔Solidity↔Lean differential) is the honest,
        exploit-free ceiling. No code/redeploy implied.

## 🛑 CRITICAL OPEN — EUF-CMA axiom inconsistency (theft_free vacuous)

- [ ] **P0 — restate `EUF_CMA_SPHINCSplusC` to a consistent shape; re-derive `theft_free`** (found 2026-06-14, `docs/EUF_CMA_INCONSISTENCY.md`). A deep adversarial audit + my own kernel-checked probe (no `sorryAx`) derive `False` from the `lean/` crypto axioms: the axiom `∀ vk transcript m s, isForgery → False` is inconsistent because a genuine valid KAT sig at the empty transcript satisfies `isForgery` (verify accepts valid sigs post-`5055d66`). `theft_free`/`theft_free_bytecode`/`cannot_forge_without_breaking_SHA256`/`theft_free_with_calldata_binding` therefore prove NOTHING until fixed. Root cause: the A3.1 fix (made verify accept valid sigs) broke A5 (mutually exclusive for an executable verifier). Fix direction under design+adversarial-test (workflow `eufcma-reconcile`): game/probability (needs mathlib PMF), reduction, abstract bounded-adversary, or demote-to-explicit-hypothesis — SPHINCS+ EUF-CMA stays cited-TCB regardless; the defect is the self-contradictory SHAPE. Steps: (1) restate A5 consistently + re-derive theft_free; (2) commit a regression probe that the old `boom : False` no longer type-checks; (3) restate `sha256_injective_on_fixed_length` as collision-resistance (false-but-latent; detonates if mathlib added); (4) reinstate claims in THE_CLAIM.md / AXIOM_STATUS.json. DONE 2026-06-14: honest downgrade landed (THE_CLAIM banner, AXIOM_STATUS critical_alert + A5 entries, this item, EUF_CMA_INCONSISTENCY.md). Component proofs (wallet-model invariants, §33 extracted, Halmos A3.*, A3.1 KAT) are NOT in the crypto closure and remain valid. NOT an on-chain vulnerability.

| 2026-06-13 | R-derivation hardening — secret-keyed, message-bound R (chosen-message FORS-saturation defense, TRNG-failure-robust) | `fors::grind_r` now derives `R = sha256(sk_seed ‖ "R_grind" ‖ [opt_rand] ‖ message ‖ nonce)[0..16]` (was `sha256("R_grind" ‖ [opt_rand] ‖ nonce)` — no secret, no message). Binding `sk_seed` + `message` makes `ht_idx = bits[143..161] of h_msg(pk_seed,pk_root,R,message)` unpredictable to anyone without the secret key for any chosen message, closing the chosen-message FORS-saturation avenue (upstream SPHINCS- SECURITY-ANALYSIS.md §2 "Avenue B") EVEN under a biased/predictable TRNG — production was previously protected only by mandatory fresh opt_rand. Mirrors FIPS 205 `PRF_msg` keying R on a secret. `opt_rand` retained as defense-in-depth (F-9 iteration-count leak + Genêt). **Signer-side only**: the frozen on-chain Yul verifier reads R from the signature and is unaffected; deployed contracts unchanged. Threaded `sk_seed` through `grind_r` (only caller `hypertree::sign_inner` has it). Updated the Lean signer model `Spec/Signer.lean::grindR` to match (faithfulness; `verify_signs` is parametric on the consistency hypothesis so unaffected) and lifted `secure/src/crypto.rs`'s opt_rand justification to cite the cryptographic argument, not just SCA/F-9. **Validation** (all green): sphincs-c10 56/56 (sign→verify roundtrip with new R); regenerated c10_test_vectors.json + bulk_vectors.json (new R, each self-verified under the unchanged Yul-matching `verify`); re-embedded KatVectors.lean + BulkVectors.lean; Lean `make verify` 10/10 (digest+htIdx+full) and `make verify-bulk` 384/384; secure host suite 2030 passed / 1 pre-existing unrelated usb_hw lint; Lean build 0 sorry. **forge NOT run** (absent in dev env) — but the Solidity KAT tests only assert `verify()==expectValid` (no exact-byte pinning), the verifier is unchanged, and every regenerated vector self-verifies in Rust + passes the Lean differential,  so forge CI will pass. This is exactly why layer 0 verified perfectly and only layer 1's last merkle step was corrupted. Ground-truthed against Rust (`pk_from_sig`/`verify_auth_path` via temp `sim_internals` export, since reverted): wp0/root0/wp1 already matched; only layer-1 final root diverged → pinned to auth[8]. | 2026-06-12 | SCP03 counter desync on bare-error responses (silicon-found, wizard-bricking) FIXED — and first full LCD-standalone first-boot wizard E2E | Symptom: `flash-hw-dual-se-lcd-standalone-debug` first-boot wizard looped forever at PIN entry — `rng_strong::fill FAILED` on every NewWallet attempt. **Two stacked causes.** (1) *OPTIGA leg:* the chip's PRL was wedged in the SCTR=0x40 alert state from an earlier failed handshake in the same powered session (this build's `dev-testkey` PBS ≠ the chip's DHUK pairing from 2026-05-12; **probe-rs flash/reset never power-cycles the SEs**, so the wedge survives reflashes) — every plaintext APDU bounced with the 7-byte `08 40` alert frame. `Optiga::random` is the one OPTIGA op with no `ensure_shield`, so wizard entropy went plaintext into the wedge; the bounce also made the still-provisioned OPTIGA read as blank (counter probes → None → "fresh chip"). Cleared by USB power-cycle; provisioning then re-paired E140 to the dev-testkey PBS via `setup_pbs_no_handshake` (LcsO=Creation). (2) *SE050 leg (the code fix):* `scp03::unwrap_response` did **not** advance the encryption counter on bare-error-SW responses. On the wiped chip the FIRST secured command (reconcile's ReadObjectAttributes on the deleted USERID) legitimately returned bare `0x6985` → host counter froze one behind the card → next command's C-ENC ICV was garbage to the card → `0x6982` (card terminates session) → every later APDU `0x6985` → wizard rng dead. **This empirically refutes the 2026-05-28 row's "counter-desync alarm was a false positive" dismissal**: silicon shows the card consumes the counter when its SM layer processes a command, regardless of applet outcome (GP Amd D §6.2.6 counts per command sent). Fix: `session.inc_counter()` in the bare-SW arm of `unwrap_response` + corrected doc comment (`secure/src/se050/scp03.rs`). Why no e2e ever caught it: provisioned-chip flows never error mid-session; the wiped-chip wizard is the natural trigger. **Validation on real B-U585I + NV3007 LCD:** full first-boot wizard E2E — 3-source entropy OK (STM32⊕OPTIGA⊕SE050), 24-word display + spot-check, `[DUAL] Provisioned` (E120 found already-provisioned → idempotent skip), `[DUAL] Unlocked: entropy reconstructed from XOR split`, NS boot + USB polling. SCP03 session survived dozens of secured commands **with interleaved benign applet errors** (the exact desync trigger) — also the first end-to-end functional silicon validation of the S-5 `P1=0x33` channel (R-ENC responses decrypted throughout); the S-5 LA capture stays open as a separate item. Host suite 2030 passed / 1 pre-existing `usb_hw.rs` failure. **Follow-ups (not landed):** `Optiga::random` should `ensure_shield` once paired (CRIT-8 gap — chip-TRNG bytes plaintext on I²C when shield down; needs the virgin-chip pair-then-draw ordering decision); generalize the 2026-05-12 `factory_reset` fix — drop `optiga.ready` on any PRL alert so the next op re-inits the chip out of the wedge; wizard should surface an actionable diagnostic on rng failure instead of silently looping to PIN entry (`pin_entry.rs:75` `let _` + generic "RNG failed"). |
| 2026-06-12 | ZK byte-pack commitment non-binding — proof-forgery fix (CRITICAL) across all 3 clear-sign circuits | Soundness audit of the circom clear-signing stack found the byte→field commitment `H_tx = Poseidon(pack31(bytes))` is **not byte-binding**: `PackBytes31` folded `acc <== acc*256 + byte` with **no `Num2Bits(8)` range check**, while the firmware computes the same hash over a genuine `[u8]`. Because byte signals were unconstrained field elements (and `256` is invertible mod r), the packing is non-injective — a malicious companion could present an in-circuit `canonical` of out-of-range "bytes" that pack to the **same** field elements as a *different, real* on-chain order, so Poseidon matches and the proof verifies while the device signs an order it never displayed (e.g. show "SELL 0.20 USDC", sign "SELL all WBTC" — `receiver`/`fee`/`validTo`/`appData` are bound ONLY via `H_tx` and were the forgery scratch). Full analysis + block-by-block constructibility (only block B3 couples; trivially met) in `docs/VULN-cowswap-zk-bytepack-nonbinding.md`. **Distinct from** the 2026-06-10 amount-overflow VULN (that range-checked the amount *value*; this is upstream of all decoding). **Fix:** `Num2Bits(8)` per input byte BEFORE the fold in all three byte-commitment packers — `circuits/lib/poseidon_bytes.circom::PackBytes31` (covers `cowswap_eip712_order`, i.e. both direct CoW **and** the Safe-wrapped presign path, which share the v3 VK), `circuits/cowswap/set_pre_signature::CsPackBytes31`, `circuits/aave_v3/clear_signing_proof::PackBytes31`. Every calldata/canonical/readable byte flows through one of these, so the check transitively covers all downstream decoders (registry pack, `Uint256BytesToField`, ABI slots). **Regen:** all 3 circuits recompiled (circom 2.2.3; constraints 21027/4937/9049, fit pot16/pot14/pot14), seed-pinned `groth16 setup`+`contribute` (added `circuits/aave_v3/contribution.seed`), new `circuit_final.zkey` + `.vk.bin` ×3; `cargo run -p dbgen` → new `VK_DB_ROOT=1380fc86…` + `nonsecure/src/vk_db.bin` + `vks.review.txt`; `vk_bin_to_rust.js` → `secure/src/zk/vk_data.rs`; aave `TEST_PROOF_A/B/C` regenerated against new zkey (`TEST_H_TX`/`TEST_H_STR` unchanged — same in-range bytes). **Validation:** `cargo run -p zk-test` PASS (firmware Groth16 verifier accepts the new aave proof+VK, both pairing paths); 59 `zk_under_test::pure_tests` PASS incl. `positive_verify_clear_signing_proof_known_good` + the unchanged-hash asserts; regenerated v3 CoW order proof verifies against new VK with `H_root == ERC20_POSEIDON_ROOT`; **negative proof the forgery is dead** — witness gen with `canonical[i]=256` now fails `Num2Bits_0` assert while `=255` succeeds; full secure host suite 2030/2030 (the 1 fail is the pre-existing unrelated `usb_hw.rs` raw-volatile lint, untouched here). Pre-deployment (no devices, no on-chain funds) so VKs overwritten freely. Files: `circuits/lib/poseidon_bytes.circom`, `circuits/cowswap/set_pre_signature/circuit.circom`, `circuits/aave_v3/clear_signing_proof.circom`, `circuits/{aave_v3,cowswap/eip712_order,cowswap/set_pre_signature}/circuit_final.zkey`, `circuits/aave_v3/contribution.seed`, `secure/data/vks/*.vk.bin`, `secure/src/db_roots.rs`, `secure/src/zk/{vk_data.rs,test_vectors.rs}`, `nonsecure/src/vk_db.bin`, `secure/data/vks.review.txt`, `docs/VULN-cowswap-zk-bytepack-nonbinding.md`. |

| 2026-06-11 | Off-chain `raw32` UserOp-forgery fix (CRITICAL) + EIP-712 encoded-data exact-length (MEDIUM) | Audit of the EIP-1271/ERC-6492 off-chain path found `CMD_SIGN_OFFCHAIN` kind=RAW32 **bare-signed** the companion's 32-byte payload with the slot key. The on-chain Type-2 path (`PQSmartWallet._validateSignature`) verifies a bare slot C10 sig over `sphincsDigest(userOp)` (plain SHA-256 of UserOp fields = firmware's `compute_sphincs_digest_v06`), so `raw32(sphincsDigest(drainOp))` was a valid UserOp signature → **full wallet drain behind a blind "! Raw 32-byte" page**, bypassing the trusted display (contradicted threat-model.md:172/:257). **Fix:** the firmware now performs the Solady replay-safe EIP-712 nesting itself for every off-chain kind. Added `aa::eip1271::replay_safe_hash(chain,wallet,H)` = `keccak(0x1901‖domainSep‖keccak(PERSONAL_SIGN_TYPEHASH‖H))` (no inner EIP-191 prefix; matches `ERC1271.sol:240-290`); refactored `personal_sign_replay_safe_hash` to `replay_safe_hash(prefixed(msg))` (kind=1 byte-identical). kind=RAW32 now sends the dapp's RAW hash H (companion no longer pre-nests) and the firmware nests it; kind=EIP712_TYPED switched from `personal_sign_replay_safe_hash(final_eip712)` (spurious double-prefix → would fail on-chain verify) to `replay_safe_hash(final_eip712)`. raw32 display/fingerprint now show H, not the internal nested hash. No contract change (on-chain `isValidSignature(H)` already nests the same way). Also MEDIUM defense-in-depth: EIP-712 render now requires `encoded_data.len() == static_head_words*32` exactly (was `>=`), so a descriptor that under-declares its member count can't hide signed-but-unshown trailing words. Tests: `aa` unit (`replay_safe_hash_matches_solady_nesting`, `personal_sign_is_replay_safe_of_prefixed`, `replay_safe_hash_never_returns_input`) + new `aa/tests/offchain_raw32_forgery.rs` (sig over a real `sphincsDigest` is never produced bare; domain-bound). `cargo test -p pqsigner-aa` green (51); secure host suite 2013/2013 (1 pre-existing unrelated usb_hw raw-volatile lint fail); firmware target `cargo check` clean. Files: `aa/src/eip1271.rs`, `secure/src/nsc/cmd_sign_offchain.rs`, `secure/src/tx/display/erc7730/mod.rs`, `proto/src/lib.rs`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/companion-app-integration.md`, `aa/tests/offchain_raw32_forgery.rs`. |

| 2026-06-10 | Firmware-signing audit (`docs/security-audit-2026-06-firmware-signing.md`) — all 12 findings fixed | Software-logic WYSIWYS audit remediated end-to-end. **C-1/H-2/M-8 (the critical):** one dispatcher-level invariant — `pick_sign_pages` (`secure/src/tx/display/mod.rs`) now calls `enforce_native_value_page`, splicing a loud `! NATIVE ETH` page (`Pages::insert_blank`) right after the banner whenever the signed outer `value != 0`, for EVERY `TxKind` (single + batch). Closes the `erc20_unknown` direct-theft hole + the ERC-7730/Safe value-omission siblings in one place a future renderer can't forget; per-TxKind regression tests added. **H-3:** field-completeness enforced HOST-SIDE in `dbgen::check_contract_field_completeness` (every contract-call arg must be a rendered/`never` field or a `tokenPath`, else build error) — where ABI arity is known and the seed corpus's deliberate `never`/`tokenPath` coverage passes; the audit's "forbid `Never` on-device" would have broken the corpus. **M-4:** unbound `tokenAmount` renders raw int + `! raw, dec=?` (no assumed 18-dec scale). **M-5:** EIP-712 format entries now carry the full 32-byte primary-type hash (gated on EIP-712 ctx; `FormatHeader::type_hash` + `FormatIter`), bound constant-time in the typed renderer instead of a 4-byte prefix — `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` regenerated (`8e8885fb…`). **M-6:** ETH amounts render fixed-width (no trailing-zero trim), matching the token anti-spoof policy. **M-7:** `Enum`/`NftName` stubs `Reject`→blind-sign. **L-9:** Groth16 verifier rejects identity points pre-loop; dead `any_identity` removed from `miller_loop_4` (a blanket mask would false-ACCEPT a 4-pairing product). **L-10:** single-tx Safe binds + Groth16 verdict FI-sentinel-hardened (batch parity). **L-11:** dropped the tautological `cross_check_eip712` contract arg (domain_separator subsumes it). **L-12:** corrected the `CLAUDE.md` multiSend claim. Validation: host suites pass (`pqsigner-erc7730`/`dbgen`/`bls12_381_pka`/secure — incl. new C-1 regression + M-5 corpus round-trip); firmware compiles clean for thumbv8m. The pre-existing `make e2e` Scenario-2 failure reproduces identically on the unmodified commit (stale firmware `PROXY_INIT_CODE_HASH` after the §bootstrap-cap `PQSmartWallet.sol` change) — unrelated to these fixes. |
| 2026-06-10 | F-29 — `check_true_into_sentinel` primitive hardened branchless (verdict-flip closed) + full FI-matrix regression re-run | The F-28 pass-3 trace showed the last SCP03 residual was *inside* the shared `pqsigner_fi::check_true_into_sentinel`: its `if cond { OK } else { FAIL }` verdict select compiled to a branch a single skip flips FAIL→OK. **Measured the blast radius first** (per the agreed measure-then-fix plan): re-ran `fi`/`c10`/`pin`/`fw-verify`/`ns-ptr` and a direct `sca_c10_verify_release(0)` probe — confirmed the bare single-check shape flips (incl. one *inside* the helper), but EVERY production gate is robust *as composed* (NS-ptr + FW-verify use two independent sentinel checks → `bypassed=0`; C10-sign needs faulted signing for `v=false`; PIN leans on SE-silicon `result`; SCP03 was the lone exploitable single-check, already fixed by F-28's infective mask). **Then fixed the primitive** anyway (defense-in-depth, to retire the "accept F-2" reliance for future gates): new branchless `select_sentinel` (`(m1 & m2 & OK) | ((!m1 | !m2) & FAIL)`, two independently `black_box`'d masks) in `check_true`/`check_true_into_sentinel`. **Verified:** inside-helper flip on `sca_c10_verify_release(0)` gone (5→4, 0 inside); **full FI matrix re-ran ZERO regressions** (`fi`/`c10`/`pin`/`ns-ptr`/`fw-verify`/`c10v`/`scp03-fi` all exit 0); 38 `pqsigner-fi` host tests pass. Remaining 4 flips are the gate's call-site glue (the documented-accepted **F-2** class — the primitive can't fix its own call site). README §Findings F-29. Low-pri follow-ups filed: auto-classify the `make c10` oracle by symbol; add `pqsigner-fi` to the `tools/sca` Makefile target deps. |
| 2026-06-10 | F-28 — SCP03 R-MAC verify gate FI-hardened (single-fault `half_E` release closed) + F-28-spawned F-29 filed | The RANK 1 SCP03 FI harness, run **exhaustively** (stride-1, 60k positions × 3 models — a strided smoke had falsely shown "no bypass"), found `[skip]` faults in `unwrap_response` that released the attacker's `half_E` from a forged (wrong-R-MAC) response. Fixed in `secure/src/se050/scp03.rs` (+ kept-in-sync `scp03_target`) in 3 passes: (1) `check_true_into_sentinel` double-CMAC + `black_box` gate (killed all stuck-at + CMAC/compare skips; 3 skip residuals); (2) redundant `read_volatile_voted` gate (3→1); (3) the survivor traced INSIDE `check_true_into_sentinel`'s own verdict branch → closed with an **infective release mask** (fold a fresh INDEPENDENT R-MAC recompute branchlessly into the output — `release_mask=(mac_matches as u8).wrapping_sub(1)`, `out[i]^=mask` — so a forged response that slips the early gate is XOR-garbled, never the real `half_E`, with no branch to skip). **`make scp03-fi` now exits 0** (GENUINE-gate-bypass=0 across all 3 models; baselines hold; compile-checked in a full `dual-se,stm32u585,…,usb` config). The pass-3 trace surfaced **F-29** (NEW, open): the shared `pqsigner_fi::check_true_into_sentinel` verdict-selection `if/else` is itself single-skip-defeatable (skip flips FAIL→OK) — affects any single-sentinel secret-release gate; C10/PIN survive only via a *second* independent check; fix = branchless verdict selection in `pqsigner-fi` + re-run all FI sweeps. README §Findings F-28/F-29. |
| 2026-06-10 | §18b FORS-forgery detection-gap closed (①②③, ④ partial) | The regression coverage `fcee705a` never shipped for the shared-FORS-forest forgery (CWE-347). **①** `sphincs-c10/tests/fors_forgery_resistance.rs` — end-to-end reassembly forgery simulator (+ a `sim-internals` feature exposing `h_msg`/`extract_*`, the passive observer's PUBLIC-input capability). Fast CI guards: parse/assemble byte-roundtrip + cross-position tree splice rejection. **②** `tests/fors_position_binding.rs` (5 tests, 0.03 s) — `fors_secret`/`fors_pk`/tree-root unique per `ht_idx`, + FORS-ADRS-carries-`ht_idx` structural assert. **③** `docs/sphincs-c10-spec-conformance-checklist.md` — reusable new-crypto-primitive checklist (code-review gate) + the 2026-06-08 review: CONFORMANT (all 9 Rust FORS ADRS sites + 4 Yul words carry `ht_idx`, PRF folds it, `pk_root`/wire unchanged). **④ partial** — Lean spec (`Spec/{Fors,Adrs}.lean` + `Verifier/Refined.lean`) resynced to bind `ht_idx`, mirroring the Yul; UNVERIFIED (Lean/Halmos/Certora/`forge` all absent) — kernel proofs + Halmos discharge against codehash `0xf1ef…` remain PENDING, recorded in `AXIOM_STATUS.json` A3.1. |
| 2026-06-10 | §18b SCA sweeps for the 72-commit-pull new secret paths (RANK 1/2/3) | New harnesses for secret-touching surfaces that no `tools/sca` harness covered, surfaced by the 2026-06-02 C10-sweep pre-flight. **RANK 1 — SE050 SCP03 response-unwrap** (`scp03_target/` `#[path]`-includes real `scp03_logic.rs`; `unwrap_response` is a kept-in-sync copy): (a) `fault_sweep_scp03.py` (`make scp03-fi`) — forged-response R-MAC gate; **exhaustive stride-1 run surfaced FINDING F-28 (the R-MAC check is single-fault-defeatable → forged response accepted, half_E released; new hardening task added)** — an earlier strided smoke run had falsely shown "no bypass"; (b) `leakage_scp03.py` (`make scp03-leakage`) — R-ENC decrypt + R-MAC CMAC TVLA, all 4 modes flat on `mem_address`, positive control leaks max\|t\|≈18 (pipeline live). **RANK 2 — `rng_strong` strict fail-closed** (`rng_strong_target/` `#[path]`-includes the real fold, stubs the sources): `fault_sweep_rng_strong.py` (`make rng-strong`, exit 0) — no single fault yields WEAK entropy from a healthy system (XOR multi-source resilience + all-zero gate hold; bypasses are the F-2/F-4 call-glue residual class). **RANK 3 — OPTIGA `verify_and_lock` LcsO-ratchet gate** (`optiga_lock_target/`, pure metadata helpers copied verbatim): `fault_sweep_optiga_lock.py` (`make optiga-lock`, exit 0) — gate IS single-fault-defeatable (~12–14/model fire the irreversible ratchet on unverified AC) but it's provisioning-time/run-once in the trusted PQ1 factory guarding the OPTIGA silent-AC-reject quirk → a yield/reliability event, not a field bypass. | 2026-06-09 | F-24 stage E Phase 1 (decoy frames) — bench-tested on the LCD @ 20 MHz, DEFENSE FAILS (repaint-limited) | New `make decoy-flicker-lcd-hw` + `decoy_flicker_test_loop` now sweeps `DECOY_HOLD` = 40/25/15/8/3/0 ms (logged). On the dev board at the production ÷8 = 20 MHz the decoy words are VISIBLE (switch) at every hold incl. DECOY_HOLD=0 — the defense fails. Root cause ≠ OLED (bistable pixels): here it's **repaint speed** — a full seed-page SPI blit is ~20 ms, above the eye's ~10–13 ms content flicker-fusion threshold, so each decoy frame reads as a distinct content change regardless of hold. The LCD's slow pixel response (~35 ms) would help only if the repaint were fast enough to exploit it. Lever = faster repaint (~40 MHz ≈ 11 ms + DMA / word-rows-only), but 40 MHz on this dev board has the LED-on-SCK flicker → the clean decisive test is on the EVT board. `decoy-frames` stays OFF (not viable at 20 MHz). Mirrors the 2026-05-19 OLED negative result (same symptom — readable decoys — different cause). Harness committed for EVT re-test. |

Remaining: Phase D (re-validate decoy frames on the LCD's slow pixels) + the EVT-board 40 MHz re-up. |

| 2026-06-09 | NV3007 LCD §28 Phase C — Display backend landed + hardware-validated | New `secure/src/ui/lcd.rs`: `ui-lcd` is now a standalone 4th `Display` backend (`build.rs` + `nsc/mod.rs` fence + `ui/mod.rs` + `Cargo.toml` de-paired from `ui-noop`, which is now a build error). Renders the SAME 16×4 char grid as the OLED, so `confirm`/`pin_entry`/`seed_wizard`/`measured_boot` port with ZERO edits. `FONT_5X8` upscaled 3× → 15×24 glyphs, landscape (software rotation onto the proven native coords — flip-X resolved on bench), white-on-black, centered. Per-glyph 720 B cell buffer + `lcd_nv3007::set_window`/`write_pixels` (no full framebuffer — 121 KB won't fit). `flush_with_secret_rows` keeps the seed **constant-time**: routes secret rows through `secret_text::secret_glyph_cols` (the 96-entry scan + `black_box` barriers) + a shared branchless RGB565 expansion that writes every pixel (FG/BG via mask) — the original plan's "CT comes for free" was wrong, the rasterization IS the leaky surface, so the CT scanner is reused explicitly. `lcd_nv3007::init()` switched to the SWRESET path (RES tied to 3V3). Flicker eliminated by dropping the per-flush full-screen clear; SPI dropped ÷4→÷8 (40→20 MHz) after tracing residual flicker to the dev board's blue LED (LD2) loading PE13=SCK — re-up to 40 MHz tracked for the EVT board. Fixed the `spi_hw` cfg1 source-pin test I'd missed in the Phase B commit (now `..._baud_gated_...`, pins both gated MBR values). Host suite back to baseline (2000 pass + 1 documented pre-existing `usb_hw` fail). STILL OPEN before flipping production to `ui-lcd`: bench the real dialogs via full boot + the `tools/sca` F-24 CT gate on the seed path; then OLED demote (keep `oled.rs` as CT reference). | Panel had been black for days; root cause found via an RM0456 audit workflow (find→verify, advisor-vetted): `spi_end` cleared `SPE` on `TXP` (FIFO-space) instead of `EOT` (shifted-out), so per RM0456 §68.4.12 it **flushed/truncated every SPI command** — single-byte init commands never reached the panel. Secondary bug: `write_cmd`/`write_data` used one CS window per byte, but NV3007 resets the param index on CS-high, dropping multi-param values. **Fixes** (`secure/src/hw/lcd_nv3007.rs`): `spi_begin(tsize)` sets TSIZE>0 with SPE=0 then waits `EOT` before clearing SPE, chunked ≤65534 B (16-bit TSIZE) with a per-chunk ES0499 `delay(16)` (asymmetric-last-SCK erratum); new `write_cmd_data(cmd,&[params])` keeps a command + all its params in ONE CS-low window. With those, the full byte-for-byte `dgen1` init renders a clean, uniform image edge-to-edge. **Refresh** (`spi_hw.rs`): SPI raised ÷32→÷4 (40 MHz, `ui-lcd`-gated; non-LCD/shared-TROPIC01 builds keep ÷32) — DWT-measured **24.3 ms = 41 fps** full-frame (bus-saturated; 60 fps would need ~58 MHz which violates the NV3007 10 ns setup/hold, and ÷2/80 MHz starves the polled FIFO). New `fill_rect` **partial update = 0.65 ms** for 40×40 (~1500 fps-equiv, 38× faster) — the foundation for Phase C. Flicker eliminated via draw-over (never clear-then-redraw; update only changed slivers). **No hardware vsync**: the EastRising module's 8-pin header (GND·VCC·SCK·MOS·RES·DC·CS·BLK) doesn't break out the NV3007 TE pin, so moving content tears slightly but static wallet screens never do — irrelevant in practice. RES tied to 3V3 (both GPIO candidates loaded) + software SWRESET. Validated on real B-U585I via `make lcd-test-hw`. New: `docs/nv3007-wiring.md`, `docs/nv3007/` datasheets, `lcd-test` feature, `make lcd-test-hw`. Next: Phase C (`ui::Display` over LCD + OLED retirement). |
| 2026-06-02 | Desk-doable security fixes (TAMP base / SCP03 fail-open / half_E write-once) | Three of the four 2026-05-29-survey desk items, advisor-vetted + adversarial-review-verified (find→verify→completeness-critic workflow, 20 agents). **TAMP:** `tamp.rs` had **four** wrong backup-domain addresses (never silicon-validated, log-only) — TAMP base `0x5600_4400→7C00`, RTCAPBEN moved AHB3ENR→APB3ENR@0xA8 (PWREN kept in AHB3ENR), PWR_DBPR `0x10→0x28`, PWR_BDCR1 `0x18→0x20`; all re-derived against RM0456 + silicon-validated `bhk.rs`; source-pin test corrected (it had been *re-asserting* the wrong base) + new offset-pin test. **SCP03 fail-open:** factory-key fallback gated behind new `se050-scp03-allow-factory-fallback` (runtime build fails CLOSED); `se050-rotate-scp03` implies it; **added to the `nsc/mod.rs` hardware-release `compile_error!` fence** after the review found the original "CI gates it off" claim was false (no firmware CI gate) — validated the fence fires on a release+fallback build + exempts the provisioning build. **half_E write-once:** new `write_binary_write_once` (drops `ALLOW_WRITE`) on `ENTROPY/VK/BOOTSTRAP_VK` + duress; shared core leaves 6 test/canary callers unchanged; **VALIDATED ON SILICON** via the new `audit_write_once_enforced` stress test (1 PASS) — create-without-`ALLOW_WRITE` succeeds (no brick), A/B control isolates the gate (control update 0x9000 vs write-once 0x6986), share survives across reinit. **Fix 4 (OPTIGA AC-builder) deferred** at the time — note the teammate's 2026-05-29 row below now closes S-1/S-2/S-3 with its own AC-builder. Build matrix green across `dual-se,tamp` / `se050-derived-scp03` / `se050-rotate-scp03` / `duress-pin` / `se050-stress`; host suite 1993/1995 (the 1 remaining is the pre-existing `usb_hw.rs` read_volatile count). |
| 2026-05-29 | S-1/S-2/S-3 OPTIGA hardening pass (code-complete, pending silicon) | Closes the OPTIGA bench-attack triad in firmware. **Shared brick-safety gate:** new `apdu::metadata_matches_expected` (tag-wise Change/Read/Execute/DataType compare, tolerant of chip re-ordering + appended tags) + `OptigaTrustM::verify_and_lock` — reads back the stored metadata and refuses the irreversible `LcsO=Operational` lock (returns `Status(0xEB)`) unless the intended AC bytes are confirmed on-chip; every `lock_oid` call site (`provision_auth_ref`, `provision_user_oid`, `provision_counter`) now routes through it; no-op delegate to `lock_oid` when `optiga-lock-operational` off, so dev iteration is unchanged. **S-1:** `build_metadata_auth_ref_luc_oid` gains a `change_is_auto` param — the real F1D0 takes `Change=Auto(F1D0)` (`D0 03 23 F1 D0`) under `optiga-lock-operational` (byte-frozen test `positive_build_metadata_auth_ref_luc_change_auto_exact_bytes` = `20 10 D0 03 23 F1 D0 D1 01 FF D3 03 40 E1 20 E8 01 31`), dev + duress-F1D8 twin stay `ALW`; `provision_auth_ref` skip-guard strengthened to require `LUC(E120) && Auto(F1D0)` on an already-locked F1D0 (else fail-closed). `Conf(E140)` deliberately rejected (cracked-PBS bypass). **S-2:** new `build_metadata_ta_junk` (Change/Read/Execute=NEV, no DataType=TrustAnchor) + `lockdown_ta_pool` (gated `optiga-lock-operational` + `factory-production-irreversible-im-sure`) junk-overwrites + verify-locks `0xE0E3..0xE0E8` as the last `store_objects` step → no on-chip anchor for a SetObjectProtected manifest; SetObjectProtected senders + `CMD 0x83`/tags/version/fragment consts now `#[cfg(feature="optiga-reset-oids")]` so the encoder is absent from production binaries. **S-3:** two `compile_error!` fences in `nsc/mod.rs` — production OPTIGA (`mode-production` or `stm32u585 + !debug_assertions`, minus `e2e-test`/`dev-testkey`) without `optiga-hw-counter` → error; `optiga-reset-oids` in a production build → error (both verified to fire). **factory_reset redesign:** under the locked profile, drop the F1D0 blank-write (would fail — Auto(F1D0), no PIN auth) and skip `reset_e120_via_transient_auth` (needs `Change=ALW`); seed still erased via the F1D1/F1D2 `Conf(E140)` arm; F1E1 stays the boot-readable provisioned sentinel; crash-safe sentinel-first + wipe-flag preserved. **Reset model:** forgotten-PIN lockout destroys the seed; re-provisioning bumps the OPTIGA OID range (SE050-symmetric) — bump-slot wiring deferred pending the on-silicon F1Dx-as-TrustAnchor promotability probe. **Tests:** new pure tests pass (4 always-on + 1 hw-counter-gated byte-exact); full host suite green except the 1 pre-existing unrelated `usb_hw.rs` failure; locked + dev + duress profiles all `cargo check` clean for thumbv8m. **Still open (on ONE sacrificial chip — see plan):** TA-promotability probe (resolves slot plan), provision→verify_and_lock→unlock→lockout→seed-erased→re-provision, and the S-2 acceptance test (SetObjectProtected signed by the Infineon sample key MUST be rejected post-lockdown). Irreversible silicon burns are gated behind `optiga-lock-operational` + `factory-production-irreversible-im-sure` and run only in the factory ceremony. |
| 2026-05-28 | S-5/S-6/S-7 SE050 hardening pass | Comprehensive SE050 driver fix with full GP SCP03 Amd D v1.1.2 + NXP AN12413 + UM11225 + SE050 datasheet + NXP plug-and-trust C reference in hand. **S-5:** SCP03 `P1=0x33` (full C-MAC + C-DEC + R-MAC + R-ENC); new `scp03::unwrap_response` verifies R-MAC over `MCV(16) || ciphered_body || SW(2)` under S-RMAC, AES-CBC-decrypts with `response_icv = AES-ECB(S-ENC, counter_block[0]=0x80)` per §6.2.7, ISO 7816-4 depad, propagates plaintext+SW; counter advance moved to unwrap success path; CT compare via `subtle`; `aes128_cbc_decrypt` round-trip KAT added to `scp03_logic::tests`. **S-6:** `store_objects` + `store_duress_objects` pass `None` for user UserID's admin-delete entry (substitution attack closed); data objects keep admin entry (DoS-wipe preserved); `admin_factory_reset` split DATA_AND_CANARY_OBJS (must-wipe) vs AUTH_OBJS_BEST_EFFORT (survival OK); canary selftest mirrors production shape with PIN-self-delete cleanup. Trade-off: chip is single-use post-lockout (USERID_OBJ orphaned; OID range bump required to re-provision). **S-7a:** `write_userid` rejects `max_attempts ∈ {0, ≥256}` with `InvalidParam`; new `write_userid_unlimited` for the 7 legitimate unlimited call sites (admin / duress / E2E test admins) explicitly omits TAG_MAX_ATTEMPTS. **S-7b:** `delete_object` no longer maps `SW=0x6986 → Ok` (was masking real policy-denial); `0x6985 → Ok` stays for idempotent-delete. **S-7c:** extended-Lc commands emit 2-byte Le per ISO 7816-4 Case 4E. **S-7d (doc):** status-code mappings cross-referenced; AuthMethodBlocked correctly mapped to documented `COMMAND_NOT_ALLOWED = 0x6986`. **Audit sweep:** dispatched 3 PDF/C-reference research agents + 1 cross-reference audit; found 1 real CT-strengthening item (use `subtle::ConstantTimeEq` instead of hand-rolled) — applied. Counter-desync alarm from audit was a false positive (NXP's `nxScp03_Com.c:236-244` increments counter only on `status = SCP_OK`, same as my code — both host and card stay at value N on bare-error, so they're in sync). **Tests:** 1991/1992 pass (1 pre-existing failure in unrelated `usb_hw.rs`); 119 SE050 pure tests all pass; 36 `scp03_logic` host-side tests including 3 new CBC decrypt KATs all pass. **Still open:** logic-analyzer silicon verification (S-5), sacrificial-part admin-delete-of-USERID confirms SW=0x6986 (S-6), iterative_wipe SW probe (S-7d). | 2026-04-16 | all-C10 slot cutover (phases 1-7) | Collapsed the multi-signer architecture to a single the legacy FORS+C signing path behind one `CMD_SIGN_USEROP`. Flash-backed `SlotState` persists `next_q` across power cycles (phase 1). Added SPHINCS+C11 master key derivation matching the SPHINCs- reference byte-for-byte (phase 2, 7 unit tests + BIP-39 vector). Unified Type 1 / Type 2 state machine emits a `[type1_len \| t1 \| type2_len \| t2]` bundle the companion submits as up to two EntryPoint v0.9 UserOps (phase 3). USB layer cut from 14 INS codes + v1 Keycard Shell compat to 7 native v2 instructions; webhid tool rewritten for the unified bundle (phase 4). `PQSmartWallet` + `PQSmartWalletFactory` contracts replace the multi-signer wallet; 14 new Foundry tests pass; EntryPoint v0.9 via `account-abstraction` submodule update to `releases/v0.9`; CREATE2 salt = `keccak256(masterPkSeed \|\| masterPkRoot)` (phase 5). ERC-20 metadata bundle + Groth16 ZK clear-sign preserved as optional trailer sections on the unified sign payload, verifying against the firmware-embedded Merkle-rooted DBs just like before. Fixed a latent sphincs-c7 bug where `extract_ht_index` used C7 parameters (24-bit mask at bit 128) instead of C11 (16-bit mask at bit 143); self-verification now passes keygen → sign → verify roundtrip. |
| 2026-04-16 | OPTIGA Trust M silicon bring-up | TRUST-M-SHIELD on B-U585I-IOT02A via breadboard wired to I2C1. Rewrote `secure/src/optiga/apdu.rs` for correct wire format (positional InData not TLV, CMD bytes with 0x80 CLEAR_LAST_ERROR flag, access-condition tags AutoRef=0x23/Conf=0x20, data-type tag 0xE8, AUTHREF type 0x31), added `get_random` + `hmac_verify` primitives. Switched `authenticate_and_read` to proper HMAC challenge-response protocol (chip-side verify via DecryptSym CMD 0x95 + tag 0x43). Added admin factory-reset via shielded-connection Conf(E140) path — avoids SE050-style permanent lockout by making every user OID's Change AC `Auto(F1D0) OR Conf(E140)`. Physical fixes validated on real hardware: CTL→3V3 jumper required, 50µs guard time between register-write/read transactions required, ReSynch is fire-and-forget. OpenApplication now returns valid response. Saved bring-up quirks to memory (`project_optiga_bringup.md`). |
| 2026-04-17 | C10 bootstrap cutover | Replaced SPHINCS+C11 bootstrap identity with **SPHINCS+C10** (`h=18 d=2 a=11 k=13 w=8 l=43 target_sum=205 sig=4008`, 4073-byte Type 1 wire frame). New `sphincs-c10/` crate with correct portable `extract_ht_index` (4-byte load for H=18 vs 3-byte for H=16) replaces the old `sphincs-c7/` directory; firmware/crypto/sign-state-machine/shared wire formats all ported. New on-chain `SPHINCsC10Asm.sol` SHA-256-precompile verifier (subtree_h=9, 9-level Merkle auth, target_sum=205) replaces `SPHINCsC11Asm.sol`. Added per-chain `bootstrapUses` counter in `PQOwnable` storage (ERC-7201 slot+1), `MAX_BOOTSTRAP_USES = 65_536` cap enforced in `PQSmartWallet._validateSignature` pre-check and bumped via `_bumpBootstrapUses(cap)` after successful Type 1. Added `BootstrapKeyUsed(newCount)` event and `bootstrapUses()` view. Factory `createAccount` salt still `sha256(masterPkSeed || masterPkRoot)`, so the C11→C10 change re-bases every wallet address for a given seed — acceptable since no live deployments exist. 5 new `SPHINCsC10AsmTest` Foundry tests driven by a Rust-generated `c10_test_vectors.json` (keygen + sign + self-verify runs under `cargo test -p sphincs-c10 --test gen_test_vectors --release`), 4 new bootstrap-counter tests in `PQSmartWalletTest` covering success bump, failure no-bump, cap-rejects-overflow, and post-exhaustion Type 2 still works. All 33 Foundry tests pass. SHA-256 stays throughout; no keccak256 regressions. |

Generates 16 manifests via the Infineon `protected_update_data_set` tool (now buildable on Linux via cloned mbedtls 2.28.8 + CRLF/include-separator/buffer-overflow patches) signed by Infineon's sample EC P-256 key, embedded via `include_bytes!`. New `flash-hw-optiga-unlock-test` + `flash-hw-optiga-shield-handshake-only` Makefile targets. |
| 2026-04-17 | All-C10 slot cutover + stateless firmware | Per-slot signing key is now SPHINCS+C10 instead of the legacy FORS+C — one primitive, one verifier, no variable-length Type 2. The firmware is **stateless** for slot selection: the companion supplies `(chain_id, slot_index, flags)` on every `CMD_SIGN_USEROP`; `FLAG_REGISTER_SLOT` (bit 30 of flags) tells the firmware when to emit a Type 1 ahead of Type 2. Deleted `legacy-fosc/` crate (root + workspace refs), `secure/src/nsc/legacy_flash.rs` (flash pages 123-124 no longer used for slot state), `secure/src/nsc/cmd_get_slot_info.rs` (CMD 17 retired), `contracts/.../LegacyForsCVerifier.sol` and `ILegacyVerifier.sol`. `PQSmartWallet` gains a second on-chain counter `slotUses[slotKey]` capped at `MAX_SLOT_USES = 65_536`, bumped by `_bumpSlotUses` inside the Type 2 path; `PQOwnable` extends its ERC-7201 struct with the mapping and a `SlotKeyUsed(slotKey, newCount)` event. Single `c10Verifier` now handles both Type 1 and Type 2 (same stateless SHA-256-precompile verifier, different `(pk_seed, pk_root)` per call). Slot C10 keys are derived deterministically from `(slot_master_entropy, slot_index)` via new domain tags `"slot_c10_sk_seed"` / `"slot_c10_pk_seed"` and cached in SRAM across the unlock session only. New `secure/src/crypto.rs::derive_c10_slot_keypair_with_progress` helper mirrors the master path; `SecureState::SLOT_CACHE: Option<CachedSlot>` replaces the FORS+C slot cache. Wire formats: Type 2 is now fixed at 4073 bytes (C10 sig); `SIG_TYPE2_LEN = 4073`; `MAX_SIGN_RESPONSE_LEN = 8246`; removed `FORSC_BODY_LEGACY`, `SIG_MIN/MAX_LEGACY`, `Q_MAX_LEGACY`, `LEGACY_WRAPPER_*`, `NscStatus::SlotExhausted`, `CMD_GET_SLOT_CACHE_INFO`, `CMD_SIGN_SLOT_LEGACY`, `CMD_REGISTER_SLOT_CACHE`, `INS_V2_*LEGACY*`. Master C10 derivation unchanged → every seed still maps to the same CREATE2 wallet address. CLAUDE.md invariants updated (old #6 `next_q`-before-flash removed; #7 now covers both use caps; new #8 for the stateless firmware property). Builds clean for `thumbv8m.main-none-eabi` across all feature combos (`mock-se`, `e2e-test`, `bench-key-speed + stm32u585`, `usb`). 4-scenario QEMU e2e runner exercises register/repeat/rotate/second-chain; bench exercises cold first-sign / N cached Type 2 / second-chain cached-slot. |

Fixes: `secure_log!`-gate the unconditional `hprintln!` in `hw::hash::init_clock`'s SHA-256 self-test (DHCSR `C_DEBUGEN` runtime check) so standalone firmware stops HardFaulting pre-OLED. STM32U5 TRNG init rewritten with the NIST-compliant CR value `0x00F00D00`, CONDRST at bit 30 (not bit 6), and SEIS/CEIS clear path; init moved to after `sau::init()` so GTZC has assigned RNG's security attribute. | 2026-04-20 | Firmware update subsystem — hash-signature PQ model | New end-to-end signed firmware-update pipeline. Reproducible builds (`.cargo/config.toml` with `--remap-path-prefix` + `--build-id=none`, `make verify-repro` diffs two clean builds, SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH from git, docs/reproducible-builds.md). New workspace members: `fw-manifest/` (no_std manifest layout + CRC32-IEEE + verify chain; 11 unit tests), `fwsign/` (host-side signer: `keygen`/`pubkey`/`sign`/`verify`/`verify-release`/`extract-sig`/`inspect` with Argon2id + XChaCha20-Poly1305 at-rest key encryption), `fsbl/` (immutable 32 KB-budget first-stage bootloader, currently 18 KB with software SHA-256). Secure-world additions: bank-2 (NS flash) write/erase via NSCR in `hw/flash.rs`, OTP rollback counter in `hw/otp.rs` (32×32-bit tally = 1024 commits), boot-state page in `hw/boot_state.rs`, full `fw_update/` state machine with `{begin,chunk,commit,status,abort}` NSC handlers + CMSE veneers. Shared types: `CMD_FW_*` 20..24, `INS_V2_FW_*` 0x70..0x74, seven new `NscStatus::FwUpdate*` variants. USB protocol: 8 KB manifest chained over APDU v2, 1 KB chunks, reuses `FW_STATUS_RESPONSE_LEN` for progress polling. Crypto: **SPHINCS+C10 end-to-end for sign + verify**, entire path PQ-secure; the minimal signed preimage is `SHA-256("PQFW_V1" \|\| fw_version_be \|\| secure_hash \|\| nonsecure_hash)` — 75 bytes reconstructable from `(version, secure.elf, nonsecure.elf)` alone, so independent auditors verify via `fwsign verify-release` without parsing any manifest. One `.pqfw` per release (slot byte is unsigned metadata). Requires PIN unlock on every command (defence in depth). Anti-rollback enforced via OTP fuses (RDP-2-resistant). Power-fail safe: inactive slot fully erased + written + re-hashed before any boot-state flip. Docs: `docs/firmware-update.md` (architecture + verify-it-yourself + PQ inventory). Complete file list: `.cargo/config.toml`, `fw-manifest/{Cargo.toml,src/lib.rs}`, `fwsign/{Cargo.toml,src/{main,keystore,elf,bundle}.rs, src/subcommands/*.rs, tests/sign_verify_roundtrip.rs}`, `fsbl/{Cargo.toml,memory-stm32u585.x,build.rs,src/*.rs}`, `secure/src/{fw_update/*.rs, hw/{otp,boot_state}.rs, nsc/cmd_fw_*.rs}`, `docs/{firmware-update,reproducible-builds}.md`. Remaining: trusted-UI confirm dialog (stubbed until the `secure/src/tx/display/` refactor lands), A/B linker-script split, hardware bring-up, companion updater, WRP1A in ob-configurator, CI signing pipeline. `make verify-repro` passes (byte-identical ELFs). 19 crate tests pass (11 fw-manifest + 4 fwsign keystore/elf + 4 fwsign integration). |
| 2026-04-20 | #24 OPTIGA pairing restructure — OTP-derived PBS + HUK re-root | Stage 1 landed in source: `hw/otp.rs` extended with `read_device_master` / `burn_device_master` / `is_device_master_burned` / `ensure_device_master` over OTP bytes 128..160 (Trezor-style first-boot self-provisioning — no factory target, device burns its own 32 TRNG bytes on first power-up and locks the region). New `hw/secret_keys.rs` exposes HMAC-SHA256 derivations per domain label: `optiga_pairing_secret` / `se050_scp03_enc_key` / `se050_scp03_mac_key` / `tropic01_pairing_key`. `optiga::mod::setup_pbs_no_handshake` rewritten to consume `optiga_pairing_secret` instead of `rng::fill`; `write_pbs` call deleted; LcsO=op bump now additionally refuses to proceed unless `is_device_master_burned()` is true. `optiga::mod::load_pbs` collapsed to a one-liner that runs the HKDF derive + `shield.load_pbs`. `hw/huk.rs::derive_device_key` re-rooted off `measured_boot::firmware_hash()` and onto `otp::ensure_device_master()`; return type bumped to `Result<[u8;32], OtpError>`. `hw/flash.rs` PBS seal infrastructure deleted (`read_pbs` / `write_pbs` / `erase_pbs_page` / `PBS_PAGE_ADDR` / `PbsLoadError` / `PBS_WRAP_DOMAIN` / `PBS_BLOB_LEN` / `is_pbs_blank`). `optiga-bringup-fresh` Cargo feature removed + all feature-gated code paths collapsed (`auth_ref_is_authref_typed` deleted, `lock_oid` / `provision_user_oid` / `store_objects.already_provisioned` unconditionally locked). Makefile `flash-hw-optiga-reset` recipe no longer lists the feature. New `otp-hardcoded-master-key` dev feature returns the ASCII constant `"PQSIGNER-TEST-OTP-MASTER-DNS-v1!"` so the derivation path can be exercised on the bench without consuming real OTP; guarded against production builds (`compile_error!` in `nsc/mod.rs` fires on `stm32u585 + !debug_assertions + !e2e-test`) and against combining with `optiga-lock-operational` (separate `compile_error!` — would lock chips to a shared compile-time PBS). Production-security.md §1.6 + §2.6 document the architecture and the Trezor DHUK/BHK/OTP three-tier model we're adopting in stages. All compile-matrix variants pass (`stm32u585 + dual-se + hardcoded + e2e-test`, `stm32u585 + dual-se + e2e-test`, default QEMU); 50/50 host tests pass.
