| 2026-05-12 | `tools/sca/fault_sweep_c10v.py` — multi-vector sweep now completes (reused-emu optimisation) + clean result + Finding F-6 | The 6-invalid-vector C10-verify sweep added in `6659e43` timed out (`timeout 1500` killed it after ~24 min — each iteration re-parsed the `sphincs-c10`-linked ELF, and `mutated-FORS-auth` / `mutated-WOTS-sigma` / `mutated-WOTS-count-target-sum-fail` make `verify` run **~1.2–1.8 MILLION instructions** — see F-6 — so emulating the full thing per sweep position is infeasible). **Fixes**: (1) reuse one persistent emulator (`reset()` + re-write the 4 input buffers + zero `verify`s ~32 KB stack region between iterations — the latter makes the reuse safe even on the fault path: a fault that skips a stack write then reads a *zero*, not a previous iterations value); the canonical rainbow pattern, ~0.2 ms/iter vs the per-iter ELF-reload bottleneck. (2) split the instruction budget: `COUNT_BUDGET = 3_000_000` for the one-time `instr_count`/baseline runs, `SWEEP_BUDGET = 25_000` per faulted sweep emulation (a normal `verify` returns in ~7.5 k); `SWEEP_CAP = 8_000` max sweep positions per vector — so the three multi-million-instr vectors get their first 8 k instructions swept (the early checks, where a forge-relevant fault would live) and each emulation caps at 25 k → a fault during the multi-million-instr chain shows up as "hung" (verify never returns → non-forge). **Result: CLEAN** — across all 6 invalid vectors (`wrong-message` / `wrong-root` / `mutated-R` at full ~7521 instr; `mutated-FORS-auth` / `mutated-WOTS-sigma` / `mutated-WOTS-count-target-sum-fail` at first 8 k) × all 3 fault models (skip / stuck-at-0 / stuck-at-FF), **no single fault made a forged C10 signature verify as good** — ~188–241 crash on an invalid instruction, ~15–43 hang, the rest correctly rejected. `make c10v` exits 0, runs in ~1–2 min; exits 1 the instant any single fault flips a reject into an accept. **Finding F-6 (low-severity observation)**: `sphincs_c10::verify` (the Rust reference impl) does ~1.2–1.8 M instructions on a malformed FORS-auth-path / WOTS-sigma / WOTS-digit value — i.e. its chain/loop lengths arent bounded defensively against an out-of-range value. Still a *reject* (correctness holds — a malformed sig never verifies). On-chain `SPHINCsC10Asm.sol` would do ~1–2 M SHA-256 precompile `staticcall`s → blow past `verificationGasLimit` → OOG revert → the bundlers `eth_estimate…`/simulation catches it → the userOp is dropped before it lands → no DoS in the ERC-4337 flow. But worth a fail-fast `digit < W` / bounded-loop assertion in `sphincs-c10::verify` (and a look at whether the Yul verifier has the same shape) so an auditor isnt surprised. **Not fixed** — `sphincs-c10` / the Yul verifier, out of scope of this tooling pass; flagged for the maintainer. README §"Full C10 verify fault sweep" + new §"F-6" updated. Files: `tools/sca/{fault_sweep_c10v.py,README.md}`, `docs/work-todo.md`.
| 2026-05-12 | `fi::check_true_into_sentinel` — sentinel-encoded FI-guard return; migrated all ~13 `check_true` callsites (mitigates F-2; partly F-5; F-4 re-framed) | Acting on the recommendations. The `if !guard() { err }` / bool-return idiom (Findings F-2/F-5) lets a single fault defeat a verify-before-release gate at the *call site* — skip the `bl check_true`, the post-check branch, or stuck-at the bool-return register. `secure/src/fi.rs`: new `pub fn check_true_into_sentinel<F: FnMut()->bool>(cond) -> u32` — same guarded body as `check_true` (double-eval `cond` / `wait_random` / hamming-distant sentinel commit / re-check) but returns `OK_SENTINEL`(0xA5A5A5A5)/`FAIL_SENTINEL`(0x5A5A5A5A) instead of a `bool`, so a garbage/`FAIL_SENTINEL` return register is overwhelmingly `!= OK_SENTINEL` and the caller's `if … != OK_SENTINEL { err }` still takes the error path. Both fns kept as standalone bodies (~10 lines duplicated): making `check_true` a *wrapper* over `check_true_into_sentinel` adds a `== OK_SENTINEL -> bool` reduction that is itself a one-skip-to-a-truthy-`FAIL_SENTINEL` step — `make fi` caught and rejected that on the first build. `check_true`'s doc-comment rewritten (points at the sentinel form; honest re F-5). Migrated all ~13 `check_true` callsites in the `secure` crate to `if crate::fi::check_true_into_sentinel(C) != crate::fi::OK_SENTINEL { err }`: `crypto.rs::c10_sign_verified_with_progress`; `nsc/mod.rs::gated_unlock` (`match result { Ok(master) if verdict == OK_SENTINEL => …, Ok(_) => Err(InternalError), Err(e) => Err(e) }`); `nsc/cmd_sign_userop.rs` x3; `nsc/cmd_sign_userop_batch.rs` x3; `nsc/cmd_sign_offchain.rs`; `dual_se.rs`; `hw/otp.rs`; `hw/flash.rs` x2. 12 of the 13 are the identical regex-applied edit; `gated_unlock` done by hand. Effect: F-2 -> 'skip the bl' and 'stuck-at the bool return' are no longer call-site bypasses at any gated site; residual = the irreducible 'skip the caller's `if … != OK_SENTINEL { err }` branch' + the boolean-source routes (corrupting `cond`, out of scope). F-5 -> the result/return-path 2-skip route and the stuck-at route gone; residual = the 2-coordinated-skip route inside `check_true_into_sentinel` itself — accept. F-4 -> re-framed to 'minor': the page-124 free-guess only hits the MCU's *redundant* counter; `gated_unlock` bumps page-124 *before* `se.unlock(pin)`, so a glitched bump still leaves `se.unlock` running and the SE counts the wrong PIN in silicon (invariant #2); boot reconciles to the strictest counter -> the SE rate-limit (10->wipe) holds -> recommend accept. Harness mirrors (`tools/sca/fi_target/src/main.rs`): `sca_c10_verify_release` / `sca_pin_attempts_bump` / `sca_pin_gated_unlock` updated. Verified: thumbv8m build clean (mock-se+…+stm32u585 and dual-se+…+stm32u585); `cargo test -p sphincs-tz-secure --release` 105/105 (incl. `glitched_unlock_returns_internal_error`, the `gated_unlock`-path cases, `fi::tests`); `make fi` / `make c10` / `make pin` all exit 0 (the `make c10` F-2 residual shrank — 'skip the bl' gone; remaining 4-6 hits = the irreducible post-check-branch / F-5-internal stuff). `make e2e` (QEMU unified-sign) timed out at the 10-min budget — QEMU's software-SHA C10-sign x2 being slow, not a regression (and likely pre-existing); `make run` smoke pending. README §Findings updated (F-2/F-4/F-5). NOT done: F-6 (`sphincs-c10::verify` ~1.2-1.8M-instr unbounded chain on a malformed digit — touches `sphincs-c10` + its Solidity/Lean twins; harmless on-chain (OOG revert -> bundler simulation catches it); flagged for the maintainer); the deferred 3-fault / 2-sign-differential harnesses. Files: `secure/src/{fi.rs,crypto.rs,nsc/mod.rs,nsc/cmd_sign_userop.rs,nsc/cmd_sign_userop_batch.rs,nsc/cmd_sign_offchain.rs,dual_se.rs,hw/otp.rs,hw/flash.rs}`, `tools/sca/{fi_target/src/main.rs,README.md}`, `docs/work-todo.md`. |
| 2026-05-15 | F-21 correction — SE050 PIN-counter reconcile is feasible after all; three-way reconcile shipped | User challenged the F-21 commit's framing ("SE050 silicon counter can't be peeked without burning an attempt"). On re-investigation that claim was wrong: NXP `Se05x_API_ReadObjectAttributes` (CLA=0x80 INS_READ P2_ATTRIBUTES) returns the USERID auth object's `auth_attempts` / `max_attempts` TLV fields over the transport SCP03 channel, no user-session auth required, no attempt consumed. SDK parsing reference: `sss_pkcs11_pal_helpers.c:603-613 pkcs11_parse_atrribute` (wire format: `obj_id(4) ‖ obj_type(1) ‖ auth_attr(1) ‖ auth_attempts(2) ‖ auth_obj_id(4) ‖ max_attempts(2) ‖ policy…`). New `Se050::pin_attempt_count_raw()` parses bytes [6..8] for `auth_attempts`, returns the **failed-attempts USED** count (aligns to OPTIGA's F1E1 semantics so reconcile is a straight `!=` against MCU page-124 — also caught and fixed an interim "remaining" semantics regression I almost shipped). Wired into the `SecureElement::pin_attempt_count` trait impl behind a `MAX_ATTEMPTS` cap. **`DualSecureElement::pin_attempt_count`** now returns `max(OPTIGA_used, SE050_used)` (strictest = highest used count). New **`SecureElement::pin_attempt_counts_divergent()`** trait method (default false; Dual overrides to flag OPTIGA↔SE050 mismatch). **`nsc::reconcile_pin_attempts`** upgraded to three-way: wipe iff `MCU != strictest_SE` OR intra-SE disagreement. Defense bound improves from "attacker resets ≤1 side" to "attacker resets ≤2 sides" — coordinated three-side reset still bypasses, but that needs OPTIGA E140 PBS rewrite + SE050 USERID delete + MCU page-124 erase in one campaign. Touched: `secure/src/{se050/mod.rs,dual_se.rs,secure_element.rs,nsc/mod.rs,main.rs}` + `docs/work-todo.md §4`. cargo check passes on `stm32u585+dual-se+bhk` and `mock-se` QEMU shapes; on-silicon `pin-gate-hw-counter-e2e` re-validation pending (silicon path covered by the unchanged validation hooks). Lesson recorded — work-todo entries that say "X can't be done" deserve a primary-source check before they become invariant docstrings; the §4 claim and the F-21 commit's docstring had each reinforced the other for weeks. |
| 2026-05-18 | F-9 scared convergence — quantitative noise-floor confirmation | New `f9_collect_for_scared.py` (lascar venv, dumps 600 × 10M × stride=1 to a 6-GB `.npz`) + `f9_scared_convergence.py` (scared-sca venv, replays `TTestAnalysis` on 30 incrementing trace-count prefixes) builds on `f9_retest.py` to test the 'max|t|=4.93 is noise floor' claim against a √N-scaling alternative hypothesis. **scared reproduces lascar's final-N max|t| byte-exactly (both 4.931 at N=600)** — independent venv, independent estraces+ttest implementation, same trace tensor, same answer. **Convergence shape**: max|t| 6.80 → 4.93 from N=20 to N=600 (slight decrease, no √N growth); 30 prefix points all stay in [4.9, 6.0]; multi-testing-corrected threshold over 10M samples is ≈ 5.6σ which the curve never crosses. **Reads cleanly as noise**: under H1 (real msg-dependent signal) we'd expect max|t| ≈ 37 at N=600 given N=20's reading; observed flat-or-decreasing rules out a √N-scaling channel. **Stronger claim than lascar's single point**: not just 'max|t|=4.93 at full N' but 'no detectable √N-scaling msg-dependence anywhere in [N=20, N=600]' — eliminates the 'we just didn't take enough traces' objection. Trade-offs caught: scared's `TTestAnalysis` only takes `precision=` (no `convergence_step=`, which is Attack-only), so the convergence loop is rolled by replaying `TTestAnalysis()` on `ths[:k]` per step; pre-casting uint8→float32 OOM-kills at 24 GB → keep uint8 + `scared.set_batch_size(64.0)`; matplotlib added to scared-sca's venv. Makefile targets `f9-scared-collect` + `f9-scared`; out/ gitignored (6 GB `.npz`). README §F-9 re-test extended with the convergence table + interpretation. |
| 2026-05-18 | SAES-DHUK CMAC wrapper leakage — 4-mode TVLA, all clean | New `saes_kdf_target/` (thumbv8m crate that `#[path]`-includes `secure/src/cmac.rs` byte-for-byte under a software-AES-256 closure standing in for the unemulable SAES coprocessor) + `leakage_saes_kdf.py` (4-mode TVLA via lascar). Tests both `cmac_generic` and `kdf_cmac_counter_generic` under: vary-key fix-msg, fix-key vary-msg, vary-key fix-label, fix-key vary-label. **All four max|t| = 0.00 over 600 traces × 7-9K samples**: rainbow's `mem_address` channel records the Hamming weight of memory access ADDRESSES (not loaded values), and neither the bitsliced soft AES nor the CMAC wrapper bookkeeping (`double_l`, CBC chain XOR, final-block branch dispatch, KDF label+counter packing) makes any input-dependent memory address. Narrow but useful claim: rules out one specific production leak class (data-dependent memory addresses in the CPU wrapper, on top of whatever the SAES silicon itself leaks). Power/EM leakage on register VALUES and the SAES coprocessor's own side-channel surface still require on-silicon SCA with a scope. Makefile targets `build-saes-kdf` + `saes-kdf`; README §SAES-DHUK KDF wrapper leakage carries the full table + interpretation + production-scope caveats. |
| 2026-05-18 | wait_random loop-count leakage — flat (max|t| = 2.68 over 736 samples) | New `leakage_wait_random.py` + `sca_fi_wait_random_n(byte)` symbol in fi_target. TVLA fixed-vs-random byte fed to `pqsigner_fi::wait_random_loop` — fixed group at byte=200, random group in [180, 220] (tight range so all traces have similar lengths and don't get truncated to the shortest). **Result: max|t| = 2.68 over 736 samples, well below the 4.5 TVLA threshold**. The loop body's per-iteration mem_address pattern is identical across iterations regardless of the RNG byte (vread/vwrite to fixed stack-resident `i_storage` / `j_storage` addresses; only the VALUES change, not the addresses). Closes work-todo §18b 'Leakage pass over the FI guards themselves' as **clean**: the FI guard is not itself a side channel on this measurement. Caveat: rainbow's mem_address channel is address-only, not value-leakage; this rules out address-side leakage but power/EM scoping on real silicon would be needed to characterise register-value leakage of the counter itself. Makefile target `wait-random-leak`. |

(b) **single-fault [stuck-at-FF]**: 3 hits in `check_true` result-register positions — exactly the F-2 stuck-at-on-return-register class documented in the README; bool-returning fns are inherently stuck-at-defeatable. (c) **two-fault [skip,skip]**: 322 total pair flips → 217 in scaffolding (expected) + 4 harness-artifact (closure cond() bypass) + **101 in pure check_true verdict/return code** — vs prior README count of 1. The 100× jump is a codegen-layout effect from the merge, not a new mechanism. (d) **wait_random sweeps**: 33-50 crashes per single-fault model on the post-loop halt check, 0 returned-normally — invariant guard fires as designed. **Production impact: none.** `grep \bcheck_true\b` outside of fi.rs's own definition confirms **zero production callers of the bool-returning check_true** — all ~13 call sites already use `check_true_into_sentinel` per F-5's documented mitigation. The bool variant exists only for the harness's testing convenience. F-5 README's 'Action taken: migrated all ~13 `check_true` callsites to compare a sentinel rather than a bare bool' is in effect. Harness flagged this as 'REGRESSION' because the single-fault [skip] sweep used to exit 0; the underlying weakness is the documented F-5 residual, just exposed more thoroughly by the merged tree's binary layout. Follow-up options (deferred): (1) update fi_target's `sca_fi_check_true` to probe `check_true_into_sentinel` instead (probes the actually-used primitive); (2) place a `udf`/long-branch padding between shim functions to defeat the fall-through class; (3) accept and update harness expected-exit-code semantics to INFO rather than REGRESSION for fi.rs check_true hits since the doc already says it's expected. |
| 2026-05-18 | **F-22 — BIP-39 wordlist lookup leaks 264 bits of entropy (production-critical)** | New `leakage_bip39.py` + `sca_bip39_word_indices` / `sca_bip39_wordlist_lookup` symbols in `kdf_target`. Control case (SHA-256 + 11-bit unpacking, NO wordlist load): **max|t| = 0.00**. Wordlist-lookup case (adds `WORDLIST[idx]` for each of 24 words): **max|t| = 27.98** (6.2× the 4.5 threshold), 5 peaks at samples 615/671/703/727/751 consistent with the 24 individual indexed loads. **Mechanism**: `Mnemonic::to_seed` (`bip39/src/lib.rs:183`) calls `self.words()` → `self.indices.iter().map(\|&i\| WORDLIST[i as usize])`, each load at `&WORDLIST[0] + idx * sizeof(&str)` — the load address encodes the secret index. 24 × 11 bits = 264 leaked bits > 256-bit BIP-39 entropy, so single-trace seed reconstruction is sufficient. **Severity HIGH (production-critical)**: this is the FIRST CPU operation on raw entropy in the first-unlock path; bypasses dual-SE split (entropy AT REST defense), FI guards (signature integrity defense), and hardware PIN gate (SE-storage defense). **Attack model**: power/EM scoping of the cold-boot first-unlock — single trace. After unlock the bip39_seed is zeroized so steady-state signs are unaffected — only the once-per-power-on derivation leaks. **Fix design**: constant-time wordlist scan (load all 2048 entries per word slot, `subtle::ConditionallySelectable` mask on idx) — ~393 KB reads per to_seed, ~1 ms on Cortex-M33; PLUS constant-time variable-length password assembly (interleaved-write per byte position, expensive but preserves BIP-39 compat — padding-based variants break compat). **Status**: documented as F-22 in tools/sca/README.md, fix DEFERRED pending crypto-eng review — implementation is substantial and high-stakes (BIP-39 hot path). Regression harness `make bip39-leak` MUST stay flat after fix AND `Mnemonic::to_seed("")` MUST stay byte-identical with the existing BIP-39 test vectors. |
| 2026-05-18 | **F-22 FIXED — BIP-39 wordlist leak closed (constant-time scan)** | `bip39/src/lib.rs` rewritten: new `WORDLIST_FLAT: [[u8; 8]; 2048]` + `WORDLIST_LENS: [u8; 2048]` (compile-time flat representation derived from the existing `WORDLIST: &[&str; 2048]` via a `const fn`); new `ct_load_word(target_idx)` does a 2048-entry mask-OR scan with `subtle`-style `(mask & bytes) | (~mask & acc)` folding (manual `ct_eq_u16` for the mask, no `subtle` dep). `Mnemonic::to_seed` rewritten in three constant-time phases: (1) resolve all 24 indices via `ct_load_word` → stack-resident `words[24][8]` + `lens[24]`; (2) cumulative offsets via pure arithmetic; (3) for each output byte position p in 0..215, scan all (word, byte) candidates and mask-OR the right byte into `password[p]` — every store goes to a loop-counter-derived address. **Critical implementation note**: `core::hint::black_box` barriers on each iteration's mask/entry/accumulator are LOAD-BEARING — without them LLVM folds the scan into a direct `WORDLIST_FLAT[target_idx]` lookup and the fix regresses to max|t| ≈ 5000 (vs the 0.00 we now get). Verification: `to_seed_matches_trezor_vectors` + 8 other bip39 tests pass (byte-identical to Trezor reference → recovery contract preserved); 1835/1835 secure host tests pass; `make bip39-leak` post-fix probe `sca_bip39_wordlist_lookup_ct` over 24,851 samples shows **max|t| = 0.00** (perfectly constant). Pre-fix probe `sca_bip39_wordlist_lookup` retained as regression sentinel (max|t| = 17.88 — confirms the harness still detects the originally-leaky pattern). Cost in production: ~393 KB stack reads + 41K inner-loop iters → ~2 ms wall on Cortex-M33, single-digit-percent of `derive_keypair_from_entropy`'s overall cost (dominated by SPHINCS+C10 keygen ~10s) and called once per first-unlock. Residual **F-23**: PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512's length-padding byte embeds the password's bit-length (sum of word lengths, ~7 bits of entropy — 30× smaller magnitude than F-22's 264 bits). Closing F-23 requires padding the word slots to fixed-length, which would break BIP-39 byte-equality with non-PQSigner verifiers; accepted as residual, flagged in the user-facing trust model. |

bounded by keystroke cadence (≤5/s long-presses), no UX impact. **Verification**: 1837/1837 secure host tests pass; 6 new tests in `bip39/tests/lookup.rs` lock in the public-API contract (`word_bytes_at` round-trips every wordlist entry; `is_exact_wordlist_entry` accepts every entry, rejects obvious non-words + the zero-padding trap + over-long inputs + ASCII case-sensitivity at the byte level); `make seedwizard-exact-leak` validates baseline-leaks + CT-clean post-fix. **Deferred hygiene**: `bip39::lookup_word_exact` still uses leaky binary search but its sole caller is `Mnemonic::from_words` invoked only from the e2e-test path with fixed TEST_WORDS (no production secret); `measured_boot::render_all_words` still uses `WORDLIST[idx]` but the displayed words derive from firmware_hash which is public by construction. Both flagged for a separate "delete leaky WORDLIST[i] access pattern" hygiene commit so this F-27 diff stays focused on the production-critical fix. Full §F-27 in tools/sca/README.md. |

| 2026-05-19 | §25 Gap 4 — SE050 silicon-locked SW → `UnlockError::PinLocked` (code landed; bench validation deferred) | Closes the corruption/desync stuck-loop case where MCU page-124 has been reset (flash fault, RDP regression mid-cycle, dev-tool intervention) but SE050 UserID is silicon-locked — pre-fix, a fresh PIN attempt hit the `Status(sw)` catch-all → `InternalError` → gateway treated as transient, no wipe → device stuck. Investigation: original work-todo guess "0x6983" was the **A71CL legacy** code (`SE_HAL_Lib.h::A71CL_SE_HAL_ERR_KEY_LOCKED = 0xCC // 6983`); SE050's `se05x_tlv.h::smStatus_t` does NOT include 0x6983. SE050 uses **0x6986** (`SM_ERR_COMMAND_NOT_ALLOWED`, AN13030 wording "Command not allowed — access denied based on object policy") for policy-denial when an auth object stops accepting VERIFY — the documented-by-deduction match (AN12413 is behind NXP login, on-silicon SW capture deferred). Fix: new `Se050Error::AuthMethodBlocked` variant in `apdu.rs`; `apdu::verify_session` adds `Status(0x6986) → AuthMethodBlocked` arm alongside the existing `0x6985 || 0x63xx → PinIncorrect`; new pure helper `classify_se050_unlock_error(&Se050Error) -> UnlockError` in `mod.rs` maps `AuthMethodBlocked → PinLocked`; `Se050::unlock` map_err refactored to call the helper (side-effect arm zeros `self.remaining` on AuthMethodBlocked). End-to-end dispatch: `apdu::verify_session` → `AuthMethodBlocked` → `Se050::unlock` map_err → `UnlockError::PinLocked` → `DualSecureElement::unlock` propagation → `cmd_request_unlock`'s `Err(UnlockError::PinLocked) => trigger_lockout_wipe()` arm. 4 new host pin-tests `gap4_*` in `se050_under_test/pure_tests.rs` (1841 total, was 1837): (a) apdu arm exists; (b) classifier maps AuthMethodBlocked→PinLocked; (c) PinIncorrect still maps to PinIncorrect (catastrophic-UX regression guard); (d) catch-all still on InternalError (no over-eager wipe on transport glitches). Conservatively narrow per advisor review — only 0x6986 mapped, not 0x6982 (generic, false-positive wipe risk worse than the rare desync). On-silicon validation: next bench session, run `iterative_wipe` against a throwaway UserID to drive it to attempts=0, send VERIFY, capture SW — if not 0x6986 update both the `verify_session` arm and the `AuthMethodBlocked` doc. `pin-gate-wipe-e2e` infrastructure already covers the wiring; just needs SW-capture on the post-lockout attempt. |

| 2026-05-19 | F-24 stage E sub-channel 4 — **Phase 1 (decoy frames) LANDED**; bench flicker validation pending | F-24 stages A-D (2026-05-18) closed the CPU-side leak surface for seed display. The four hardware-only residuals were documented but not implemented. Phase 1 closes sub-channel 4 (I²C bus-EM emission) via persistence-of-vision decoy frames. Implementation: `secure/src/ui/seed_wizard.rs::show_mnemonic` generates N=4 valid BIP-39 mnemonics from independent `rng_strong::fill` entropy at wizard entry. New `show_mnemonic_page_with_decoys` frame loop interleaves the real mnemonic (200 ms hold) with one decoy per cycle (40 ms hold), cycling through all 4 decoys round-robin — 5:1 real:decoy time ratio. Every frame (real OR decoy) routes through the same constant-time `render_mnemonic_page` so the per-frame CPU trace is content-independent (F-24 stages A-D contract preserved). Button events return immediately from the frame loop; idle timeout triggers wipe via the existing path. **Mnemonic's Drop zeroizes indices** so decoys wipe automatically on wizard exit; the raw 32-byte entropy buffers are explicitly zeroized after Mnemonic construction. **Failure path**: if rng_strong::fill fails for any decoy (extremely rare — 3-source XOR'd TRNG would need multiple simultaneous faults), show_mnemonic returns Cancelled with "RNG failed retry" status; wizard outer loop retries. 5 new source-text regression sentinels in `secure/src/ui_under_test/pure_tests.rs::negative_*decoy*` pin: (a) rng_strong as entropy source (not deterministic), (b) Mnemonic::from_entropy for valid BIP-39 checksums (un-filterable from real), (c) entropy zeroize post-construct, (d) 5:1 time ratio constants pinned, (e) frame loop routes through render_mnemonic_page (CT path). 1847/1847 secure host tests pass (was 1842; +5 sentinels). cargo check clean on stm32u585+dual-se+bhk+ui-oled with and without debug-log. **Hardware flicker validation outstanding** — bench user reads 24 words off real silicon (SSD1306 @ 400 kHz I²C, ~36 ms per flush_with_secret_rows call) and reports whether the 200ms:40ms cadence is readable. If unreadable, increase REAL_FRAME_HOLD_MS to 400-500 (smooths visual, weakens defense). **Phase 2 deferred**: constant-pixel-count encoding (sub-channel 3, power-rail) requires per-word XOR-complement tables that bring every row's lit-pixel count to a constant — significantly more work than Phase 1; revisit after Phase 1's UX is validated. Threat-model boundary: Phase 1 defends an EM-bus attacker without optical access (foil-wrapped device, dark room, internal RMA scenarios). The realistic threat (camera attacker) is unaffected — decoys are still visible to the camera; the user's responsibility for physical privacy during provisioning is unchanged. tools/sca/README.md §F-24 sub-channel 4 updated to reflect Phase 1 landing + Phase 2 deferred. |
| 2026-05-19 | F-24 stage E Phase 1 — bench-tested on OLED, defense doesn't work on bistable displays; code gated behind `decoy-frames` feature pending LCD hardware | New `decoy-flicker-test` Cargo feature + `make decoy-flicker-hw` Makefile target. Short-circuits `main()` into a forever-loop that renders page 0 of a fixed test mnemonic interleaved with 4 fixed decoys at the production cadence — no wizard, no buttons, no SE access, just OLED rendering for bench visual inspection. Result: at 5:1 (200ms:40ms) the user sees N+1 mnemonics cycling, NOT a stable real with subliminal decoys. Bumping REAL_FRAME_HOLD_MS to 2000 didn't help (user still perceives content changes, not flicker). **Root cause**: OLED pixels are bistable — once painted, they hold state indefinitely until repainted. Persistence-of-vision (Trezor's referenced mechanism) works on CRTs / movie projectors (natural decay between frames); on OLEDs the eye locks onto every painted frame. We cannot paint faster than ~25 ms over I²C @ 400 kHz, and a 25 ms content change is well above the eye's flicker-fusion threshold (~10 ms for content). **Code disposition**: Phase 1 decoy-frame code stays in tree (`show_mnemonic_with_decoys`, `show_mnemonic_page_with_decoys`) gated behind a new `decoy-frames` Cargo feature (default OFF). `show_mnemonic` now splits to `show_mnemonic_simple` (default) vs `show_mnemonic_with_decoys` (feature-gated). 5 source-text regression sentinels preserved. Production wizard reverts to the simple stable-render path so users can read their seed. `make decoy-flicker-hw` target stays for future revalidation. **Path forward**: re-test on the ZT165M017AT TFT LCD (NV3007 driver, SPI, 142×428, Tr+Tf typ 35 ms max 40 ms) when it's wired. On slow-response LCDs the pixel response time IS the persistence-of-vision mechanism — a decoy painted for ~5-10 ms and immediately overwritten by real never reaches full pixel transition. Tune REAL_FRAME_HOLD_MS / DECOY_FRAME_HOLD_MS empirically on the LCD via `decoy-flicker-test`, then flip `decoy-frames` ON in the shipping profile if validated. Phase 2 (constant-pixel-count encoding for sub-channel 3 power-rail) also becomes less necessary on the LCD: backlight current is constant regardless of pixel state (3 white LEDs in parallel, ~60 mA typ), so the power-rail leak signature on the LCD is structurally weaker than OLED. tools/sca/README.md §F-24 sub-channel 4 updated with full bench finding + LCD path. 1847/1847 host tests pass (5 regression sentinels still match the in-tree decoy code). |

| 2026-05-19 | Factory provisioning — Phase A.1: rehearsal mode + compile fence + OTP sentinel + RDP2 sequencing docs | Three foot-gun protections added on top of the Phase A scaffold (commit `cab0530`): **(1) Rehearsal mode** via new `factory-provisioning-rehearsal` Cargo feature — steps 4 (DualSeProvision), 5 (WipeUserState), 6 (PostWipeValidation) SKIP their destructive calls in this build. OLED still cycles all 7 panels for visual validation; OTP sentinel still gets written but with `BIT_REHEARSAL` (not `BIT_PRODUCTION`). Distinct success panel "REHEARSAL OK / SE NOT changed / NOT for ship!" — operator + host fixture can't confuse it for production. New `make build-hw-factory-provisioning-rehearsal` target. **(2) Compile-time fence** — refuses to build the irreversible production profile (`optiga-lock-operational` or `bhk` without hardcoded-master-key or NEITHER `dev-testkey` NOR `otp-hardcoded-master-key`) without an explicit `factory-production-irreversible-im-sure` opt-in feature. Catches the "I forgot dev-testkey in the Makefile" class of accidental chip burns. Anyone editing Cargo can add/remove the opt-in — this is a foot-gun guard, not a security gate. Empirically validated: all 5 build paths (default, factory+dev-testkey, factory+rehearsal, factory+LcsO=Op+opt-in, factory+LcsO=Op-without-opt-in→compile error). **(3) OTP factory sentinel** at byte offset 160 of the STM32 OTP region (one 128-bit quad-word, just past the device master key). 3-bit tally encoding: bit 0 = "ran at least once", bit 1 = "rehearsal completed", bit 2 = "production completed". One-way per OTP semantics, but rehearsal-then-production is the expected dev iteration path (results in `0xFFFFFFF8` = both bits cleared). The host-side factory fixture reads the sentinel via probe-rs at `0x0BFA_00A0` BEFORE bumping the irreversible RDP=Level-2 option byte; fixture interprets `0xFFFFFFFA` (production only) or `0xFFFFFFF8` (both modes) as RDP2-ready, refuses RDP2 on any other state. Step 7 (new) writes the sentinel; the pre-populated-state check (step 3) also checks the sentinel + refuses to re-run a chip whose `BIT_PRODUCTION` is already cleared (`E0702`). New OTP API: `factory_sentinel_read() -> u32` + `unsafe factory_sentinel_record(bits_to_clear) -> Result<(), OtpError>`. `OTP_RESERVED_BYTES` bumped from 160 to 176 to include the sentinel region; matching host test updated. **Operator manual** (`docs/factory-provisioning.md`) updated with: rehearsal-mode panel preview + interpretation table, OTP sentinel encoding + host-fixture decode table, RDP2 section explaining the post-ceremony irreversible step + why the fixture must verify the sentinel before bumping, build-profile safety guard table. 1847/1847 standard host tests pass; 7 factory_provisioning tests (was 5 — added step-count + factory_sentinel_bit_layout). cargo check clean on all 5 build paths (4 valid + 1 deliberately-refused-by-fence). |
| 2026-05-19 | Factory provisioning — Phase A.2: flash + verify + RDP2-bump Makefile targets, early "ran" sentinel | Wires the host-side orchestration on top of Phase A.1's foot-gun protections. **Three new Makefile targets** + 1 helper script: (a) `make flash-hw-factory-provisioning` chains probe-rs download (NS + S) + STM32_Programmer_CLI TZ option-byte config + probe-rs reset + the new verifier script. Does NOT bump RDP2. (b) `make flash-hw-factory-provisioning-rehearsal` same as above but uses the rehearsal build (steps 4-6 skip destructive calls). (c) `make bump-rdp2-after-factory` re-reads the OTP sentinel, refuses unless `BIT_PRODUCTION` is cleared, prompts the operator to type `BUMP RDP2` as final confirmation, then runs `STM32_Programmer_CLI --optionbytes RDP=0xCC`. (d) `make factory-status-hw` read-only sentinel inspection (no flashing, no state change). **Host-side verifier script** `tools/factory-provisioning-verify.sh`: polls the OTP factory sentinel at `0x0BFA_00A0` via `probe-rs read 32` for up to 60s, requires the sentinel value to be stable across two successive reads (guards against mid-write race), decodes into one of 5 outcomes (DID_NOT_START / STARTED_FAILED / REHEARSAL_ONLY / PRODUCTION_OK / BOTH_OK), maps each to a script exit code (0=RDP2-eligible, 1=not-yet-complete, 2=probe failure, 3=corrupt sentinel, 4=bump refused). `--bump-rdp2` flag does the RDP2 transition only if the sentinel is RDP2-eligible AND the operator types `BUMP RDP2` at the interactive prompt. Configurable via `CHIP` / `OTP_SENTINEL_ADDR` / `POLL_TIMEOUT_SECS` / `POLL_INTERVAL_SECS` env vars. **Firmware fix**: `factory_provisioning::run_and_halt` now writes `BIT_RAN` to the OTP sentinel BEFORE step 1 (was only at step 7). This lets the host verifier distinguish "chip never started" (`0xFFFFFFFF`) from "started + halted at failure panel" (`0xFFFFFFFE`) — without the early write, both states looked identical and the host fixture would timeout on every failure. The early write is best-effort (silent on OTP fault) so a stuck OTP write doesn't brick the ceremony at entry. **Operator manual** updated: new "Quick procedure" section showing the three-make-target flow, new "Source map" entries for the script + targets. All 5 cargo build paths still clean; 1847 standard host tests pass; shell-script `bash -n` syntax check clean. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — phase B awaits the user's hardware-on-bench session. |

| 2026-05-19 | Prodtest firmware — Phase C (communication tests: OPTIGA + SE050 + USB integrity) | Adds the third tier of prodtest commands — communication tests that exercise the I²C and USB stacks end-to-end. **3 new commands at IDs 106-108:** `CMD_PRODTEST_OPTIGA_HANDSHAKE` lazily runs `OptigaTrustM::init()` (RST pulse + `OpenApplication`) then `GetRandom(16)`. On a fresh chip there's no PBS yet so the APDU goes through the plain non-shielded path (`shield.active == false` → `send_command` falls through to `transceive` without wrapping). Catches missing chip / broken solder / wedged I²C / wrong RST line / power-rail or clock issues / chip RNG defect (all-zero or all-0xFF). `CMD_PRODTEST_SE050_HANDSHAKE` same shape — `Se050::init()` (interface_reset + ATR + SCP03 session with NXP default platform keys) then `GetRandom(16)`. Catches missing chip / wrong ENA line / cold-boot timing (the existing 3-attempt retry loop already covers this) / pre-rotated SCP03 keys (chip wasn't blank) / RNG defect. `CMD_PRODTEST_USB_LOOPBACK` echoes N≤256 bytes — the fact the firmware received the command already proves USB RX works; this proves TX + full round-trip byte integrity for non-trivial payloads. Catches USB OTG FS TX corruption / HID report fragmentation bugs in the NS-side stack / buffer-overflow in the transport layer / sustained-TX VCC instability. **Wiring:** 3 new `#[cfg(feature = "prodtest")]` dispatch arms in `secure/src/nsc/mod.rs`, 3 new CMSE veneers (`nsc_prodtest_optiga_handshake` / `nsc_prodtest_se050_handshake` / `nsc_prodtest_usb_loopback`), 3 new handlers in `secure/src/nsc/prodtest.rs` that access the global `crate::SE` via the established `addr_of_mut!(crate::SE)` pattern (serialised by the dispatcher's non-reentrant invariant). The handshake handlers reuse `OptigaTrustM::random` / `Se050::random` so the test surface is just "does the existing random primitive work" — no new code path to bench-validate. **Host fixture runner** (`tools/factory-prodtest-runner.py`) gets `test_optiga_handshake` + `test_se050_handshake` (both check status==Ok + 16 bytes received + bytes ≠ all-zero / all-0xFF, log RNG output for per-die-uniqueness DB) + `test_usb_loopback` (deterministic XOR-with-0xA5 pattern catches byte-substitution + bit-flip + buffer-overflow, reports first-mismatch offset for diagnostics). New module pin-tests: `positive_phase_c_handshake_rng_lens_pinned` + `positive_usb_loopback_cap_matches_proto_doc`. Operator manual (`docs/factory-prodtest.md`) command table extended to 9 commands; per-command sections added with pass criteria + diagnostic value for each. **Build validation:** 4 build paths still pass — default (no prodtest), prodtest+dev-testkey, prodtest+factory-production-irreversible-im-sure, prodtest-without-opt-in→compile error (fence still fires). 1847/1847 default-features host tests pass; module-internal tests still pending host harness wiring (same situation as Phase A+B + LCD + factory_provisioning modules). `make build-hw-prodtest` succeeds. Python runner `--help` works; syntax check clean. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — Phase B awaits user's hardware-on-bench session. |

| 2026-05-19 | Prodtest firmware — Phase E (USB launch fix + INS dispatch + Python HID framing) | First Phase that makes the prodtest commands actually reachable over USB. **Three pieces:** (1) **Firmware USB-launch fix**: the prodtest main-loop short-circuit was halting in WFI before `boot_ns::boot`, so the NS USB stack never started — every "Phase A/B/C/D done" was firmware-only and not actually reachable from a host. Rewired to run the final-stage init that NS world depends on (`hw::usb_hw::init`, `setup_systick`, DWT cycle counter) then call `boot_ns::boot(NS_FLASH_BASE)` — same exit primitive the production main flow uses. The "PRODTEST READY" OLED panel shows briefly then NS world takes over. (2) **Per-CMD INS allocation + NS dispatch**: 10 new `INS_V2_PRODTEST_*` constants at 0x80..0x89 in `proto/src/lib.rs` (one INS per command — no cmd_id overhead inside the APDU body, full LC=255 budget per command). Mechanical mapping `INS = 0x80 + (CMD - 100)`. NS-side: 10 extern `nsc_prodtest_*` declarations in `nonsecure/src/nsc_api.rs::transport`, 10 `pub(super)` transport wrappers, 10 `pub fn prodtest_*` public API entries. NS USB dispatcher (`nonsecure/src/usb/commands.rs`): 10 `#[cfg(feature = "prodtest")]` match arms + 10 `cmd_prodtest_*` methods + new `prodtest_finalize(n, status)` helper that writes outputs to `RESP_BUF[..n]` followed by 2-byte SW (SW_OK=0x9000 on Ok, SW_INTERNAL_ERROR=0x6F00 otherwise). USB_LOOPBACK capped at 254 B (255 - 2 B SW headroom inside the 256 B `RESP_BUF`). Nonsecure Cargo gets a new `prodtest = ["stm32u585", "usb"]` feature. Makefile `build-hw-prodtest` updated to pass the new feature to nonsecure crate. (3) **Python host-side HID framing**: `tools/factory-prodtest-runner.py::ProdtestTransport` rewritten from a stub into the production transport — `_build_apdu` (CLA=0xF0 + INS + P1=0 + P2=0 + LC + data; LC ≤ 255 verified pre-build), `_frame_apdu` (chan/tag/seq/total_len header, 57 B first chunk + 59 B continuation chunks, byte-for-byte mirror of `tools/webhid_test.html::frameApdu`), `_read_response` (state machine matching `shared/src/apdu_framing.rs::HidFrameAssembler`'s expected/seq/chan_id reassembly). Linux hidapi quirk handled (advisor): leading 0x00 report-ID byte on `device.write` (kernel hidraw inspects byte 0 as report ID even when the descriptor has none); `device.read` does NOT include the byte so reassembly reads 64-byte frames directly. Status code mapping: SW_OK → STATUS_OK = 0 (so existing test_* wrappers' `status != STATUS_OK` checks Just Work); other SW values pass through unchanged so error logs preserve the wire SW. 60 s read timeout to accommodate BUTTON_TEST's ~30 s budget. INS_FOR_CMD lookup table for cmd→INS translation. **Build validation**: 4 secure-side build paths still clean (default, prodtest+dev-testkey, prodtest+factory-production-irreversible-im-sure, prodtest-no-opt-in→fence fires). New `make build-hw-prodtest` builds both secure + nonsecure cleanly with `--features stm32u585,usb,prodtest` on the NS side. 1847/1847 default-features host tests pass. Python AST clean; `--help` works. Operator manual extended with "Phase E wire format" section documenting APDU + HID framing layout + Linux hidapi quirk. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — Phase B awaits the user's hardware-on-bench session, which can now actually drive every prodtest CMD over USB rather than being firmware-only. |

| 2026-05-19 | §31b — FW-update trusted-UI confirm wired (all 4 fields) | Re-audit on 2026-05-19 corrected the original premise: `secure/src/ui/confirm.rs` was NOT a WIP — it's the production sign-confirm path used by `cmd_sign_userop` / `cmd_sign_userop_batch` / `cmd_sign_offchain`. The "stable again" comment on the `confirm_commit` stub was stale. Wiring the four §31b fields needed building a 4-page `Page` array and calling `ui::confirm::confirm(&pages)`. **Page 1** — title + "to vA.B.C.D" (u32 fw_version decoded as 4 BE bytes per Trezor's MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH.BUILD) + `UPGRADE`/`SAME VERSION`/`DOWNGRADE!` indicator (from `otp::rollback_floor()` vs manifest's fw_version) + "R-tap = next" hint. **Page 2** — new firmware fingerprint, 8 BIP-39 words in 4-row × 2-col layout, format `"1f abcde 5f fghij"`. The `'f'` discriminator next to each digit distinguishes this page from page 3. **Page 3** — signing-key fingerprint, same layout with `'k'` discriminator. `vendor_pubkey::VENDOR_PK_FPR` is pre-computed at build time so runtime is one `hash_to_word_indices` call. **Page 4** — `Confirm update? / R-hold = YES / L-hold = NO / L-tap = review`. 5-char word truncation safe because BIP-39 English wordlist has unique 4-letter prefixes by spec (so 5 chars always disambiguates). Helpers: `build_version_page` / `build_fingerprint_page(words, disc)` / `build_confirm_prompt_page` + local `format_u8_decimal` (14 lines, kept local to avoid pulling in the EIP-712 cowswap_display module). Updated host test `positive_confirm_commit_calls_trusted_ui_outside_e2e_test` pins the new behavior: each of the four page builders must be referenced AND `rollback_floor()` consulted AND the final `matches!(confirm(&pages), ConfirmResult::Confirmed)` pattern present. Replaces the old `negative_confirm_commit_defaults_to_user_cancel_outside_e2e_test` test. Build paths: `make build-hw-prodtest` clean; production HW path (`stm32u585+dual-se+ui-oled+saes-dhuk+hw-sha256`) clean; e2e-test path clean. 1847/1847 host tests pass. Together with §31a item 1 (channel-ID regression-test) this closes the Trezor-derived hardening review — §31c documents the 4 items rejected after audit with rationale. |
| 2026-05-14 | Phase 2 — host ERC-7730 IR compiler + Merkle DB + xtask + policy enforcement | EF launched Clear Signing on 2026-05-12 (`clearsigning.org`); Phase 1 (`d6beefb`) shipped the on-device `pqsigner-erc7730` crate (IR parser + bundle verifier + binding cross-check). Phase 2 lands the **host build pipeline** so the firmware-pinned `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` is generated from a curated descriptor catalogue that has cleared the ERC-8176 attestation policy at compile time — preserving invariant #5 (no classical signer anywhere). **`dbgen/src/erc7730.rs`** (~1300 lines, pure-Rust): parses ERC-7730 v2 JSON (drops the deprecated `abi` section, derives parameter names from format keys like `"transfer(address _to, uint256 _value)"`); JCS-canonicalises (RFC 8785 subset — integers + strings, the only types the registry uses) to compute the 32-byte `descriptor_hash` baked into IR offset 30..62; enforces `policy.toml` (`min_attesters` / `trusted_attesters` / `allow_unattested_dev_descriptors`); compiles every format into the 134-byte BE IR header + metadata pool + formats table that `pqsigner_erc7730::ir::Erc7730Ir::parse` accepts byte-for-byte; emits one IR per `(chainId, contract)` deployment so multi-chain descriptors (USDC's 6 chains, USDT's 2, WETH's 2) expand to 20 Merkle leaves from 8 source files; computes EIP-712 `domainSeparator` per deployment via the present-field-only typehash rule; interns repeated paths/params via a per-descriptor pool cache; truncates non-ASCII owner/contractName to ≤15 ASCII per the on-device parser's anti-spoof rule. Catalog blob format: 32 B LE header (magic=`"P730"` / version 1 / entry_cnt / ir_pool_off / ir_pool_size / proof_depth / proofs_off), 72 B per entry (chain_id_le / contract / primary_type_hash / context_kind / ir_off / ir_len), IR pool, per-entry Merkle proofs. **Seed corpus** (`secure/data/erc7730/*.json`): 8 self-contained registry pulls from `ethereum/clear-signing-erc7730-registry` (tether-usdt, weth, lido-stETH, opensea-wyvern Wyvern Exchange, circle-usdc-twa/rwa, aave-v3-pool trimmed to supply+withdraw+supplyWithPermit, uniswap-v3-router trimmed to exactInputSingle) → 20 leaves, prod root `0x4b8adbb75193a7fd5fe15581cb17f5d016015a89e6ff8d2f52f58f493a7e8ff3`. `includes` resolution explicitly rejected for Phase 2 (Phase 3 wires the registry-mirror submodule for ERC-2612 permit templates). **`secure/data/erc7730/policy.toml`**: dev mode (`allow_unattested_dev_descriptors = true`) for the hand-pulled seed corpus; CI must gate production on `false` once the registry-mirror submodule lands (same pattern as `e2e-test` / `otp-hardcoded-master-key`). E2E variant (`secure/data/erc7730-e2e/`) with WETH + USDT only, gated to `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` under `#[cfg(feature = "e2e-test")]` (mirror of the existing `SELECTOR_DB_ROOT` pattern). **dbgen restructure**: split into `dbgen/src/lib.rs` (public module exports + record types + loaders + helpers) + `dbgen/src/main.rs` (CLI wrapper) so integration tests in `dbgen/tests/` can reach `dbgen::erc7730::*` directly. **`xtask` subcommand `gen-erc7730-descriptors [--check]`**: re-uses the dbgen library to build prod + e2e in-memory and diff against checked-in artifacts (the binary, the review file, the root constant block in `secure/src/db_roots.rs`); same CI gate shape as `gen-solidity-constants --check`. **Round-trip integration test** (`dbgen/tests/erc7730_roundtrip.rs`, 5 tests): builds the seed corpus, synthesises the wire-format bundle the companion would ship, hands it to `pqsigner_erc7730::bundle::verify_erc7730_bundle` byte-for-byte, then runs `cross_check_contract` / `cross_check_eip712` against the IR's `(chain_id, contract, domain_separator)`. Negative tests cover wrong root, tampered proof, chain_id flip, contract flip. **Makefile**: `make test-unit` now runs `cargo test -p pqsigner-erc7730 --lib` + `cargo test -p dbgen --test erc7730_roundtrip`; new `check-erc7730-descriptors` target wraps `pqsigner-xtask -- gen-erc7730-descriptors --check`, aggregated under `make check-codegen`. **Artifacts emitted by `cargo run -p dbgen`**: `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db.bin` (10,919 B, 20 leaves), `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db_e2e.bin` (1,444 B, 4 leaves), `secure/data/erc7730.review.txt` (vendor-readable per-leaf summary), `secure/src/db_roots.rs` (now also carries `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` cfg-gated for prod/e2e). **All gates green**: `cargo test -p pqsigner-erc7730` 24/24, `cargo test -p dbgen` 15 lib + 5 integration = 20/20, `cargo test -p sphincs-tz-secure --tests` 118/118 (no regression), `cargo build -p pqsigner-erc7730 --target thumbv8m.main-none-eabi` clean, `xtask gen-erc7730-descriptors --check` reports `erc7730: in sync` and `xtask gen-solidity-constants --check` still works. Pool TLV constants for path opcodes (0x10..0x24), format opcodes (0x01..0x0E), and formatter params (0x30..0x3F including `Visibility=0x3F`) are now stable wire constants — the Phase 5 walker MUST not renumber them. Open items intentionally left for later phases: (a) full `includes` resolution for upstream-registry ingestion (Phase 3), (b) widening the EIP-712 selector slot to a full typehash via a discriminator byte (Phase 3+), (c) the `clearsig` Python parity test (`tools/cross_parity_erc7730.py`) — recommended in handoff but punted because the host pipeline is pure Rust and self-validating via the round-trip test, (d) flipping `allow_unattested_dev_descriptors` to `false` once the registry-mirror submodule + real attestation chain land. Files: `pqsigner-erc7730/src/{ir,bundle,binding,walker,abi,lib}.rs` (already shipped in Phase 1, unchanged), `dbgen/{Cargo.toml,src/lib.rs,src/main.rs,src/erc7730.rs,tests/erc7730_roundtrip.rs}`, `xtask/{Cargo.toml,src/main.rs}`, `secure/data/erc7730/{policy.toml,*.json}`, `secure/data/erc7730-e2e/{tether-usdt,weth}.json`, `secure/data/erc7730.review.txt`, `secure/src/db_roots.rs`, `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db{,_e2e}.bin`, `Makefile`, `docs/work-todo.md`. |
| 2026-05-14 | Phase 4 — ERC-7730 display renderer + ERC-8213 fingerprint pages + EIP-712 typed offchain sign completion | Closes the loop between Phase 3's wire format (`5f12728`) and the trusted UI: descriptor-driven pages for contract-context UserOps + offchain typed-data signs, plus an ERC-8213 calldata-digest / EIP-712-final-hash fingerprint page appended to every sign flow so users can cross-check off-device. **`tx-core/src/erc8213.rs`** (new, +8 tests): streaming-Keccak `calldata_digest(data) = keccak256(uint256(len) || data)` and `eip712_final_hash(ds, sh) = keccak256(0x1901 \|\| ds \|\| sh)`. No 4 KiB staging buffer; chained `Keccak256::update` so a 4 KiB calldata blob does not balloon stack. `tx-core/src/lib.rs` + `secure/src/tx/mod.rs` re-export shims keep the call path uniform. **`secure/src/tx/erc7730_render/{mod,params,visibility}.rs`** (new, pure logic, host-test-reachable): `RenderErr::{Reject(&'static str), NoFormat, PageBudget}`; TLV parameter parser for tags 0x30..0x3F (token_path / token / threshold / message / addr_types / addr_sources / date_encoding / enum_ref / decimals / base / prefix / suffix / nested_selector / nested_callee / fallback_label / visibility) including visibility-value-list sub-TLV (Phase 5 forward-compat); `should_render(params, resolved) -> Action::{Render \| Skip \| Reject}` with the locked Phase 4 reading of the 5 Visibility variants: Always/Optional → render, Never → skip-without-walking, IfNotIn → render (no value-list in current `dbgen` wire), MustMatch → `Reject("7730 must-match unsupported")` so the caller falls through to blind-sign rather than silently render a rule it cannot enforce. **`secure/src/tx/display/erc7730/{mod,formatters,intent,calldata_nested}.rs`** (new): `render_erc7730_pages(tx, inner_data, &VerifiedDescriptor, Option<&Erc20Metadata>, &NameResolver) -> Result<Pages, RenderErr>` for contract context; `render_erc7730_eip712_pages(chain_id, &verifying_contract, &primary_type_hash, encoded_data, &VerifiedDescriptor, Option<&Erc20Metadata>, &NameResolver)` for EIP-712 typed-data. 14 `FormatOp` dispatchers (0x01 Raw → 0x0E Encrypted) — Amount uses `write_amount_two_rows` with `params.decimals` (default 18, params.base default "ETH"), TokenAmount matches `params.token_path` / `params.token` against the supplied `Erc20Metadata` for decimals + ticker (falls back to "???" if no match), AddressName via `write_addr_full_or_name`, Date renders UTC `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` (Howard Hinnant civil-from-days), Duration as `Xd Yh Zm Ws`, ChainId via `chain_name(chain_id)`, InteroperableAddressName as ERC-3770 long-form `eip155:<chainId>:0x<addr>` (chain short-name registry is Phase 5+ scope), Encrypted shows `params.fallback_label` only (no FHEVM on-device). Path resolution is intentionally a direct walker (sum FieldIdx → byte-slot offset → 32 B word from `inner_data[4..]`) rather than threading `pqsigner_erc7730::walker::resolve_path` — Phase 3's walker needs an `AbiView` tree and the on-device IR does not yet carry ABI type info; the direct walker is correct for the static-type seed corpus and is replaced by a full AbiView build in Phase 5 if dynamic types land. **`secure/src/tx/display/erc8213.rs`** (new): `Kind::{CalldataDigest, Eip712Final, Raw32, SafeTxHash}([u8;32])` + 2-page `append_fingerprint_page` (banner + 32-byte hash split 4 rows × 8 hex bytes per row — full digest, no truncation, vs the existing 14-hex-truncated `write_calldata_hash_rows`). **`secure/src/tx/display/mod.rs:172-238`** — `pick_sign_pages` grows an `erc7730: Option<&VerifiedDescriptor<'_>>` parameter between `safe_v1` and `erc20`; new rung between Safe-V1 (#3) and the plain-ETH check (#4) returns the ERC-7730 pages on success, surfaces a `ui::show_status("Sign", msg)` banner on `RenderErr::Reject`, and silently falls through on `NoFormat` / `PageBudget` to the existing ladder. `Pages` gains a `push_blank() -> Result<usize, ()>` method so the dynamic-field renderer doesn't pre-allocate. **`secure/src/nsc/cmd_sign_userop.rs:449-501, 791-811`**: `erc7730_verified.as_ref()` flows into `pick_sign_pages`; the Phase 3 `let _ = &erc7730_verified;` sink is deleted; `Kind::CalldataDigest(calldata_digest(inner_data))` fingerprint appended before `confirm`. **`secure/src/nsc/cmd_sign_offchain.rs:263-441`**: the Phase 3 `"phase 4 needed"` bail for `OFFCHAIN_KIND_EIP712_TYPED = 2` becomes the full sign pipeline — compute `final_eip712 = eip712_final_hash(ds, keccak256(typehash \|\| encoded_data))`, derive `wallet_addr = proxy_address(bootstrap_pubkey)`, wrap via `personal_sign_replay_safe_hash(chain_id, &wallet_addr, &final_eip712)` (passes the 32-byte EIP-712 hash AS a personal-sign message — Solady's `ERC1271` PersonalSign branch matches because our `SignatureWrapper` carries no appended data per `aa/src/eip1271.rs:5-8` + `contracts/smart-wallet/src/PQSmartWallet.sol:362-395`; NO new typehash, NO on-chain change), render via the descriptor, append `Kind::Eip712Final(final_eip712)` fingerprint, confirm, then join the shared §10-14 sign path. The PERSONAL_SIGN and RAW32 paths get fingerprint pages too (PersonalSign carries `Kind::CalldataDigest(calldata_digest(payload))` so the user sees a hash they can `cast keccak`; RAW32 carries `Kind::Raw32(hash_to_sign)`). **`secure/src/nsc/cmd_sign_userop_batch.rs:280-405`**: per-tx descriptor routing — `erc7730_for_this_tx = erc7730_verified.as_ref().filter(|v| ptx.to == v.ir.contract)` so a batch's one verified descriptor only renders the inner tx it actually matches; other inner txs use the legacy typed-call / ERC-20 / blind-sign ladder. Per-tx `Kind::CalldataDigest` fingerprint after `wrap_pages_with_batch_banner`, plus a batch-final `Kind::Raw32(keccak256(concat(per_tx_digests)))` appended to `build_final_summary_pages` — a single hash binds the entire bundle so users can cross-check the whole batch off-device. The Phase 3 dead `let _ = &erc7730_verified;` sink at line 282 is deleted. **`dbgen/tests/erc7730_roundtrip.rs`**: new `seed_corpus_param_tlv_blobs_are_well_formed` walks every IR's formats table and validates every field's `param_off` TLV blob from the host-emitter side — confirms `blob_len` matches the sum of inner TLVs, every tag is in 0x30..=0x3F, and fixed-width tags carry the documented payload size (TOKEN=20, THRESHOLD=32, ENUM_REF=2, NESTED_SELECTOR=4, NESTED_CALLEE=20). 8 → **9** integration tests. **Phase 4 architectural decisions documented in `~/.claude/plans/fully-implement-phase-4-nested-puppy.md`**: (1) MustMatch failure → fall-through-to-blind-sign with status banner (conservative reading of "descriptor is invalid for this transaction"); (2) Batch mode → render-the-match + blind-sign-the-rest (multi-descriptor batches are a Phase 5 wire-format expansion); (3) Encrypted (0x0E) → fallback label only, no FHEVM on-device; (4) InteroperableAddressName (0x0D) → long form `eip155:<chainId>:0x<addr>` (chain short-name registry is Phase 5+); (5) EIP-712 typed sign-envelope → reuse `personal_sign_replay_safe_hash` with the 32-byte final hash AS a message (no new typehash); (6) Phase 4 goldens → hand-pinned where present; Cyfrin `clearsig` Python parity is Phase 5; (7) Optional visibility → collapsed to Always for Phase 4 (compact-mode toggle in Phase 5). **Verification status**: `cargo test -p pqsigner-erc7730 --tests` 41/41 (unchanged), `cargo test -p pqsigner-tx-core --tests` 23 → **31** (+8 erc8213), `cargo test -p dbgen --test erc7730_roundtrip` 8 → **9** (+1 TLV-well-formed), `cargo test -p sphincs-tz-secure --tests --no-default-features --features mock-se,debug-log,ui-semihosting` 118 → **137** (+9 params + 5 visibility + 5 deep params tests), thumbv8m cross-build clean for both `--features mock-se,debug-log,ui-semihosting` and `--features dual-se,ui-oled,stm32u585,debug-log`, `xtask gen-erc7730-descriptors --check` reports `erc7730: in sync`, `xtask gen-solidity-constants --check` clean. `make e2e` not re-run interactively this session (QEMU sign latency per the 2026-05-12/13 entries); Phase 3's Scenario 5m still verifies the bundle and now drives the full Phase 4 render path through QEMU when CI runs the smoke. **Punted to Phase 5**: `erc7730-dev-unattested` Cargo feature, registry-mirror submodule + `allow_unattested_dev_descriptors = false` flip, Cyfrin `clearsig` parity scripts (`tools/cross_parity_erc7730.py` / `tools/cross_parity_erc8213.py`), FI-hardened cross-check pairs on every binding via `crate::fi::check_true_into_sentinel`, compact-mode display toggle (uses `Optional` visibility distinction), HW-bench target `make e2e-erc7730-hw`, sign-time benchmark assertion, `docs/erc7730-integration.md` + `docs/erc8213-fingerprints.md` + README/CLAUDE.md updates, `tests/ui_fixtures_erc7730.json` ui-capture goldens, fuzz harness extension for `params::parse` (requires moving `params`/`visibility` into `pqsigner-erc7730` so fuzz can reach them), nested-calldata recursion (Phase 4 stub rejects → falls through to blind-sign; Safe v1 paths use the pre-existing `safe_display` renderer), full `AbiView` build for dynamic types + Uniswap-style deep tuples (Phase 4 ships static-only path-walker, which covers the simple seed-corpus cases like USDT.transfer / WETH.deposit / USDC.transfer). | 2026-05-20 | ERC-7730 `path_off=0` collision fix + `approve(MAX)` "unlimited" rendering + companion docs refresh | Post-merge follow-up on the ERC-7730 PR. The Phase 5 host render tests (`secure/src/display_under_test/erc7730_render_pure_tests.rs`) documented a latent bug: `dbgen::erc7730::Pool::new` started empty, so the first interned path program landed at pool offset 0, colliding with the on-device walker's `path_off == 0` "no path" sentinel (`pqsigner_erc7730::walker::path_bytes`) and the param parser's `param_off == 0` "default params" sentinel (`secure/src/tx/erc7730_render::params::parse`). Every descriptor whose first interned program ended up at offset 0 silently fell through to blind-sign with a `"7730 missing path"` banner — affecting weth.deposit, tether-usdt.transfer/approve, every aave-v3-pool.* and every circle-usdc-*. The bug rode three `#[should_panic(expected = "7730 missing path")]` markers in the host render tests so the tree stayed green. **Fix** (PR #2, commit `eef09386`): `Pool::new` now pushes a 1-byte `0xFF` filler — matches the convention already documented in `walker::tests::path_bytes_reads_prefix`. Catalog IR pool grows by 1 byte per descriptor. **Root rotation**: prod `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` `0x4b8a…8ff3` → `0x650d…dc19`; e2e `0x4324…c15f` → `0xcef0…9934`. Companion blob: `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db.bin` 10919 → 10939 B, `_e2e.bin` 1444 → 1448 B. **Bonus UX**: `render_token_amount` now honors `params.threshold` — when on-chain `value >= threshold` the renderer writes `"unlimited <ticker>"` on row 1 instead of running the digit formatter. ERC-20 `approve(uint256.MAX)` against USDT now displays `"unlimited USDT"` instead of the previous `"!AMOUNT OVERFLOW"` fallback. BE byte-array `>=` matches numeric u256 comparison so the check is a direct array compare. **No wire format change** — descriptor's existing `threshold` TLV (tag `0x32`) drives this; companions don't need to change anything beyond refreshing the blob. **Test surface**: three `#[should_panic]` markers removed; the USDT-transfer / USDT-approve-unlimited / WETH-deposit tests now pass real assertions. `positive_usdt_approve_unlimited_renders_approve_intent` additionally pins "USDT" appearing next to "unlimited" and asserts no `AMOUNT OVERFLOW` leak through the threshold short-circuit. `positive_weth_deposit_pulls_value_from_envelope` relaxes the amount assertion to accept either single-row "0.5" or `write_amount_two_rows`'s split "0" / ".5 ETH" rendering. 1890/1890 secure host tests + 41 erc7730 + 27 tx-core --tests + 5 proto + 9 dbgen roundtrip pass; thumbv8m cross-build clean on `mock-se,debug-log,ui-semihosting` and `dual-se,ui-oled,stm32u585,debug-log` profiles; `xtask gen-erc7730-descriptors --check` in sync. **Docs refresh**: `docs/companion-erc7730-implementation-guide.md` §7.2 (expected display now reads `"unlimited USDT"`), §10 (current pinned roots table + catalog byte counts), §12.1 (rewritten from known-bug to "fixed — companion must refresh blob + re-pin root"); `docs/erc7730-integration.md` (pool offset 0 reservation note + `path_off`/`param_off` sentinel semantics inline + TokenAmount threshold behaviour). Files: `dbgen/src/erc7730.rs`, `secure/src/tx/display/erc7730/formatters.rs`, `secure/src/display_under_test/erc7730_render_pure_tests.rs`, `secure/data/erc7730.review.txt`, `secure/src/db_roots.rs`, `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db{,_e2e}.bin`, `docs/companion-erc7730-implementation-guide.md`, `docs/erc7730-integration.md`, `docs/work-todo.md`. |

| 2026-05-20 | §18 P1 — entry jitter at the PIN gate (random delays before security-critical ops) | Added `crate::fi::wait_random()` at the entry of `nsc::gated_unlock` and `cmd_request_unlock::verify_pin_with_chip`. Advisor-scoped to ONLY these two — not the sign path (F-16's 43!×13! WOTS/FORS shuffle already jitters the secret-dependent timeline) and not an always-on RDI timer like Trezor's (`systimer`-driven `rdi_handler` burning random cycles in an ISR would land mid-USB-bulk-transfer / mid-SE050-T=1' / mid-OPTIGA-Shielded-handshake; Trezor's own `rdi_disabled = sectrue` default + enable/disable scaffolding exist because they hit exactly those intermittent-stall bugs — not worth the risk before on-silicon SCA shows the gap matters). The PIN gate is the right target: linear from external trigger (USB `CMD_REQUEST_UNLOCK` dispatch / boot-unlock / PendSV re-unlock) straight to the F-15 sentinel with no internal shuffle, so a Masaryk-thesis-class profiled single-fault attacker (~76% on STM32U5) lands a glitch at a FIXED offset from the trigger. Entry jitter desyncs that offset by 0..255 `wait_random_loop` iterations ≈ 0..19 µs at 160 MHz. **Honest threat bound documented in-source + work-todo**: meaningful window against an uncalibrated single-shot attacker; does NOT defeat a profile-then-attack rig with multi-attempt statistical recovery — the F-15 sentinel-encoded gate + F-17 rate limiter stay load-bearing. **Assembly-verified** on the linked release ELF (`arm-none-eabi-objdump -d`): a real `bl c00599e <...fi11wait_random>` exists in both functions, so a glitch that skips the call skips only the jitter, not the gate (no LLVM prologue-folding — `wait_random` and `wait_random_loop` are both `#[inline(never)]`). Two source-grep regression tests (`positive_gated_unlock_has_entry_jitter_before_the_gate`, `positive_verify_pin_with_chip_has_entry_jitter_before_gated_unlock`) pin that the entry `wait_random()` PRECEDES the gate it desyncs (before first `pin_attempts_read` / before the `gated_unlock` call) — a refactor that moved the jitter after the gate would trail the thing it's meant to protect and fail loudly. Platform-agnostic (outside the `#[cfg(stm32u585)]` block), no-op under e2e-test so test timings don't drift. 1892/1892 host tests pass; stm32u585+dual-se+ui-oled+dev-testkey release + QEMU mock-se+ui-noop both build clean. RDI-timer path stays open as a follow-up. |
| 2026-05-20 | §18 SHAKE-vs-SHA2 #2 — masked-SHA2 cost bench harness | New `masked-sha2` workspace crate (first-order 2-share boolean-masked building blocks: ISW masked AND `sec_and` + Kogge-Stone secure boolean adder `sec_add`, generic over an RNG closure, pure no_std) + `secure/src/bench_masked_sha.rs` DWT-timed bench behind the `bench-masked-sha` feature + `make bench-masked-sha-hw` target. Advisor-reviewed approach: measure the two cost-dominating masked gates and PROJECT the per-block masked-SHA-256 cost from SHA-256's fixed structure (ADDS_PER_BLOCK=600, ANDS_PER_BLOCK=320) rather than building a full (easy-to-get-subtly-wrong) masked SHA-256. The projection is algebra, not guesswork, because SHA-256 is a fixed-structure standard. 7 host correctness oracles prove the gates correct (sec_and==AND / sec_add==wrapping_add over 50k random inputs each + full-ripple carry edge cases + Ch/Maj composition over 20k) — the Kogge-Stone indexing is verified, not assumed. Bench measures one-compression (55-byte single-block input) of HASH peripheral + sha2 software baselines, plus sec_and/sec_add BOTH ways: TRNG-inline (conservative, masks straight from hardware TRNG) and rand-pre-drawn (pure gate logic ≈ DRBG-fed production lower bound). Prints projected masked-block cycles/µs + slowdown ratios. Decision rule (advisor): masked/HW ≲3× → SHA2 stays viable + SHAKE drops priority; ≳5-10× → SHAKE arm worth building. Build profile `bench-masked-sha,debug-log,ui-noop,e2e-test,mock-se` (e2e-test escapes the production fence + permits mock-se; bench short-circuits at boot before any SE access). masked-sha2 dep is `optional = true` / `dep:` gated so default + production builds don't link it. 7/7 masked-sha2 host tests pass; 1892/1892 secure host tests unaffected; bench firmware + normal hardware build paths both clean. NOT yet run on silicon — harness is ready, user runs `make bench-masked-sha-hw` on the bench and records the numbers in §18. |
| 2026-05-20 | §18 SHAKE-vs-SHA2 #2 — masked-SHA2 cost MEASURED on silicon | Ran `make bench-masked-sha-hw` on STM32U585 @ 160 MHz (20k iters/measurement). HASH peripheral 1-block = 485 cyc; sha2 software = 4265 cyc; TRNG word = 214 cyc; sec_and = 22/214 cyc (gate/TRNG-inline); sec_add = 213/2147 cyc. Projected masked SHA-256 block = 134,840 cyc (~842 µs) DRBG-floor / 1,356,680 cyc (~8.5 ms) raw-TRNG → ~278× to ~2797× the HASH peripheral. VERDICT: masking the full SHA-2 hot path is infeasible (1-2 orders past the 5-10× decision line). Takeaways: (1) TRNG draw dominates → a DRBG is mandatory for any masking (~10× win); (2) de-prioritizes the SHAKE migration — even ~5× cheaper Keccak masking lands at ~56×, still infeasible, and there's no SHAKE peripheral; (3) the viable SCA path is "mask only the secret-touching hash subset (sk_seed PRF + first hash of each WOTS/FORS secret chain), not everything" + existing F-16 shuffle/consumption-mask/RDI, driven by sca-trigger on-silicon leakage measurement. Next step shifts from "build the SHAKE #3 benchmark" to "count secret-touching hashes/sign + leakage-characterize just those." Bench infra fix during the run: dropped debug-log from the make target (hw::rng::fill secure_log!s per call → semihosting flood under the TRNG-heavy bench; results print via unconditional hprintln! which works under probe-rs regardless). |
| 2026-05-20 | §18 SCA step (a) — secret-touching hash count: 70,754/sign (corrects my ~200 by 350×) | Added `hash-counters` feature to sphincs-c10 + `tests/hash_counts.rs`; one C10 sign = 44,118 wots_secret + 26,636 fors_secret = 70,754 sk_seed-touching PRF calls (13.1% of 539,616 total). My prior-turn "~200, mask the subset, ~1 s" was WRONG by ~350× — SLH-DSA regenerates every leaf of every signed subtree (auth path) + every FORS leaf, each a PRF(sk_seed, ADRS). CONSEQUENCE: masking the subset ≈ 70,754 × 134,840 cyc ≈ ~60 s/sign — infeasible, same wall as masking everything. (c) "mask the subset" is DEAD at every granularity. Midstate caching can't rescue it (sk_seed is a 32-byte non-block-aligned prefix; rounds 8..63 still secret-derived, ~14% saving only). F-16 shuffle is only partial (sk_seed at same intra-call offset → horizontal averaging ignores order). REFRAMED forward path: (b) is now a standalone residual-characterization measurement (does the PRF leak sk_seed on this silicon with F-16+consumption-mask+noise? → TVLA max|t| via sca-trigger; parked with RDI until an SCA-capture bench session) — NOT a precursor to (c). v2 fix recorded: PRF restructure padding sk_seed to a full 64-byte first block → 1 masked compression/sign instead of 70,754, but it re-bases all keys/addresses (violates launch invariant #6) so it's a v2/KDF-revision item. Instrumentation is feature-gated + zero-cost off; default tests + production HW build unaffected. Commit 354af1b. |
| 2026-05-20 | §12 closed — signature parameter security level decision recorded | The "SLH-DSA-128f → 192f migration" entry was stale: it predated the all-C10 cutover and referenced a `slh-dsa` crate / `Sha2_128f`/`Sha2_192f` type params / 17,088-35,664-byte sigs that no longer exist (verified: zero matches in tree). Production signer is the custom `sphincs-c10` (SIGNATURE_LEN=4008, N=16 → 128-bit / NIST Category 1). Rewrote §12 from a migration TODO into a recorded DECISION: C10/128-bit is the production target, chosen for on-chain verifier feasibility (4008-byte sig keeps the Yul SPHINCsC10Asm per-validateUserOp gas viable). A 192-bit (Cat-3) variant would ~2× sig/gas/sign-time + need a new verifier + re-base every CREATE2 address (breaks launch invariant #6 → launch/KDF-boundary only). 128-bit hash-based security is the standard defensible choice (no quantum lever beyond Grover, which SLH-DSA is designed against). No code change beyond fixing a stale `proto/src/lib.rs::CMD_SIGN_BOOTSTRAP` docstring (reserved/non-dispatched legacy CMD 10) that still claimed a "17,088-byte SLH-DSA signature" — corrected to the C10 4008-byte reality with a historical note. |

| 2026-05-20 | §32 timing-channel measured on silicon — flips the drift-fix decision | `make duress-timing-hw` (probe extended to DWT-time the SE verifies at production speed, no debug-log). RESULT: one OPTIGA HMAC verify ~160 ms (157-167, n=15), one SE050 UserID verify ~103 ms (102.6-105.4), skip-vs-run "extra real verify" delta ~263 ms with ~6% jitter. The advisor + I had assumed ~10-50 ms/verify; the verifies are 2-3× slower (OPTIGA shielded multi-transaction + SE050 SCP03 round-trips). Unlock is SE-bound (the ~1-3 s C10 keygen runs later on first sign/address, NOT during unlock), so 263 ms is a large, low-jitter, observable fraction of unlock → TIMING UNIFORMITY IS LOAD-BEARING. The hoped-for "just skip the real verify on duress, problem dissolves" path is OFF the table. Narrows the drift fix to two real options: (A) run both verifies (perfect timing) + OR'd-AC E120 reset + SE050 reduced to informational → two-of-three lockstep; (B) skip the real verify on duress + busy-wait a calibrated ~263 ms ± jitter → NO drift, full three-way lockstep preserved, simpler, ~±10 ms timing residual. Leaning B (preserves more security properties + simpler); decision pending. New `make duress-timing-hw` target + `Se050::probe_auth_existing_userid` + timing block in the duress-probe e2e (hprintln output so it prints with debug-log OFF). |
| 2026-05-20 | §32 option B chosen + plain-vs-auto-state pad delta measured | DECIDED option B (skip the real verify on a duress match → no counter drift → full three-way lockstep preserved) after the spec check confirmed SE050's MAX_ATTEMPTS is creation-only with no cross-credential counter-reset (so A is forced to two-of-three). Minimization: pad the skipped real verify with a REAL second duress-credential verify (natural jitter, harmless counter) instead of a NOP busy-wait. Measured the OPTIGA plain-vs-auto-state delta on silicon (new `probe_hmac_auth_luc_at` mirrors the production F1D0 auto-state/LUC verify): F1D8 plain ~160 ms vs F1D0 auto-state ~149 ms → ~10.4 ms (auto-state faster, leaner Trezor nonce shape). SE050 side is a perfect match (~103 ms both). So a simple plain pad leaves a ~11 ms consistent residual (24× down from the raw 263 ms, practically unexploitable for coercion but consistent); a matched-LUC duress credential (F1D8 bound to its own unenforced LUC counter → auto-state path) nulls it to ~0 for the cost of one extra counter object. Finishing choice (simple-plain vs matched-LUC) pending. All measurement is probe-only/feature-gated; no production change yet. |

| 2026-05-20 | §18 SHAKE-vs-SHA2 architectural decision — CLOSED (stay on SHA-2) | Formally closed the long-deferred parameter-set fork (was the last open P1 in §18). Decision: stay on SHA-2, SHAKE rejected. Rationale: (1) masking infeasible on either hash — on-silicon masked-SHA2 = 278–2800× the HASH peripheral, and 70,754 secret PRF calls/sign make masking even the secret subset ≈ 60 s/sign; (2) STM32U5 HASH peripheral accelerates SHA-1/SHA-2 only, NOT SHA-3/Keccak/SHAKE (RM0456) — so SHAKE forfeits the 485-cyc/block HW path and is a net slowdown even unmasked, and masked-SHAKE is still ~70k software permutations/sign; (3) the only pro-SHAKE argument (Fluhrer ePrint 2024/500 "1.7× PRF-tree") is unverifiable/implausible; (4) a parameter switch re-derives every key, moving the CREATE2 address (frozen-wallet invariant #6) → launch-breaking. SCA posture stays SHA-2 + the landed F-13/F-16/F-17 + consumption-mask + multi-source OptRand; the v2 PRF restructure (pad sk_seed to a full first block) is the SHA-2-preserving KDF-revision escape hatch if step-(b) leakage measurement ever shows an exploitable leak. No code change — decision + doc closure (user-confirmed conclusion from the masked-sha2 silicon round). |
| 2026-05-20 | Invariant #4 GTZC/TZSC enforcement — SILICON-VALIDATED | `make gtzc-enforcement-hw` PASSED on real B-U585I-IOT02A. NS probed all 7 peripherals the secure boot marks SECURE in `sau.rs` (I2C1@0x40005400, I2C2@0x40005800, AES@0x420c0000, HASH@0x420c0400, RNG@0x420c0800, PKA@0x420c2000, SAES@0x420c0c00) via their NS-aliased control registers; every access RAZ-returned 0x00000000 AND raised the GTZC violation IRQ, bumping `hw::tzic::VIOLATION_COUNT` by exactly 1 per probe (baseline 0 → final 7, delta 7, expected 7) → `[NS][gtzc] === PASS ===`. Proves the `GTZC1_TZSC_SECCFGR{1,3}` fix actually enforces on silicon (the CRIT-4 regression's designed-fix was QEMU-only until now). Non-destructive (mock-se, no SE writes, no PIN attempts); all changes runtime/reversible (no RDP2/WRP burn; TZEN/SECWM already set). Closes the "NS read of RNG_DR bus-faults" half of the CLAUDE.md TZSC caveat. Remaining: assert "USB still enumerates" under the same GTZC config (OTG stays NS — covered in practice by USB HID builds), + TAMP (GTZC2) follow-up. |
| 2026-05-20 | USB-C device enumeration fixed + validated on silicon | While closing the TZSC caveat's "USB still enumerates" half, found the device wouldn't enumerate over USB-C-to-USB-C and root-caused two real `secure/src/hw/usb_hw.rs::init_ucpd` register bugs (confirmed vs ST's authoritative LL driver + CMSIS): (1) it set `UCPD_CR.CC1TCDIS/CC2TCDIS=1` thinking they disable dead-battery, but those are the Type-C voltage *detector* disables (`LL_UCPD_TypeCDetectionCC1Disable()=SET_BIT(CC1TCDIS)`) → blinded the CC detectors → `UCPD_SR.TYPEC_VSTATE` stuck at 0; (2) it never disabled the actual dead-battery, which on STM32U5 is `PWR_UCPDR.UCPD_DBDIS` bit 0 (`LL_PWR_DisableUCPDDeadBattery()`), leaving the dead-battery Rd in parallel with the UCPD Rd → shifted CC voltage. Fix: leave detectors enabled (drop CCxTCDIS) + `PWR->UCPDR |= UCPD_DBDIS`. The CC pins (PA15/PB15) were CORRECT (verified vs Zephyr hal_stm32 STM32U585AII pinctrl; the "PB6/PB7" code comments were a wrong cross-family value). Validated on real B-U585I: enumerates as `1209:7051 "Generic PQSigner OS"` in ~1 s over USB-C (5V jumper on 5V_UCPD). Methodology gotcha that cost time: `probe-rs reset` halts the core on this setup (UCPD_CR read back 0 = unconfigured = firmware never ran) — runtime/USB tests must use `probe-rs run` or a power-cycle. Commit b325dd8. Ship cable is USB-C↔USB-C, so this was a real launch blocker. |
| 2026-05-20 | Warm-reset follow-up — CHARACTERIZED (firmware re-init OK; USB-C-power-bootstrap edge only) | Localized the warm-reset non-recovery. Test: with the board on STABLE power (5V_STLK jumper) + USB-C connected, an NRST press → device drops → RE-ENUMERATES (`1209:7051`, fresh device number) in ~10 s. So **firmware re-init after a reset works whenever power is held — it is NOT a firmware bug.** The earlier 5V_UCPD non-recovery is purely the USB-C-*sole-power* re-bootstrap: an NRST cuts VBUS → board loses power, and that specific transient gets stuck (a clean unplug/replug = full POR → dead-battery → boot always works). Implication for the shipped (USB-C-only-powered) device: cold-plug ✅, brownout (full power loss → POR → dead-battery) should recover, watchdog reset (host holds VBUS through the brief reset → firmware re-runs) should recover (matches the 5V_STLK+NRST result); only the artificial "NRST while USB-C-sole-powered" transient got stuck, recoverable by replug. Severity downgraded to LOW. Remaining to 100%-confirm watchdog/brownout recovery: an LA capture of VBUS+CC1/CC2 through a reset cycle — DEFERRED (probing the CC lines is involved; replug always recovers). The DBDIS-persists-across-NRST hypothesis was disproven (PWR_UCPDR resets to 0). |
| 2026-05-20 | USB-C standalone enumeration CONFIRMED + warm-reset follow-up | Definitive standalone validation: with the board self-powered from USB-C (5V_UCPD jumper) and NO debugger attached, an unplug/replug of the USB-C-to-USB-C cable cold-starts the board and enumerates it fresh as `1209:7051 "Generic PQSigner OS"` (new device number each plug) — the exact shipped scenario. So the init_ucpd fix (commit b325dd8) works end-to-end on real hardware. **Follow-up (robustness, non-blocking):** a WARM reset (NRST button / watchdog / brownout) *while* USB-C-powered did NOT re-enumerate until a physical replug — likely because `PWR_UCPDR.UCPD_DBDIS` (the dead-battery disable we set in firmware) persists across NRST, so post-warm-reset there's no passive dead-battery Rd to re-bootstrap VBUS (and the board, powered only by USB-C, can't present UCPD Rd until it has power → deadlock until unplug). Recoverable by unplug/replug. Harden by re-enabling dead-battery on the reset path (or confirm DBDIS reset-domain behavior); matters for brownout/watchdog resilience on a device whose only power is the USB-C cable. Tracked for a future session. |
