| 2026-05-28 | FSBL-rooted firmware fingerprint (manufacturer-trust elimination) | Closed the self-attested-measurement gap: the 8-BIP-39-word "OS Fingerprint" is now rendered by the WRP1A-locked FSBL *before* it branches into the slot, so a malicious update can no longer forge the words on the OLED. Implementation: (1) `bip39` crate gains a build-script-generated `WORDLIST_PREFIX5: [[u8; 5]; 2048]` (~10 KB) and a `firmware_fingerprint_lines(digest) → [[u8; 16]; 4]` pure function shared between FSBL and `secure/src/measured_boot.rs`; the heavy 18 KB `WORDLIST_FLAT` / `Mnemonic` / PBKDF2 / CT exact-lookup surface is now gated behind the `full-wordlist` feature (on by default for secure-world, off for FSBL). (2) `fsbl/src/verify.rs::verify_images` returns `Option<[u8; 32]>` (the verified secure-image digest) so the FSBL render path is driven by the same trusted bytes FSBL just SHA-256'd. (3) New `fsbl/src/oled.rs` + `glyphs.rs` ports the SSD1306 + I2C1 driver + the 5×8 ASCII font (vendored from `secure/assets/font_5x8.raw`) without any embedded-graphics / secure_log / hw::mmio dep — ~3.5 KB compiled. (4) `fsbl/src/main.rs` calls `render::render_fingerprint(&secure_digest)` after slot selection and before `branch::into_slot`. FSBL release footprint = 30,888 / 32 KB (1,880 B headroom). Tests: 12 in `bip39/tests/prefix5_roundtrip.rs` (prefix table integrity + fingerprint_lines layout pins), 1 in `fsbl-tests/tests/footprint.rs` (CI gate ≤ 32 KB), 6 in `fsbl-tests/tests/source_invariants.rs` (verify_images returns digest, FSBL renders before branch, no-OLED graceful fallback, measured_boot self-attests, render uses bip39's pure fn), 3 in `fwmeasure/tests/byte_identity.rs` (host fwmeasure output matches FSBL render for same digest), 3 `#[ignore]`d QEMU integration placeholders documenting future empirical proofs. Docs: new `docs/measured-boot.md` (threat model + trust chain + user workflow), updates to `docs/firmware-update.md`, `docs/reproducible-builds.md`, `CLAUDE.md` Lifecycle. Threat model: defends post-provisioning (any update can't fake the words); does NOT defend factory-time integrity (malicious vendor at provisioning) — the user must compare the very first boot's words to `./measure.sh` to establish the baseline. |
| 2026-05-28 | USB-C warm-reset re-enumeration SOLVED in firmware (cc_open_then_reset) | The warm-reset non-recovery (a firmware `sys_reset` over USB-C didn't re-enumerate without a physical replug, because VBUS stays asserted by the host so Linux's typec layer keeps the port bound) is now fixed in firmware. Root insight: force a **real Type-C detach** by holding CC fully open long enough (~1.5 s, past tCCDebounce) before the reset — then the post-reset dead-battery Rd re-engages and the host sees a fresh attach. To open CC you must drop BOTH device-side Rd sources (UCPD `CCENABLE`+`ANAMODE` cleared AND `PWR_UCPDR.UCPD_DBDIS=1`) while leaving the TCPP03 EN (PB5) HIGH — driving PB5 low is the trap (it puts the TCPP03 in dead-battery mode = Rd presented, opposite of open; that's why the earlier 200 ms + PB5-low attempts failed). New `hw::usb_hw::cc_open_then_reset()`; wired into `cmd_fw_commit` (reboot into new firmware) + `cmd_fw_begin::arm_wipe_and_reset` (reboot into wiped device). `hw::tzic` stays on the fast `soft_disconnect_then_reset` (IRQ-context). HW-PROVEN via the `fwup-transport-hw-iwdg` wipe-trigger: kernel dmesg logged `USB disconnect` → `new full-speed USB device number N` (1209:7051) re-attach with NO replug, device stable after; observed across multiple runs (device 9→10→11). Latency ≈ 20-25 s (dominated by device boot in the e2e build; production boot is faster). Committed on the dmesg/usbwatch evidence — a single clean automated full-test PASS was rig-limited (the bench USB transport degraded into a flaky heavy-BEGIN hang mid-session, proven NOT cc_open-related: the cc_open-less baseline hangs identically). Re-run `make fwup-transport-hw-iwdg` on a healthy rig for the green checkmark. |
| 2026-05-28 | Evaluated Trezor no-reset FW handoff — REJECTED, we're already at parity | Prompted by the #26 USB-C warm-reset work ("is Trezor's branch-not-reset way better?"). Read `/home/nicola/repos/trezor-firmware`: on **STM32U5** Trezor's firmware→bootloader transition is `NVIC_SystemReset()` (`bootutils.c:161`); only the bootloader→firmware leg is a `jump_to_next_stage()` branch (`bootloader/main.c:551`); the direct `jump_to_vectbl` firmware→verifier path is `#elif STM32F4`-only (they reset, not warm-branch, across the firmware↔verifier boundary on U5 — TrustZone state makes a warm branch unsafe). Their UCPD code is just `HAL_PWREx_DisableUCPDDeadBattery()` (`startup_init.c:259`, == our `UCPD_DBDIS`) with no CCENABLE/ANAMODE/TCPP03 → passive fixed-Rd CC, vs our UCPD-sink + TCPP03. **We already match the U5 pattern**: commit → `sys_reset` → FSBL → branch into the new A/B slot; both re-verify signature every boot. So it's security PARITY, not "we beat Trezor." The only "save-the-reset" variant — a runtime direct slot-A→slot-B branch — was rejected because it bypasses the FSBL per-boot re-verify + FSBL-rooted measured-boot fingerprint (commit `7d0ec47`), i.e. strictly less secure than our current design. The warm-reset re-enumeration gap was purely CC hardware and is already solved by `cc_open_then_reset` (#26). Net: no architectural change. See memory `trezor-fw-handoff-parity`. |
| 2026-05-28 | §18 RDI timer — RE-SCOPED to NOT-MOTIVATED (emulation measurement) | The §18 "always-on RDI" item was gated on first measuring the three non-sign secret CPU paths. Completed the emulation half in `tools/sca/` (rainbow/lascar `mem_address` TVLA, 600 traces, fresh on current tree): AES-GCM entropy wrap (`sca_aesgcm_wrap`), SAES-CMAC(DHUK) KDF wrapper (`sca_saes_cmac`/`sca_saes_kdf_one_block` ×4), and a NEW dual-SE XOR-reconstruction probe (`sca_dual_se_xor`, mirror of `dual_se.rs::xor_32`, added to `kdf_target` + `leakage_seed_derivation.py`) — **all `max\|t\| = 0.00` → flat**. Two facts retire RDI for these paths: (1) the address channel (the only multi-trace channel emulation can test) is already flat on all three; (2) the residual is a value-channel leak that is *single-trace* (boot-once ops, no chosen-input averaging surface), and RDI misaligns *many* traces — it does nothing against single-trace SPA/template. Same disqualifier kills the cheaper per-op `wait_random()` middle step (same mechanism). Consistent with the framework's record: every real single-trace leak (F-22 wordlist, F-24 display, F-23 recovery) was fixed with constant-time code, never RDI. Decision: do NOT build the RDI subsystem. On-silicon value-channel confirmation on these three paths stays tracked under §18b (`sca-trigger`); if it ever shows an exploitable leak, the fix is constant-time/masked code on that op, not a global timer fighting the NS-world USB stack. Ticked §18 RDI `[x]` RE-SCOPED. |
| 2026-05-28 | SE050 silicon-findings closure — A1/A2/A3 documented + harness fixes applied | First on-silicon stress run (commit 67f3bfdb, 6 PASS / 11 FAIL) surfaced three real chip behaviors and four pre-existing harness bugs. None breach the confidentiality invariant — all failures are DoS or test-wrapper assumption breakage; see `docs/se050-silicon-findings.md`. **A1**: `ReadObjectAttributes` is NOT policy-gate-independent on B-U585I-IOT02A — returns SW=0x6986 on a user-PIN-gated UserID with the standard two-entry policy. Doc retracted at `secure/src/se050/mod.rs:485-510`; production `pin_attempt_count_raw` returns `None` and the boot-time reconcile silently skips the SE050 leg (OPTIGA + MCU page-124 still reconcile). **A2**: Failed `write_userid` UPDATE delete-then-creates; chip refuses the create but the delete is already through — any caller with valid SCP03 can DoS-destroy a UserID via one APDU without satisfying any policy. Accepted under the confidentiality-only threat model (BIP-39 backup is the recovery path); `audit_admin_cannot_rotate_user_pin` rewritten to assert silicon truth (refused + destroyed + attacker PIN unusable). **A3**: Failed session ops leave the chip in a session-pending state; subsequent non-session APDUs (`check_exists`, attribute reads) return SW=0x6982 until `Se050::reinit()`. Mitigated in `StressCtx::open_user_session` (reinit-on-failed-verify) + inline in `userid_no_admin_delete`. **S-7d closure**: `apdu.rs::verify_session` now maps both SW=0x6986 and SW=0x6982 to `AuthMethodBlocked` (silicon-observed on the 4th wrong PIN against `max_attempts=3` UserID); replaces the documented-by-deduction prediction. **Harness fixes**: `object_extended_lc_boundary` payload cap dropped 1024→960 B (fits inside the 1024-B ApduBuf with TLV overhead), unchaining cascade failures in `scp03_wtx_endurance` and `trng_quality_basic`. PIN-counter tests P1/P2/P4/P5 reworked: P1 + P2 use behavioral burn-arithmetic inference instead of attribute-read snapshots; P4 repurposed and renamed `pin_attribute_read_refused_on_user_userid` as an A1 silicon regression validator; P5 drops the `(0,0)` marker assertions and relies on the 100-burn loop + a post-burn correct-PIN sanity. Re-run pending: `make se050-stress-destructive` to confirm 17 PASS. |
| 2026-06-10 | CowSwap ZK clear-signing amount-overflow forgery FIXED (circuit + native, both sides) | LLM-found, PoC-verified critical soundness bug (`docs/VULN-cowswap-zk-amount-overflow.md`): `FormatTrimmedAmount` (`circuits/lib/format.circom`) computed `scaled <== raw_amount * scale_factor` in the BLS12-381 scalar field with `raw_amount` bounded only to `< 2²⁵⁴`; the product reached ~2³¹⁴ and wrapped r, so a malicious companion could produce a VALID Groth16 proof (honest circuit + real pinned VK — an under-constrained-circuit forgery, not a verifier/toxic-waste bug) where the trusted UI shows `0.2000 USDC` while the signed `sellAmount` is ~2.6e70 USDC, draining the wallet. **Fixed both sides:** (1) circuit — `Num2Bits(190)` range-check on `raw_amount` before the multiply in BOTH `FormatTrimmedAmount` and `circuits/aave_v3/formatting.circom :: FormatAmount` (`2¹⁹⁰·10¹⁸ < r` over ℤ ⟹ no wrap; lossless, max displayable ≈ 2⁹³); (2) firmware — `amount_within_field_safe_bound()` / `RAW_AMOUNT_FIELD_SAFE_BITS=190` in `secure/src/tx/eip712/cowswap/mod.rs`, called in `verify.rs` step 1b to reject canonical sell/buyAmount ≥ 2¹⁹⁰ before trusting the ZK readable — closes the class on-device even against a future circuit regression. **Regenerated (cowswap):** rebuilt the cowswap_eip712_order zkey/VK with **pot16** (circuit is 17.7k constraints — exceeds the pot14 the repo pins; even the pre-fix circuit did, so the committed zkey was always built out-of-band with pot16), re-pinned `circuit_final.zkey` + `secure/data/vks/cowswap_eip712_order.vk.bin` (`sha256 e0de34d8…`), `cargo run -p dbgen` rotated `VK_DB_ROOT` `0086…`→`3cda5293…` (+ `nonsecure/src/vk_db.bin`); `ERC20_POSEIDON_ROOT` unchanged. Fresh proof verifies end-to-end against the new VK. Fixed the stale `gen_cowswap_eip712_e2e_vector.js` (was missing the v3.1 `is_sub_precision` inputs, 415 vs 417). **Tests:** native `extra_tests.rs` (PoC 254-bit amount rejected, 2¹⁹⁰ boundary, 10²⁸ max-displayable accepted) + circuit-level `docs/cowswap-zk-poc/run_overflow_negative_test.sh` (forged witness fails witness-gen, benign passes). 2007/2008 secure host tests pass (the 1 failure — `usb_hw … negative_usb_hw_raw_volatile_only…` — is pre-existing and unrelated). **Ship-blocker tracked:** aave `FormatAmount` source is fixed but its VK was NOT regenerated (would invalidate the upstream-ZKlarity proof in the host Groth16 tests; needs the `../zk_clear_signing` witness + `tools/export_zk_constants.js`); regenerate the aave VK against the fixed circuit before production. Files: `circuits/lib/format.circom`, `circuits/aave_v3/formatting.circom`, `circuits/test/format_trimmed_overflow_test.circom`, `circuits/scripts/gen_cowswap_eip712_e2e_vector.js`, `circuits/circuits.json`, `circuits/cowswap/eip712_order/circuit_final.zkey`, `secure/src/tx/eip712/cowswap/{mod,verify,extra_tests}.rs`, `secure/data/vks/cowswap_eip712_order.vk.bin`, `secure/src/db_roots.rs`, `nonsecure/src/vk_db.bin`, `docs/VULN-cowswap-zk-amount-overflow.md`, `docs/cowswap-zk-poc/`. |

(1) Regenerated the Aave VK against the now-fixed `formatting.circom` (`build_vks.sh aave_v3_pool`, 6538 constraints, fits pot14, new vk.bin sha `927bc1b7…`); re-pinned `circuit_final.zkey`. | 2026-06-10 | ERC-7730 walker slot-confusion clear-signing forgery FIXED (`docs/VULN-erc7730-walker-slot-confusion.md`) | LLM-found, proof-test-verified trusted-display divergence: the on-device calldata path walker computed a head-word slot by SUMMING logical field ordinals × 32, correct only when every preceding ABI field is one 32-byte word. A field preceded by a multi-word static type (fixed array `T[N]`, non-leading static tuple) resolved to a DIFFERENT head word than the EVM decodes → OLED shows a benign recipient/amount while the contract executes on the attacker's, no fault injection, no warning banner. Latent in the current single-word corpus (masked by luck; Uniswap `exactInputSingle` tuple is leading + single-word), structurally reachable the moment a normal `T[N]`/non-leading-tuple descriptor is curated. **Fixed at the source + fenced so the class cannot silently recur (4 closures):** (1) `dbgen::erc7730::compile_structured_contract_path` (new) emits `FieldIdx` ops whose summed args equal the field's true ABI **head-word** offset via a new recursive `static_head_words` width helper (`T[N]`=N words, static tuple=Σ members, dynamic=1 offset word); the on-device walker is unchanged — its sum is now correct by construction. Context-aware: EIP-712 `encodeData` (one word/member) and `@`/`$` roots keep the prior ordinal/keccak-discriminator encoding — only the `#` contract-calldata root uses ABI widths, so every existing descriptor (incl. the Uniswap nested tuple) compiles byte-identically. (2) dbgen **refuses to pin** a hazardous descriptor — hard error on array index/slice, dynamic-tuple descent, dynamic or multi-word-static terminal field, or a name absent from the signature (keccak fallback retained ONLY for `@`/`$` envelope discriminators). (3) IR `SCHEMA_VER` `0x01→0x02` (calldata `FieldIdx` changed meaning); `Erc7730Ir::parse` strict-rejects old slot-confusable descriptors. (4) On-device head-bound guard: each format header carries `static_head_words`; `head_bounded_body` (`secure/src/tx/display/erc7730/mod.rs`) truncates the body to the static head so any out-of-head slot fails the walker bounds check (rejected, never silently rendered). On-chain verifier, `pk_root`, CREATE2 addresses unaffected; `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` (prod `99a8a25a…` + e2e) regenerated via `cargo run -p dbgen`, `gen-erc7730-descriptors --check` in sync. **Tests:** `formatters.rs` proof module flipped `walker_slot_confusion_proof`→`walker_slot_confusion_fixed` (walker reads EVM-decoded word; head-bound guard rejects out-of-head/short-head); dbgen golden-value unit tests for multi-word-array/non-leading-tuple/nested-tuple/dynamic-predecessor/EIP-712-ordinal/`@`-discriminator + array-index/unknown-name/dynamic-target/multi-word-target refusals. Seed corpus recompiles + round-trips; firmware builds clean for thumbv8m; 2013/2014 secure host tests pass (the 1 failure — `usb_hw … negative_usb_hw_raw_volatile_only…` — is pre-existing/unrelated, the in-progress MMIO refactor).
